Mirror in `loto_presentation.html`. Bump shared `Spec revision` to `v27` (Phase 7.1 ships right after Phase 7) with a v27 revision note; run `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh`.

**Out of scope for 7.1:** installment (cheque-plan) impact preview — `POST /transaction/installment/preview` (Phase 6) already previews the *rows*; forecasting the downstream report impact of an N-row plan is a heavier extension covered by Phase 7.2.

> **Phase 7.1 completion notes (implementation differences from the original plan):**
> - **No-linked-operator status (7.1.9 / 7.1.10):** the plan text said a Member with no linked operator surfaces `TokenWithoutPermissionException` (403). The shared create flow actually throws `OnValidationException` (**400** `TRANSACTION_MEMBER_REQUIRES_OPERATOR_LINK`) for this case, because `TransactionRecordedByOperatorResolver` runs **before** `MemberAccountScopeGuard` inside `TransactionCreatePreamble`. Preview/write parity is the binding invariant ("same errors the create flow throws"), so the use-case test asserts `OnValidationException` and the WebApi test asserts **400** — matching `CreateTransactionUseCaseTest` exactly. The 403 path is still exercised by the **Member out-of-scope account** case (`TRANSACTION_MEMBER_ACCOUNT_OUT_OF_SCOPE`), which justifies the controller's `403 ProducesResponseType`.
> - **Projector (7.1.3):** implemented as a `ProjectCreate(...)` method on the existing `TransactionEditImpactProjector` (not a new class), keeping it the single DI-registered Reports helper per item 12.2. No new DI registration was needed (7.1.4).
> - **DTO rename (7.1.2):** `ResponseTransactionEditImpactJson` was renamed to the neutral `ResponseTransactionImpactJson`, now shared by both previews; the §6.14 edit-preview subsection and the v26 references were updated for consistency (free rename — v26 unreleased).
> - **Preamble context (7.1.1):** `TransactionCreateContext` now carries the resolved `Account` (surfaced from `TransactionBranchConsistencyService.ResolveAndValidate` via a new `TransactionBranchConsistencyResult` record) so the hypothetical-create value object can read `AccountType` without the use case injecting `IAccountsRepository`. Behavior-preserving for the existing create/installment write flows.
> - **Preview-never-commits scan (7.1.8 / 12.3):** rather than only recording the namespace, the Phase 12.3 architecture test was implemented early as `ArchitectureTest.PreviewAndReportUseCases_DoNotDependOnUnitOfWork` — a reflection scan over `UseCases/Reports/`, `UseCases/Transactions/InstallmentPreview/`, `UseCases/Transactions/EditPreview/`, and `UseCases/Transactions/CreatePreview/` asserting no constructor declares `IUnitOfWork`. This enforces the create-preview invariant the moment it ships instead of waiting for Slice 12. Phase 12.3 now verifies/owns this test rather than authoring it from scratch.

### Phase 7.2 — Cheque Installment Impact Preview

`POST /transaction/installment/preview?asOfDate?` keeps its Phase 6 row-plan preview and adds downstream impact forecasting for the would-be cheque plan. The request body remains `RequestCreateTransactionInstallmentJson` **verbatim**; `asOfDate?` is query-string only. The response stays additive/backward-compatible: existing `TotalValue`, `InstallmentCount`, and `Rows` remain unchanged, and a new `Impact` object forecasts open-cheque aging, fiado balance, and cash variance. Same auth/scope as `POST /transaction/installment`: `[TokenAuthenticateBranch]`, with Member linked-operator + account-scope checks inherited from `TransactionCreatePreamble`.

- [x] **7.2.1** Add the additive DTO surface. Extend `ResponseInstallmentPreviewJson` with `Impact: ResponseInstallmentPreviewImpactJson`. Add `ResponseInstallmentPreviewImpactJson { OpenChequeAgingImpact, FiadoBalanceImpact, CashVarianceImpact }`. Reuse `ResponseFiadoBalanceImpactJson` and `ResponseCashVarianceImpactJson` verbatim. Add `ResponseOpenChequeAgingImpactJson { GroupAppearsInOpenCheques, OutstandingTotal, OldestOpenDueDate?, OldestOpenBucket?, OpenRowCount, TotalRowCount, ClientId?, ClientName?, AccountId?, AccountName?, Description?, Rows }` and `ResponseOpenChequeAgingPreviewRowJson { Index, DueDate, Value, DaysOutstanding, Bucket, Description }`. Do **not** expose preview `OriginTransactionId` or row `TransactionId`; preview row identity is positional via `Index`.
- [x] **7.2.2** Add `HypotheticalTransactionInstallmentPlan` under `UseCases/Transactions/InstallmentPreview/`. Built from the request, `TransactionCreatePreamble` context, and generated `InstallmentPlanRow` list. Carry branch id, account id/name/type from `context.Account`, optional client id/name, request `Date`, `Direction` from `TransactionType.Category.DefaultDirection` (not payload), `Status` from `SaveAsDraft`, total value, and generated rows. Branch scope stays explicit and comes from the preamble-resolved context.
- [x] **7.2.3** Add `ProjectInstallment(...)` to the existing concrete `TransactionEditImpactProjector` (no interface, no new DI Reports helper; keep the class name in this phase to avoid unrelated internal churn). For `Draft`, keep returning the generated preview rows but make all impact sections empty/zero because draft transactions are excluded from reports and cash variance. For `Active`, compute:
  - `OpenChequeAgingImpact`: one aggregate would-be group from the generated rows. `OutstandingTotal = sum(row.Value)`, `OldestOpenDueDate = min(row.DueDate)`, `OldestOpenBucket = ReportAgingBucketizer.BucketFor(oldest, asOfDate)`, `OpenRowCount = TotalRowCount = rows.Count`; row `DaysOutstanding` and `Bucket` use the same rules as `GET /report/cheques/open-aging`.
  - `FiadoBalanceImpact`: when `AccountType == Tab && ClientId != null`, emit exactly one aggregated delta for that client using `sum(Direction.Out ? +row.Value : -row.Value)`; empty otherwise. This closes the parity hole for cheque transaction types that also require Tab account + client.
  - `CashVarianceImpact`: `VarianceDelta = -sum(NetFlow(Direction, row.Value))` for the generated rows on `(AccountId, Date)`. If a non-`Draft` close exists for `(branch, account, Date)`, populate `CurrentVariance` and `ProjectedVariance = CurrentVariance + VarianceDelta`; for no close or a `Draft` close, follow the existing null-current/projected rules. Document `ProjectedVariance` as the variance if current close counts remain unchanged after these cheque rows are recorded.

Flow remains validate → preamble resolve → `OperatorEnteredCheque` conflict guard → build plan → resolve `asOfDate ?? branchClock.LocalBusinessDate(branchClock.UtcNow())` → build hypothetical plan → project impact → return existing row preview plus `Impact`. The Phase 12.3 architecture scan already covers `UseCases/Transactions/InstallmentPreview/`, so no architecture-test namespace change is needed.

Route and auth filter stay the same: `POST /transaction/installment/preview` with `[TokenAuthenticateBranch]`. `ProducesResponseType` declarations remain the Phase 6 set unless implementation reveals a new actual status path.

Projector math should be covered with the real concrete projector.

> **Phase 7.2 completion notes (implementation differences from the original plan):**
> - **Projector (7.2.3):** implemented as a `ProjectInstallment(...)` method on the existing `TransactionEditImpactProjector` (still the single DI-registered Reports helper), returning the new `ResponseInstallmentPreviewImpactJson`. No new DI registration was needed — the use case, projector, and `IBranchClock` were already registered.
> - **Client name (7.2.2):** `HypotheticalTransactionInstallmentPlan` carries both the optional client **id and name**. Following the 7.1 `Account` precedent, `TransactionBranchConsistencyService` now surfaces the client it already loads (instead of discarding it) on `TransactionBranchConsistencyResult`, and `TransactionCreateContext` carries it through, so the VO reads `context.Client?.Name` with no second client load and no post-validation empty-name race. The projector uses the carried name for both the open-cheque group and the fiado delta. The open-cheque group `Description` is the origin (first) row's prefixed description, matching what the real report joins from the origin row.
> - **No-close cash variance (7.2.10):** covered both in the seeded-close determinism test (real post-write recompute equals `ProjectedVariance`) and a dedicated no-close test asserting `VarianceDelta` is present while `CurrentVariance`/`ProjectedVariance` are null.

  Operator resolution (matches M5 Phase 4 list-style precedent):
  - Member with **no linked operator** → empty summary short-circuit (`OperatorId = Guid.Empty`, `OperatorName = ""`, zero counts and totals, no repository call).
  - Member with a linked operator AND (`Mine = true` OR `OperatorId == own linked` OR `OperatorId omitted`) → use own linked operator.

### Phase 10 — Monthly Reconciliation Report

`GET /report/monthly-reconciliation/{year}/{month}` — Manager/Admin. The gatekeeper screen managers consult before advancing `Setting.LockDate`. Returns per-day account-close status, transaction status counts, per-day variance, the `LockReady` boolean, and a structured `Blockers` list (typed items clients format/localize: unapproved closes carry day + account + status; draft-transaction blockers carry day + count). Bumps spec to `v29`. The projection logic lives **inside the use case** (no separate `MonthlyReconciliationProjector` service class).

- [x] **10.1** Add `MonthlyTransactionCountRow(DateTime Date, TransactionStatus Status, int Count)` projection record for the new aggregate from item 10.2b. The monthly reconciliation response projection stays inline in the use case because a separate `MonthlyReconciliationRow` intermediary adds no behavior and obscures the direct close-to-response mapping.
- [x] **10.2** Extend `IDailyClosesRepository` with `Task<IReadOnlyList<DailyClose>> ListByBranchIdAndYearMonthAsNoTracking(branchId, year, month)`. Eager-includes `Account` only (needed for `AccountName` and the `Account.Name` ordering); the response contract carries no operator field, so `SubmittedByOperator` is intentionally NOT included here — unlike the List/Get methods whose DTOs surface `SubmittedByOperatorName`. Branch-scoped, `Active = true`, deterministic ordering `Date ASC, AccountName ASC`. Variance values do NOT come from a `.Include` on `DailyCloseItems` here — Phase 10 reuses Phase 8's existing `IDailyCloseItemsRepository.ListVarianceValuesByBranchIdAndProductIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking` with the month window, after the use case resolves the `Diferença Caixa` product id via `ICashVarianceProductResolver`. This keeps the close query lean and reuses the variance projection already shipped.
- [x] **10.2b** Extend `ITransactionsRepository` with a dedicated aggregate `Task<IReadOnlyList<MonthlyTransactionCountRow>> CountByBranchIdAndYearMonthGroupedByDateAndStatusAsNoTracking(branchId, year, month)`. Branch-scoped, `Active = true` (status spans all of Active/Draft/Cancelled — that's the point), groups by `(Date, Status)`, projects `(Date, Status, Count)`. Do NOT reuse the paginated `ListByBranchIdAsNoTracking` for this — that path paginates and can silently drop rows; and it requires materializing every row in the month, which is wasteful for a count-only need.

- [x] **10.4** Extend `DailyClosesRepositoryBuilder` and keep request test setup on `RequestMonthlyReconciliationJsonBuilder`; no separate monthly query builder is needed because the request DTO already carries the two route values.
- [x] **10.5** Add DTOs: `RequestMonthlyReconciliationJson { int Year, int Month }`; `ResponseMonthlyReconciliationJson { Year, Month, bool LockReady, IReadOnlyList<ResponseMonthlyReconciliationDayJson> Days, IReadOnlyList<ResponseMonthlyReconciliationBlockerJson> Blockers }`; `ResponseMonthlyReconciliationDayJson { Date, IReadOnlyList<ResponseMonthlyReconciliationDayCloseJson> Closes, ActiveTransactionCount, DraftTransactionCount, CancelledTransactionCount, NetVariance }`; `ResponseMonthlyReconciliationDayCloseJson { DailyCloseId, AccountId, AccountName, DailyCloseStatus Status, VarianceValue }`; structured `ResponseMonthlyReconciliationBlockerJson { MonthlyReconciliationBlockerType Type, int Day, Guid? DailyCloseId, Guid? AccountId, string? AccountName, DailyCloseStatus? CloseStatus, int? DraftTransactionCount }` (new `MonthlyReconciliationBlockerType` enum: `UnapprovedClose` | `DraftTransactions`) so clients format/localize blocker text and deep-link to the offending close via the ids. Add the request builder.
- [x] **10.6** Add `MonthlyReconciliationFluentValidation`: `Year ∈ [2000, 2100]` (→ `REPORT_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE`), `Month ∈ [1, 12]` (→ `REPORT_MONTH_INVALID`).
- [x] **10.7** Implement `GetMonthlyReconciliationUseCase` under `UseCases/Reports/MonthlyReconciliation/`. Manager/Admin → validate inline → compute window `[dateFrom = (year, month, 1), dateTo = end-of-month]`. Sequential calls (scoped DbContext — no concurrency):
  1. `closes = await dailyClosesRepository.ListByBranchIdAndYearMonthAsNoTracking(branchId, year, month)`.
  2. `productId = await cashVarianceProductResolver.GetIdAsync(branchId)`.
  3. `varianceRows = await dailyCloseItemsRepository.ListVarianceValuesByBranchIdAndProductIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking(branchId, productId, accountId: null, dateFrom, dateTo)` — reuses the Phase 8 aggregate to get `(Date, AccountId, AccountName, Value, DailyCloseStatus)` rows for the month.
  4. `statusCounts = await transactionsRepository.CountByBranchIdAndYearMonthGroupedByDateAndStatusAsNoTracking(branchId, year, month)` — the new aggregate from item 10.2b.

  The projection logic is **inline in this use case** (or as a private method in the same folder — `MonthlyReconciliationProjection.cs` as a static helper alongside the use case if it grows past ~50 lines):
  - Build a `(Date, AccountId) → varianceValue` lookup from `varianceRows`. **Do not key only on `Date`** — a single day can have multiple closes (one per account) with different variance values, and a Date-only key would collapse them and report the wrong variance per close.
  - Build a separate `Date → Σ varianceValue` aggregate from the same `varianceRows`, used for the per-day `NetVariance` field on the response day row.
  - Build a `(Date, Status) → Count` lookup from `statusCounts`.
  - For each day in the month, build the response day row directly with the day's close list (each close's status + variance from the `(Date, AccountId)` lookup keyed by that close's `AccountId`), transaction counts pulled by status from the lookup, `NetVariance` pulled from the `Date → Σ` aggregate.
  - `LockReady = true` iff every close in the month is `Approved` AND `statusCounts` has zero rows for `Status = Draft` in the month.
  - `Blockers`: for each non-Approved close, append an `UnapprovedClose` item (`Day`, `DailyCloseId`, `AccountId`, `AccountName`, `CloseStatus`). For each day with `DraftTransactionCount > 0`, append a `DraftTransactions` item (`Day`, `DraftTransactionCount`). Structured — clients format/localize the display text and deep-link to the offending close via the ids.
- [x] **10.8** Register in DI.
- [x] **10.9** Add `GET /report/monthly-reconciliation/{year:int:min(2000):max(2100)}/{month:int:min(1):max(12)}` to `ReportController` (Manager/Admin). Out-of-range year/month returns 404 from the route constraint (no `ProducesResponseType` needed, M6 Phase 6.9 convention). Full `[ProducesResponseType]` for 200/400/401/403.

- [x] **10.12** Add `WebApi.Test` happy + unhappy: seeded month data exercises the LockReady flip on a single Draft close; out-of-range year (e.g. 1900) returns 404 from the route constraint.

### Phase 11 — Time-Entry Balance Summary Report

`GET /report/timeentry-balance?operatorId?&dateFrom&dateTo&mine` — any branch role. Operator self-check ("how many hours have I worked? am I behind target?") and manager payroll prep. Re-runs `ITimeEntryCalculationService.Calculate` per row so in-progress current-day rows surface live-recomputed hours (§6.7 / M5 Phase 4 pattern). The per-row recompute and period-sum logic lives **inside the use case** (no separate `TimeEntryBalancePeriodSummarizer` service class).

> **Superseded in part by the Phase 11 Addendum (2026-06-27) below.** Items 11.1–11.12 record the original **single-operator-only** contract as built and shipped green. The addendum adds a **branch-wide (all-operator) mode** for Manager/Admin and reshapes the response into a per-operator list; implement it on top of this slice. Where the addendum and the original items disagree (response shape in 11.5, resolution rule in 11.7, controller type in 11.9, tests in 11.11/11.12), the addendum wins.

The use case reads `RequestTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson` directly and passes loose params (`branchId, operatorId, dateFrom, dateTo` — 4 args, under the 5-param split threshold) to the repository. Do **not** add a `TimeEntryBalanceSummaryQuery`.

OperatorId, DateTime DateFrom, DateTime DateTo, bool Mine }`; `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson { OperatorId, OperatorName, DateFrom, DateTo, TotalHours, TotalBalanceHours, PresentDays, AbsentDays, OwingDays, AbonadoDays, bool ContainsInProgress, IReadOnlyList<ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryDayJson> Days }`; `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryDayJson { Date, TimeEntryStatus Status, TotalHours, BalanceHours, bool IsInProgress }`. Add the request builder.
- [x] **11.6** Add `TimeEntryBalanceSummaryFluentValidation`: date-range rules, `Mine + OperatorId` mutex.
- [x] **11.7** Implement `GetTimeEntryBalanceSummaryUseCase` under `UseCases/Reports/TimeEntryBalance/`: any branch role → validate inline → operator resolution **matches Phase 9 exactly** (no linked operator → empty summary; explicit other operator → 403 `REPORT_MEMBER_NOT_OWN_OPERATOR`; Manager/Admin any). Load branch `Setting` once; capture `branchLocalNow = branchClock.LocalBusinessDateTime(branchClock.UtcNow())` once. Call `ListByBranchIdAndOperatorIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking`. **Inline** in the use case: for each row, build the segment-input list, call `ITimeEntryCalculationService.Calculate(...)` with the captured `branchLocalNow`, build the per-day response item, accumulate `TotalHours` / `TotalBalanceHours` / day-category counts; set `ContainsInProgress = entries.Any(t => t.Segments.Any(s => s.Active && s.ClockOut is null))`.

  Day categorization (per §6.7):
  - `PresentDays` = `Status == Present`
  - `AbsentDays` = `Status ∈ {JustifiedAbsence, UnjustifiedAbsence}`
  - `OwingDays` = `Status ∈ {DayOff, UnjustifiedAbsence}` (per the owing branch — DayOff contributes `-DailyTarget`)
  - `AbonadoDays` = `Status ∈ {Sunday, Holiday, Vacation, JustifiedAbsence}` (per the abonado branch)
  - `UnjustifiedAbsence` legitimately counts in both Absent and Owing — the response surfaces both views.

### Phase 11 Addendum (2026-06-27) — Branch-wide all-operator mode

**Why**: A Manager/Admin who names no operator (and does not pass `mine`) should get a payroll-prep roll-up for **every** operator in the branch, not a `400`. The original slice (item 11.7) rejected that case with `REPORT_OPERATOR_ID_REQUIRED`; this addendum turns "no operator named" into a branch-wide view. Member scope is unchanged — a Member still only ever sees their own operator. The single-operator drill-down (a Member's own, `mine=true`, or a Manager naming one `operatorId`) is unchanged in behavior; only the JSON envelope changes.

**Response reshape** (one endpoint, one response type — no polymorphic payload, so OpenAPI/TS codegen stays a single shape). The current single-operator `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson` body becomes a per-operator element inside a wrapper:

- `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson { DateTime DateFrom, DateTime DateTo, IReadOnlyList<ResponseTimeEntryBalanceOperatorJson> Operators }` — the window lives on the wrapper (one range for all operators).
- `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceOperatorJson { Guid OperatorId, string OperatorName, decimal TotalHours, decimal TotalBalanceHours, int PresentDays, int AbsentDays, int OwingDays, int AbonadoDays, bool ContainsInProgress, IReadOnlyList<ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryDayJson> Days }` — the old top-level body minus `DateFrom`/`DateTo`.
- `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryDayJson` unchanged. `RequestTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson` unchanged.
- Single-operator results (Member own / `mine` / targeted `operatorId`) return exactly **one** element in `Operators`. Unlinked Member returns an **empty** `Operators` list (no more empty-scalar object).
- Clients derive "anything open in the branch" via `Operators.Any(o => o.ContainsInProgress)`; no branch-level `ContainsInProgress` field is added (kept lean; easy to add later).

**Resolution after auth** (only the last bullet is new):

- Member → own operator (one element) or empty `Operators` (unlinked). Explicit other-operator → 403 `REPORT_MEMBER_NOT_OWN_OPERATOR`. *(unchanged)*
- Manager/Admin + `operatorId` → that operator, one element; 404 `OPERATOR_NOT_FOUND` on cross-branch. *(unchanged)*
- Manager/Admin + `mine` → own operator, one element; 404 if no linked operator. *(unchanged)*
- **Manager/Admin + neither → NEW branch-wide mode**: one element per operator that has ≥ 1 active entry in the window. The `REPORT_OPERATOR_ID_REQUIRED` throw is removed from this use case (the resource string stays — Phase 9 `operator-summary` still uses it).

**Decisions baked in** (flagging for review — say the word to change either):
- **Zero-entry operators are omitted.** Branch-wide mode lists only operators with at least one entry in the range (pure projection of existing rows). Listing *all active operators* with zero-filled summaries (a "who didn't log hours?" view) is a deliberate non-goal for now.
- **One endpoint, always-wrapped.** Alternative considered and rejected: keep the single-operator shape and add a separate `…/branch` endpoint. Rejected because you asked for the same endpoint to cover both; the cost is reshaping the already-built single-operator response + tests.
- **Operator ordering**: `OperatorName` ASC; days within each operator stay `Date` ASC.

**Checklist**:

- [x] **11A.1** Add repo `Task<IReadOnlyList<TimeEntry>> ListByBranchIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking(branchId, dateFrom, dateTo)` — branch-wide, `Active`-filtered, eager-load `.Include(t => t.Operator)` + `.Include(t => t.Segments.Where(s => s.Active))`, ordered `Operator.Name` then `Date`. Keep the operator-scoped `ListByBranchIdAndOperatorIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking` (11.2) for the member / targeted / `mine` paths.
- [x] **11A.2** Infrastructure impl + extend `TimeEntriesRepositoryBuilder` with the exact-argument helper for the new branch-wide method.
- [x] **11A.3** Reshape DTOs: new wrapper `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson` + new `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceOperatorJson`; `ResponseTimeEntryBalanceSummaryDayJson` and `RequestTimeEntryBalanceSummaryJson` unchanged.
- [x] **11A.4** Use case: extract a shared `BuildOperatorSummary(operatorId, operatorName, entries, setting, branchLocalNow)` that does the per-row `Calculate` loop + day-category counts (currently inline in `BuildResponse`), and call it from both paths. Single-operator path → wrap one element. Branch-wide path → fetch branch rows, `GroupBy(OperatorId)` (name from the included `Operator`), build one element each, order by name. Capture `branchLocalNow` **once** for the whole report (every operator shares one "now"). Remove the `REPORT_OPERATOR_ID_REQUIRED` throw.
- [x] **11A.5** Controller: response type → new wrapper. Auth (`[TokenAuthenticateBranch]`) and `[ProducesResponseType]` set unchanged (200/400/401/403/404); 400 now comes only from the validator (inverted / too-wide range, `mine`+`operatorId` mutex).
- [x] **11A.6** Validator unchanged — the mutex stays; the "operator required" rule never lived in the validator (it was a use-case role check, now removed).
- [x] **11A.7** Update `UseCases.Test`: single-operator cases assert `Operators.Single()`; the former "Manager omits both → 400" case becomes "Manager omits both → all operators." Add branch-wide cases: two operators with mixed statuses grouped + ordered by name; branch isolation (other branch's operators excluded); Member still scoped to own; zero-entry operator omitted.

> **Phase 12 completion notes (close-out audit findings — items 12.1–12.10 complete; the former item 12.11 relocated to Milestone 7.6 so Phase 12 closes cleanly):**
> - **`[ProducesResponseType]` audit (12.1):** all eleven M7 actions were cross-checked against their actual throwable codes. Ten had declarations that exactly matched their reachable sets and needed no change — Manager/Admin reports (daily-ledger, cash-variance) reach 404 via a cross-branch `AccountId` lookup; fiado/balance, fiado/aging, open-cheque-aging have no use-case 404 path and correctly omit it; the three preview actions reach 403/404/409 through `TransactionCreatePreamble` (create/installment) or the edit-preview role/guard chain. The eleventh — **monthly-reconciliation** — had a mismatch (declared 400 unreachable, route-constraint 404 undeclared) fixed via a route correction (see the dedicated note below); its `[ProducesResponseType]` set itself was left at `{200,400,401,403}`, now fully reachable. One coverage gap (not a declaration gap) was also found and closed — see 12.4.
> - **monthly-reconciliation 400 vs 404 — contract correction (12.1):** the original route `{year:int:min(2000):max(2100)}/{month:int:min(1):max(12)}` mirrored `MonthlyReconciliationFluentValidation`'s bounds exactly, so an out-of-range value was rejected by routing (a body-less framework **404**, not a `ResponseErrorJson`) before the action ran. That left the declared `[ProducesResponseType(400)]` unreachable over HTTP **and** a real 404 undeclared — so the endpoint's contract did not match its declarations. Phase 12 fixed it: the route now carries bare `:int` constraints (no `min/max`), so `Validate(request)` (which runs before the `new DateTime(year, month, 1)` window is built — no overflow risk) owns the range check and an out-of-range `year`/`month` returns a real 400 `ResponseErrorJson` (`REPORT_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE` / `REPORT_MONTH_INVALID`) — the same shape every sibling report's 400 uses. The old `MonthlyReconciliation_ShouldReturn404_WhenYearViolatesRouteConstraint` test became `…_ShouldReturn400_WhenYearOutOfRange`, and a `…_ShouldReturn400_WhenMonthOutOfRange` was added. The declared `{200,400,401,403}` set is now fully reachable and WebApi-tested. (A non-integer path segment still fails the `:int` match with the universal typed-route 404 — that is framework behavior shared by every `:guid`/`:int` route, not a documented contract response.) §6.14 and the v30 note updated.
> - **Coverage catch-up (12.4):** `GET /report/fiado/balance` declared `[ProducesResponseType(400)]` (the `FiadoBalanceFluentValidation` `AsOfDate != default` rule) but its unhappy-path suite asserted only 401/403. Added **`FiadoBalance_ShouldReturn400_WhenAsOfDateIsDefault`** (`asOfDate=0001-01-01` → 400 `REPORT_AS_OF_DATE_INVALID`), matching the existing sibling tests on fiado/aging and open-cheque-aging. **One new WebApi test case.** No other endpoint had an untested declared code.
> - **Architecture coverage (12.2):** `TransactionEditImpactProjector` is a `public`, non-record class under `server.Application.Services.Reports`, so it is already auto-discovered **and** resolution-pinned by `AllPublicServicesAndExceptionHandlers_AreResolvableFromConfiguredRootContainer` (its namespace matches the `server.Application.Services` sweep prefix; `AddScoped<TransactionEditImpactProjector>()` makes it resolvable). All eleven M7 use cases end in `UseCase` and are covered by `AllUseCases_AreRegisteredInApplicationDi`. `ReportAgingBucketizer` stays out of DI (static; covered by `ReportAgingBucketizerTest`). **Catch-up:** the registration test only proved each `*UseCase` service type is present in the collection — it never built the provider, so a use case registered with an *unregistered* constructor dependency would have passed and only failed at runtime. A new `AllUseCases_AreResolvableFromConfiguredRootContainer` test builds the full API+Application+Infrastructure container and actually resolves every `*UseCase`, satisfying 12.2's literal "resolve every M7 use case" wording for the entire application surface.
> - **Preview-never-commits test name (12.3):** the test landed in Slice 7.1 as `ArchitectureTest.PreviewAndReportUseCases_DoNotDependOnUnitOfWork` (the name this doc and the 7.1 note already pin), not the prompt's illustrative `…_DoNotInjectIUnitOfWork`. It is functionally exactly what 12.3 requires — a reflection scan over all four namespaces (`UseCases/Reports`, `…/Transactions/InstallmentPreview`, `…/EditPreview`, `…/CreatePreview`) failing the build if any constructor declares an `IUnitOfWork` parameter — so it was verified present and green rather than renamed (renaming would churn two doc references for no functional gain).
> - **Spec drift (12.9):** the only §6.14 drift across Phases 1–11 was the **missing time-entry-balance contract** — the Phase 11 addendum reshaped the response into the `Operators[]` wrapper and added the branch-wide mode, but §6.14 only carried it in the permission-bucket table. Phase 12 added the explicit "Time-entry balance summary" subsection (11A.9 follow-through) and a clarifying note on the bucket table. All other subsections matched the implementation. Spec bumped to **v30** across the three sync-group files; `check-loto-doc-sync.sh` green.
> - **Former 12.11 relocated (OpenAPI `required` metadata):** the project-wide OpenAPI `required`-metadata gap is a systemic schema/operation-transformer task, not part of the M7 reporting close-out, and it was blocking a clean "Phase 12 complete". It has been **moved to the new Milestone 7.6 — Backend OpenAPI Contract Hardening** (Phase 1), which is also the server-track milestone the `shared/` named-enum codegen gate already names as a precondition. M7's Done criteria are read-contract criteria and do not depend on it; the v30 status line and revision note now carry the "shape-stable, with `required`-metadata pending" caveat so the "stable for code generation" claim is not overstated.

### Done criteria

- All eleven M7 endpoints live: `GET /report/daily-ledger`, `GET /report/fiado/balance`, `GET /report/fiado/aging`, `GET /report/cheques/open-aging`, `GET /report/cash-variance`, `GET /report/operator-summary`, `GET /report/monthly-reconciliation/{year}/{month}`, `GET /report/timeentry-balance`, `POST /transaction/installment/preview`, `POST /transaction/{id}/edit-preview`, `POST /transaction/preview`.
- Every M7 endpoint either reads (`GET`) or computes without persisting (`POST … /preview`). No M7 use case calls `IUnitOfWork.Commit`; pinned by unit + integration tests AND the Phase 12.3 architecture test.
- Preview/write parity for all three preview endpoints is pinned by behavior tests (same payload → same exception or same outcome) plus end-to-end determinism tests (preview output matches post-write state). No `Received()` assertions on validators or shared M3 collaborators — the codebase's convention is to wire dependencies through test builders.
- Manager-only reports return 403 with the existing manager / admin role keys when called by a Member; operator-self reports (Phases 9, 11) return scoped results for Members and any-operator results for Manager/Admin; no-linked-operator Members get an empty summary short-circuit (not 403) on operator-self reports.
- Aging buckets at the day-30 / day-31 / day-90 / day-91 boundaries are exercised by tests (Phases 1.3 + 4.12).
- `POST /transaction/installment/preview` and `POST /transaction/installment` produce position-for-position identical row sequences for the same input, pinned by the determinism test in Phase 6.7; Phase 7.2 additionally pins downstream open-cheque aging, optional fiado, and cash-variance impact parity.
- `POST /transaction/{id}/edit-preview` and `PUT /transaction/{id}` produce consistent impact predictions, pinned by the determinism test in Phase 7.10.
- `POST /transaction/preview` and `POST /transaction` produce consistent impact predictions, pinned by the determinism test in Phase 7.1.11.
- Spec sync covers §6.14 with all eight contract subsections (base reporting contract, fiado aging, open-cheque aging, edit impact preview, create impact preview, installment impact preview, monthly reconciliation, time-entry balance summary); shared revision metadata is at `v30` and `check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes.
- Validator, use-case, Web API, and architecture tests pass.

### Out of M7 scope (explicit deferrals to later ledger milestones)

Both come straight from the original M7 sketch. Both would be write-flow work and therefore violate this milestone's read-only scope boundary. Calling them out so they aren't forgotten:

- **Bounced cheque lifecycle.** If product signal emerges that bounced cheques must stay in receivable totals and aging views, a follow-up adds `TransactionStatus.Bounced = 3` (or a parallel `BouncedAt` audit column on `Transaction`), updates §6.4 and §6.11, and threads the new state through the fiado balance + open-cheque aging reports from this milestone. Treating bounced cheques as a `CancellationReason` overload would invalidate their inclusion in aging — so do this properly when it ships.
- **Installment provenance metadata.** If audit/legal asks the system to distinguish operator-entered manual rows from auto-generated rows, a follow-up adds a structured `InstallmentSource` (or similar) on the origin transaction, not on `Description`. Then surface it in the M7 reports that already show installment groups.

## Milestone 7.5 — Frontend UX Contract Gaps

**Status: complete (closed at spec `v31`, 2026-07-05).** Phases 1–2 shipped the dashboard and review endpoints; Phase 3 resolved as a documented deferral; Phase 4 audited the contracts, extended the never-commits architecture scan, synced §6.14, and bumped the doc group to `v31`.

**Goal:** Close the high-payoff backend contract gaps surfaced by the first frontend screen catalog: a single manager dashboard aggregation endpoint and a daily-close review context that exposes opening values. These are read-oriented UX contracts that let web/mobile build the manager work queue and close approval/review screens without fragile client-side joins or hidden business-rule duplication.

**Source:** these gaps were surfaced by the frontend M0 screen catalog — see `design/docs/screens.md` → "Backend contract gaps — tracked on the server track (M7.5)". Mapping: variance-on-list → Phase 3, opening-values → Phase 2, dashboard-endpoint → Phase 1. A fourth observation (draft-finalize) needed no work — `POST /transaction/{id}/finalize` already exists.

**Scope boundary:** Additive read/reporting endpoints and response fields only. No schema changes, no new lifecycle states, no write flows, no lock-date advancement changes, no bounced-cheque lifecycle, and no installment provenance metadata. Existing M7 report endpoints remain valid; this milestone may reuse their repositories/projections but should not make clients stitch them manually for the first manager screen.

**Precondition:** Milestone 7 is closed and the frontend M0 screen catalog has locked the manager dashboard and daily-close approval semantics. DailyClose, CashVariance, Account, OperatorAccount, and M7 reporting repositories already exist.

**Key behaviors:**

- **Backend owns derived dashboard facts.** The manager dashboard should not reconstruct "pending approval", "not submitted", "variance for this close", or branch-local date behavior from three unrelated calls. M7.5 centralizes those derivations in one contract.
- **Dashboard is exception-first.** The endpoint optimizes the manager's first workflow: pending approvals, not-submitted accounts/operators, and highest-variance closes for one selected branch-local date.
- **Opening values are server-derived.** §6.5 already defines opening values as the most recent prior close for the same `(BranchId, AccountId)`. The review/close context must expose them directly instead of making clients rediscover the prior close and recalculate the mapping.
- **List enrichment is secondary.** Adding `VarianceValue` to `ResponseListDailyCloseItemJson` is useful only if list/approval screens still need inline variance after the dashboard and review-context endpoints exist. Treat it as a deliberate gate, not automatic DTO bloat.
- **No domain query/filter objects (Phase 11 precedent).** Use cases read the request DTO directly and pass loose params (`branchId`, `date`, `productId`, …) to repositories; repo aggregate returns use projection records in `server.Domain/Models/Projections/`. Do **not** wrap params in a `DashboardQuery` / review-query object that never reaches the data path. `RequestDashboardJson` is the wire contract (validated input), not a domain query wrapper.

### Phase 1 — Manager dashboard aggregation endpoint

`GET /report/dashboard?date=YYYY-MM-DD` — Manager/Admin only. Returns the selected branch-local date, close rows with variance, pending approval count/list, not-submitted rows, and branch-level variance aggregates for the day.

- [x] **1.1** Add dashboard response DTOs under `server.Communication`: `ResponseDashboardJson` with `Date`, `TotalVariance`, `MeanVariance`, `PendingApprovalCount`, `IReadOnlyList<ResponseDashboardCloseJson> Closes`, and `IReadOnlyList<ResponseDashboardNotSubmittedJson> NotSubmitted`. Close rows carry `DailyCloseId`, `AccountId`, `AccountName`, `SubmittedByOperatorId?`, `SubmittedByOperatorName?`, `DailyCloseStatus Status`, `SubmittedAt?`, `ApprovedAt?`, and `VarianceValue?` (null when no submitted cash-variance row exists). Not-submitted rows carry the expected account/operator identity and, when a Draft close exists, the `DailyCloseId` + status so the UI can deep-link.
- [x] **1.2** Define the "expected closer" rule explicitly before implementation. Default expectation: active `AccountType.Terminal` accounts with at least one active `OperatorAccount` link are expected to close. If existing operational behavior requires BankAccount or Tab closes, document that and adjust the rule before writing code. **Decision:** the default rule stands, tightened to links whose `Operator` is also active; Bank/Tab accounts are never expected (§6.5 daily closing covers terminal cash counts, §6.4 keeps fiado query-time). The row carries the primary active link's operator, falling back to the first active link by operator name. A future branch-local date (`IBranchClock`) has no expected closers, so `NotSubmitted` is empty for dates after branch-local today. Draft closes surface only in `NotSubmitted` (with deep-link id); `Closes` carries submitted-or-later rows.
- [x] **1.3** Extend repositories with purpose-built projections instead of reusing paginated list endpoints for dashboard computation. Reuse `IDailyCloseItemsRepository.ListVarianceValuesByBranchIdAndProductIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking` where it fits, but add a dedicated account/operator projection if the not-submitted rule needs active terminal assignments.
- [x] **1.4** Implement `GetDashboardUseCase` under `UseCases/Reports/Dashboard/`: Manager/Admin → validate date → resolve `Diferença Caixa` product id → load closes for the date → load variance rows keyed by `(Date, AccountId)` → load expected closers → project rows, pending count/list, not-submitted rows, and aggregate variance in the use case.
- [x] **1.5** Add `GET /report/dashboard` to `ReportController` with `[TokenAuthorize(Role.Manager, Role.Admin)]` and full `[ProducesResponseType]` for 200/400/401/403/404 if account/operator lookups introduce a 404 path; otherwise no spurious 404 declaration.
- [x] **1.6** Add validator, use-case, and WebApi tests: happy mixed day; empty day with not-submitted rows; Draft close counts as not submitted but carries its close id; Submitted close increments `PendingApprovalCount`; Approved/Rejected are not pending; variance rows join by `(Date, AccountId)` and never by date alone; Member receives 403; cross-branch data is excluded.

### Phase 2 — Daily-close review context with opening values

Preferred contract: `GET /dailyclose/{dailyCloseId}/review` (name may be refined in-phase) returning the close header plus per-product opening/closing context for the close-day form and manager approval review. Use a dedicated response shape unless implementation proves that enriching `ResponseDailyCloseJson` is cheaper and does not make generic reads misleading.

- [x] **2.1** Add review/context DTOs: `ResponseDailyCloseReviewJson` wrapping the close header fields plus `IReadOnlyList<ResponseDailyCloseReviewItemJson> Items`. Each item carries `ProductId`, `ProductName`, `OpeningValue?`, `ClosingValue`, and `IsCashVarianceProduct`. `OpeningValue` is null for the system `Diferença Caixa` row unless the implementation intentionally documents a different display rule.
- [x] **2.2** Implement the opening-value projection from §6.5: find the most recent prior active close for the same `(BranchId, AccountId)` ordered by `Date DESC, CreatedAt DESC, Id DESC`, map prior active items by `ProductId`, and surface zero or null according to the DTO rule chosen in 2.1. Do not include the prior `Diferença Caixa` row as a normal opening product value.
- [x] **2.3** Reuse the existing DailyClose get permission semantics: missing/cross-branch id → 404; Member without a linked operator or outside account scope → the existing DailyClose read-scope 403 path; Manager/Admin elevated. The review endpoint must not leak cross-branch prior-close existence through opening values.
- [x] **2.4** Add `DailyCloseController` route + `[ProducesResponseType]` declarations matching actual returnable codes.
- [x] **2.5** Add use-case and WebApi tests: no prior close → opening values empty/zero by the chosen rule; prior close skips weekends/holidays naturally via most-recent-prior ordering; product removed since prior close is handled predictably; `Diferença Caixa` is flagged as system-calculated; branch isolation; Member in-scope success; Member out-of-scope 403; Manager/Admin success.

### Phase 3 — DailyClose list variance enrichment gate

**Outcome: deferred (no code change), decided 2026-07-05 after Phases 1–2 shipped.**

- [x] **3.1** After Phases 1–2, decide whether `ResponseListDailyCloseItemJson` still needs `VarianceValue?`. If dashboard and review context cover the first frontend flows, leave the existing list DTO untouched and document the deferral here. **Decision: defer.** Both cataloged consumers of inline list variance (`design/docs/screens.md` gap §1) are covered without it: the Manager Work Queue is date-scoped and exception-first, which is exactly what `GET /report/dashboard` returns — `ResponseDashboardCloseJson.VarianceValue` inline per close row, joined server-side by `(Date, AccountId)` — and the Daily-close approval screen reads the flagged cash-variance row (`IsCashVarianceProduct`) with opening/closing values from `GET /dailyclose/{id}/review`. The only other cataloged consumer of the paginated `GET /dailyclose` list (Operator Day Cockpit) reads close state, not variance. Reopen this gate if a future multi-date list/history screen needs inline variance per row.
- [x] **3.2** Not applicable — the field was not added (3.1 deferred). `ResponseListDailyCloseItemJson` and the list use case are untouched.
- [x] **3.3** Deferral path chosen: no code change, so no new tests; the 3.1 note is the documented outcome.

### Phase 4 — Contract hardening + spec sync

- [x] **4.1** Audit `[ProducesResponseType]` declarations for every M7.5 endpoint and add WebApi coverage for each declared status. **Audit result: no gaps.** `GET /report/dashboard` declares `{200, 400, 401, 403}` — 400 from `DashboardFluentValidation`, 403 from the auth filter + use-case role check, and deliberately no 404 (a missing `Diferença Caixa` seed product is an `InvalidOperationException` bootstrap defect, not a client-visible not-found). `GET /dailyclose/{id}/review` declares `{200, 401, 403, 404}` — no request body/query, so no 400 path. Every declared code was already hit by at least one WebApi test; no catch-up tests were needed.
- [x] **4.2** Confirm architecture tests resolve any new use cases/services. Projection helpers that stay private/static in the use-case folder do not need DI registration. **Result:** the project-wide `*UseCase` reflection sweeps auto-discovered `GetDashboardUseCase` and `GetDailyCloseReviewUseCase` (registration + root-container resolution), and both controller actions declare explicit auth intent — no explicit resolution test was needed. One catch-up: `server.Application.UseCases.DailyCloses.Review` was added to the `PreviewNeverCommitsNamespacePrefixes` scan so the review context's read-only invariant is pinned at build time like the report use cases.
- [x] **4.3** Update `loto-specs.md` §6.14 with the dashboard aggregation contract and the daily-close review/opening-values contract. Mirror relevant product-facing notes in `loto_presentation.html`; `loto_entity_relationship_diagram.html` should remain unchanged unless implementation introduces schema changes, which this milestone should avoid. Bump the shared spec revision after the current M7 close-out revision (expected `v31` if M7 closes at `v30`) and run `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh`. **Done at `v31`** (M7.5 shipped before M7.6, so it took `v31`; M7.6 moves to `v32`). §6.14 gained "Manager dashboard aggregation", "Daily-close review context", and the Phase 3 deferral note; the presentation gained two closing-slide cards; the ERD changed only its revision metadata (no schema change). `check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes green.
- [x] **4.4** Run `dotnet test tests/Validators.Test`, `dotnet test tests/UseCases.Test`, and `dotnet test tests/WebApi.Test`. **All green at close-out: 299 / 941 / 748 passed, 0 failed.**
- [x] **4.5** Update this milestone with completion notes where implementation intentionally differs from the plan. See "Completion notes" below.

**Completion notes (M7.5 close-out, 2026-07-05):**

- **Phase 3 resolved as a deferral** — `ResponseListDailyCloseItemJson` is untouched; the dashboard covers the work queue's inline variance and the review context covers approval (rationale recorded in Phase 3 above and in §6.14).
- **Expected-closer rule as implemented:** the item 1.2 default stood, tightened to active `OperatorAccount` links whose `Operator` is also active; the surfaced operator is the primary active link with a first-active-by-name fallback; future branch-local dates produce an empty `NotSubmitted`.
- **The review kept a dedicated `ResponseDailyCloseReviewJson`** (the 2.1 preferred contract) rather than enriching `ResponseDailyCloseJson`, because the generic DTO is also a write-operation response and opening values would make it context-dependent.
- **No new failure modes:** neither endpoint introduced a 404 lookup path beyond the review's existing DailyClose read-scope 404, and no `[ProducesResponseType]` correction was required — the Phase 1/2 slices had declared the reachable sets correctly.

### Done criteria

- `GET /report/dashboard?date=...` gives the manager dashboard everything needed for the selected date without client-side stitching across daily-close list, cash-variance summary, and account/operator assignment calls.
- The daily-close review/close context exposes opening values derived by the server from the most recent prior close for the same account, with branch and member-scope isolation pinned by tests.
- `VarianceValue` on `ResponseListDailyCloseItemJson` is either intentionally deferred after the Phase 3 gate or added with tests as a nullable additive field.
- No M7.5 code writes persisted state or changes ledger lifecycle semantics.
- Spec sync passes and all validator, use-case, WebApi, and architecture checks are green.

## Milestone 7.6 — Backend OpenAPI Contract Hardening

**Goal:** Make the generated OpenAPI document a faithful, codegen-grade contract so downstream TypeScript (web + mobile, via `shared/api`) is generated with correct required/optional fields and named — not bare-numeric — enums. This is the server-track milestone the `shared/` milestone's named-enum codegen gate names as a precondition (`shared/docs/milestones.md`: "the backend **OpenAPI Contract Hardening** milestone … does not exist yet"). It exists now.

**Why this is its own milestone (relocated from M7 Phase 12.11):** the gap is systemic and project-wide — it touches every request/response DTO and the `AddOpenApi(...)` pipeline, not the M7 reporting surface specifically. Folding it into the M7 read-contract close-out would have mixed a cross-cutting serialization concern into a feature milestone and blocked M7 from closing. M7 closed at `v30` "shape-stable" (endpoints, DTOs, status codes, semantics locked); this milestone makes the *emitted metadata* match that shape.

**Scope boundary:** OpenAPI document fidelity and the serialization contract only — no new endpoints, no DTO field changes beyond annotations/serialization attributes, no lifecycle or business-rule changes. Integer enum input may still be accepted during a transition window; the change is what the document advertises and what responses serialize.

**Precondition:** Milestone 7 closed at spec `v30`. `server.Communication` DTOs and `ApiDependencyInjection` (currently only a Bearer document transformer) exist.

### Phase 1 — `required` metadata transformer (was M7 Phase 12.11)

The built-in .NET 10 generator emits no `required` array for response/request DTO members and no `required: true` on non-nullable `[FromQuery]` params, so generated TypeScript makes every field optional — directly undermining "contract stable for downstream code generation". Surfaced during the M7 Phase 11 Addendum review; carried here as the headline phase.

- [x] **1.1** Add a schema/operation transformer in `AddOpenApi(...)` that marks non-nullable response/request DTO members and non-nullable `[FromQuery]` parameters as `required`. Fix uniformly — **not** per-DTO, and do not bolt one-off `[Required]` attributes onto the Phase 11 (or any) DTOs.
- [x] **1.2** Decide the non-nullable signal (C# nullable-annotation context on `server.Communication` members + parameter nullability) and document it so new DTOs inherit the behavior automatically.
- [x] **1.3** Pin it with an OpenAPI snapshot/assert test: a sample of response DTOs (including at least one M7 report envelope) and at least one request DTO and one non-nullable query param show up `required` in the generated document.

> **Phase 1 completion note (2026-08-26):** Dedicated schema and operation transformers now derive requiredness from directional C# nullable metadata while preserving accepted omission defaults. The schema rule is restricted to the `server.Communication` assembly, uses each member's `System.Text.Json` name, reads write-state for requests and read-state for responses, and covers referenced component schemas; request-body Boolean members and enum members with a defined zero value remain optional, while invalid-zero request enums and response members remain required. The operation rule preserves omission semantics for direct parameters with explicit C# defaults, initialized flattened DTO properties, Boolean query flags whose omission binds `false`, and defined-zero enum query members; required sentinel dates/IDs, invalid-zero enums, and non-initialized paging integers remain required. Parameter references are resolved explicitly, and case-colliding descriptions/properties are handled without taking down document generation. A semantic WebApi contract test loads the real `/openapi/v1.json` document and pins response/request requiredness, an all-nullable DTO with no `required` array, untouched non-Communication enum components, required dashboard/cash-variance values, initialized paging and optional Boolean list filters, and both defaulted Brazilian-holiday query inputs on preview/import. Focused transformer tests pin referenced-parameter mutation, collision-safe lookup, asymmetric read/write nullability, and matching query/body enum omission rules. No DTO received a one-off `[Required]` attribute, and no DTO field type changed.

### Phase 2 — Named-enum response contract

The `shared/` named-enum codegen gate asserts the generated TS exposes enum *names* (string unions / named objects), never bare numeric unions. The server half: public contract enums serialize as names on responses, and the OpenAPI document lists named values.

- [x] **2.1** Make public contract enums serialize as names on responses (`Draft`, `Active`, `Cancelled`, …); accept integer input during the transition.
- [x] **2.2** Ensure the OpenAPI document lists named enum values, not bare integers, for those enums.
- [x] **2.3** WebApi/OpenAPI tests pin at least one request enum and one response enum end-to-end.

> **Phase 2 completion note (2026-08-26):** MVC JSON now applies one global declared-name enum policy: responses write member names with canonical declared casing and fall back to the numeric backing value for undefined legacy/out-of-band response data so result serialization cannot truncate an already-started response. Request bodies accept declared names case-insensitively, matching MVC query binding, plus integer input during the transition. Integers representable by an enum's backing type bind into the DTO so the owning FluentValidation `IsInEnum` rule retains its feature-specific localized message; a reflection convention test requires that rule for every request enum property, including nullable members. Invalid or case-insensitively ambiguous names fail at the binding boundary with a localized enum-name message, while malformed JSON, empty bodies, wrong member types, numeric overflow, and other model-state failures fall back to the generic request message. The API-wide model-state projection always returns `ResponseErrorJson`, suppresses raw framework messages, and exposes only explicitly allowlisted backend-authored messages. The built-in OpenAPI pipeline uses the same reflected enum declarations to emit `type: string` plus canonical declared names for component schemas, including enums reached only through nullable properties such as `TimeEntryTapAction`, as well as body, response, and query contracts. Real HTTP/OpenAPI regressions pin named and numeric `SettlementRule` input on `POST /transaction-type`, the boundary/feature validation split across transaction type, category, branch-user, and nullable time-entry enums, a named DailyClose response, and representative transaction, time-entry, daily-close, and aging schemas without duplicating enum value lists.

### Phase 3 — Audit, snapshot, and spec sync

- [x] **3.1** Audit required/nullability fidelity across a representative sample of DTOs (not just the M7 reporting DTOs).
- [x] **3.2** Add/extend an OpenAPI document snapshot test so contract regressions fail the build.
- [x] **3.3** Bump the shared `Spec revision` (expected `v33` — M7.5 took `v31`, the §6.12 Direction-semantics clarification took `v32`) with a revision note describing the OpenAPI contract hardening, mirror the product-facing note where relevant, and run `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh`. **Done at `v41`** — see the deviation note below.

> **Phase 3 completion note (2026-08-27):**
>
> - **3.1 audit (cross-feature, not a spot check).** The generated document was diffed against the C# DTO graph across auth/session, branches, accounts, clients, operators, categories/transaction types, products, holidays, transactions, daily closes, settings, reports, and time entries. The audited baseline was the pre-fix document: 146 components (132 object schemas + 14 enum components) over 68 paths and 96 operations. The two fixes below restored three previously absent auth response schemas, so the checked-in snapshot pins **149 components (135 object schemas + 14 enum components)** over the same 68 paths and 96 operations. Findings: (a) requiredness matched directional nullability everywhere, with no `required` entry carrying a CLR name instead of its `System.Text.Json` name and no `required` entry missing from `properties`; (b) nested DTOs and collections behave as the policy predicts — `RequestCreateTransactionInstallmentJson.installments` (non-nullable `IReadOnlyList<T>` initialized to `[]`) is required while its element schema requires both members, and the nullable `RequestPutDailyCloseItemsJson.items` / `RequestUpsertTimeEntryJson.segments` stay optional; (c) every nullable enum (`Role?`, `TimeEntryTapAction?`, `DailyCloseStatus?`, `AgingBucket?`) renders as `oneOf [null, $ref]` and stays optional; (d) optional filters are optional across all list/report endpoints. Two real gaps were found and fixed, both metadata-only: `POST /user/login`, `/user/register`, and `/user/renew-token` declared no `[ProducesResponseType]`, so `ResponseUserLoginJson`, `ResponseUserRegisterJson`, and `ResponseTokenJson` were absent from the document entirely — the exact failure mode this milestone exists to prevent — and `register` advertised `200` while returning `201`. One deliberate non-change: `Page`/`PageSize` are `required` on `/report/cash-variance`, `/report/fiado/aging`, and `/report/cheques/open-aging` because those three DTOs declare no initializer, so omission binds `0` and fails `PageBounds`/`PageSizeBounds`. That is faithful, and normalizing the DTO defaults is a contract change outside this milestone's scope boundary — recorded here rather than silently repaired.
> - **3.2 snapshot.** `OpenApiContractSurfaceSnapshotTest` fetches the live `/openapi/v1.json` and compares a normalized projection (`OpenApiContractSurface.Render`) against the checked-in `tests/WebApi.Test/OpenApi/Snapshots/openapi-contract-surface.txt`. The projection is deterministic and reviewable by construction: it drops `servers` (the test host's port changes per run), `info`, descriptions, examples, tags, formats, and patterns, collapses the generator's duplicate `application/json` / `text/json` / `text/plain` copies of one schema, and ordinally sorts schemas, properties, paths, parameters, and response codes so one contract change is one line of diff. A mismatch writes `openapi-contract-surface.received.txt` next to the expected file for review. Three assertions run independently of the snapshot text so regenerating it can never quietly accept a regression: no schema anywhere in the document declares a non-string `enum` value; every contract enum exists as a named string component whose values equal `Enum.GetNames`; and the configured MVC `JsonSerializerOptions` serializes every declared value of every response-reachable enum as its declared name. The reflection helper the named-enum test already had moved to a shared `CommunicationContractTypes`.
> - **3.3 deviation — `v41`, not `v33`.** The plan's `v33` estimate predates M7.7, which shipped Phases 2/4/6/7 while M7.6 was open; `v33`–`v40` are all taken, so the next free revision was `v41`. The spec gained §6.15 "Generated API contract" (both systemic rules, the financial-header carve-out, and the `Page`/`PageSize` explanation) plus a §4 "Wire representation" note, because §4 lists the enums with their numeric database values and without that note reads as if the numbers are the wire format. The presentation gained one closing card; the ERD changed only its revision metadata, since nothing about the schema moved. `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes green.

### Done criteria

- Generated OpenAPI marks non-nullable members and non-nullable query params as `required`; generated TypeScript no longer makes every field optional.
- Public contract enums appear by name in the document and on responses; the `shared/` named-enum codegen gate's server-side precondition is satisfied.
- Snapshot/assert tests pin both guarantees so regressions break the build.
- Spec sync passes and all validator, use-case, WebApi, and architecture checks are green.

**M7.6 complete (2026-08-27).** All three phases are closed and every Done criterion is met: requiredness is derived project-wide from directional C# nullability (Phase 1), all fourteen contract enums are published and serialized by declared name (Phase 2), and `OpenApiContractSurfaceSnapshotTest` plus its three snapshot-independent assertions pin both guarantees against the live document (Phase 3). Spec sync is green at `v41`; `Validators.Test`, `UseCases.Test`, `WebApi.Test` (architecture tests included), and `dotnet build server/server.sln` are green. The `shared/` named-enum codegen gate's server-side precondition is satisfied — `shared/` may now generate TypeScript from this document. Integer enum input remains accepted during the transition window; retiring it is a separate, deliberately versioned change. Two review findings were also cleared during close-out. **Notes accuracy:** the 3.1 audit figure above now distinguishes the pre-fix baseline (146 components) from the checked-in snapshot (149), and the `v41` revision note now separates *runtime* status codes — unchanged — from *emitted* metadata, which changed by design, `register`'s declared `200` → `201` included. **Dependency hygiene:** `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` 10.0.5 → 10.0.11 and `Testcontainers.PostgreSql` 4.11.0 → 4.14.0, which resolve `Microsoft.OpenApi` 2.0.0 → 2.7.5 (GHSA-v5pm-xwqc-g5wc, first patched 2.7.5) and `SSH.NET` 2025.1.0 → 2026.0.0 (GHSA-q939-rpr3-3284, first patched 2026.0.0). Both were fixed by moving the *direct* dependency forward rather than pinning a transitive override, so each package stays at a version its own consumer declares. `dotnet list package --vulnerable --include-transitive` now reports all ten projects clean, and the contract-surface snapshot matched byte-for-byte across the `Microsoft.OpenApi` major-minor jump — the first real regression the snapshot was asked to catch, and it proved the document did not move.

> - **Review round 1 (2026-10-17): needs fixes — corrected here, awaiting repeat review.** The independent review reproduced the build, every suite, the export hash and runtime parity, and found four gaps in required edge cases that today's 101 operations do not exercise. 4.2–4.4 went back to unchecked; 4.1 stands.
>   1. **[P2] Filter factories hid authorization.** `[AllowAnonymous]` + `[ServiceFilter(typeof(TokenAuthenticateFilter))]` returned 401 without a token but was published as `security: []`. Fix:
>      - the resolver now classifies every descriptor without instantiating it — `[TypeFilter]` by implementation type and arguments (so a plain `TypeFilter(typeof(TokenAuthorizeFilter))` has its roles read, and malformed arguments fail), and `[ServiceFilter]` by service type (a wrapped identity/branch filter counts; a wrapped `TokenAuthorizeFilter` fails because its roles are not declared; interface/abstract or unmodeled services fail);
>      - any other `IFilterFactory` fails generation, except the three framework factories observed on all 101 production actions (`SaveTempDataAttribute`, `ClientErrorResultFilterFactory`, `ModelStateInvalidFilterFactory`), which never create an authorization filter.
>   2. **[P2] Shared numeric schemas aborted the document.** Using `ResponseProductJson` as both body and result made `/openapi/v1.json` return 500. Fix: a widened component that both directions reach keeps its input form, and a deterministic `{id}Output` copy is added for responses (also for components that only reference a split one). Every response reference is rewired to it, response roots are copied before narrowing, and an existing `{id}Output` id fails generation. Requiredness, formats, nullability and named enums carry over unchanged, because the copy is the generated schema itself.
>   3. **[P2] Nested number handling was ignored.** Inline descendants inherited their parent's decision, so an inlined child member written as a string was published as `integer`. Fix:
>      - an operation transformer stamps each operation (in non-serialized `Metadata`) with the response CLR types ApiExplorer declares; that replaces the schema-name lookup;
>      - the document transformer walks each response schema alongside the MVC serializer's `JsonTypeInfo`. Object properties use property, then declaring type, then options. Collection elements and dictionary values inherit the member's handling one level deep, which System.Text.Json behavior was verified to do.
>      - It fails on: a number with a custom converter; a number nested in a collection of collections whose handling differs from the options; a schema property the serializer does not write; one component reached as two CLR types; a response without a declared type; and an unmodeled keyword.
>   4. **[P3] Mixed-token precedence was misdescribed.** The resolver still refuses identity plus branch filters, but its message and the docs now state the observed behavior. With the branch filter first, a branch token passes, because the identity filter returns the user the branch filter cached. With the identity filter first, both tokens are refused. No runtime auth, cache or serializer code changed.

>   - **Fix.** The walk now carries each value's inherited handling and resolves it with System.Text.Json's precedence, verified case by case against real serialization:
>     - a member value takes its property attribute, else its declaring type's attribute;
>     - a collection or dictionary then falls back to its own type's attribute, then to the options;
>     - elements take their collection's handling;
>     - a collection that is itself an element starts again from its own type (`List<List<int>>` under a string-writing declaring type writes inner numbers, and `List<StringList>` writes inner strings).

A generic comparator walks each published response schema against the actual response JSON and requires a string alternative exactly where the wire has a string, with no precedence rule restated in the test.
