## Milestone 7.7 — Domain Integrity & Close-Day Contract Fixes

**Goal:** Close the integrity and operator-UX holes surfaced by the 2026-07 design-phase catalog review (design M0 / 0.3.3 close-out; this milestone owns gap-register items **A, B, D, E, F–M, O, S, T, and W** in `design/docs/screens.md`, plus **X, Y, and Z** from the 2026-10 prototype review): the counting flow that cannot show its variance before submit (D+F), the approved variance that can silently go stale, the month lock that is only a UI gate, the frontend-support contracts design 0.4.2 depends on, the Work Queue backlog aggregate (W), and the smaller contract conveniences the screen catalog currently composes around. Design M4 blueprints for the affected screens are gated on this milestone's outcomes.

**Scope boundary:** Hardening/expanding existing endpoints plus a small number of new commands; no visual/frontend work. The fiado *settlement* implementation stays in this milestone (Phase 5) — M9.5 is verification/documentation-only and serves as an input, never the owner. Every factual contract change bumps the shared Spec revision and runs `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh`; endpoint contracts and persistence behavior are pinned in **WebApi.Test** (UseCases.Test covers use-case logic only).

**Precondition:** Independent of M7.6 (can run before or in parallel). Phase 1 decisions (owner: dev team) gate the phases marked with their item number; Phase 2 needs only 1.5, and Phase 6 items 6.1–6.4 have no decision dependency (6.5/6.6 need 1.7).

### Phase 1 — Decision sign-off *(owner: dev-team; each outcome is recorded in the gap register + the relevant §6 spec note before its implementation phase starts)*

- [x] **1.1** **Gap G — submitted/approved close vs a moving ledger.** Verified: transaction create / draft-finalize / cancel guard only `LockDate`, never the close state of their `(account, date)` — an approved close's persisted variance goes stale and feeds the dashboard, cash-variance summary, and monthly reconciliation. Choose one of two **approval-preserving** strategies: **(a)** freeze the account/day ledger at submit, with explicit reopen + re-approval; or **(b)** any variance-affecting ledger mutation invalidates the close and forces re-review — and the strategy must define the **`Submitted`** case explicitly, not just `Approved`: under (a) the freeze starts at submit; under (b) a mutation against a `Submitted` close either blocks or returns the close to `Draft` for resubmission. Live recomputation on read surfaces may **supplement** either (as a divergence display aid) but is not a standalone option — recompute-only leaves an `Approved` close whose changed number nobody approved. Also decide two companion rules: outstanding `Draft` rows at submit → block, or force a finalize/cancel prompt; and whether the silent `Submitted → Draft` recall-on-item-save is replaced by an explicit **Recall submission** action (recommended — a state transition hidden inside an ordinary save is a footgun).
- [x] **1.2** **Gap K — atomic month-lock contract.** Confirm the lock-month command shape (e.g. `POST /setting/lock-month {Year, Month}`) and its server-enforced condition set — every existing close `Approved`, zero `Draft` transactions, expected terminal closers all closed, month not in the future — plus the definitions that make those checks well-founded:
  - **Expected `(terminal, date)` pairs:** which dates count — weekends (lotéricas open Saturdays), branch holidays, explicitly closed days, and account/operator activation-deactivation windows. The M7.5 expected-closer rule is per-date and link-based; the month check needs the date dimension defined.
  - **Current-month rule:** whether locking the current, unfinished month is prohibited (recommended) or allowed through today.
  - **Whole-interval validation:** the command validates every month in `(current LockDate, requested month]` — May cannot be skipped by locking June directly.
  - **Initial state:** a new branch seeds `LockDate = DateTime.MinValue` (`CreateBranchSeedFactory`), so "earliest unlocked month" resolves to year 1 — define the floor (e.g. branch `CreatedAt` month, or first data month).
  - **Concurrency:** transaction isolation so a ledger write cannot race the readiness check and land inside a just-locked month (serializable check-and-set, or equivalent guard).
  - **Empty-month rule:** whether a month with expected closers but **no closes at all** is lockable (today `LockReady` is `closes.All(...)` — vacuously true when empty).
  - **CEF attestation:** whether locking requires an explicit manager attestation that the CEF borderô was reconciled (§7.2), or that stays out of scope for MVP.
  Confirm `LockDate` becomes read-only through `PUT /setting` (the Settings catalog entry already notes this).
- [x] **1.3** **Gap H — fiado settlement representation.** Verified: balance is `Out − In` ignoring `PaidAt` (§6.4) while aging keys on `PaidAt IS NULL` **direction-unfiltered** (`BuildOpenReceivablesQuery`) — settling a sale row removes it from aging without reducing the balance, and repayment `In` rows appear in aging as debt. Choose: client-level balance + explicit *Receber pagamento* flow (per-row `PaidAt` dropped from fiado semantics; leanest) **or** payment allocation with partial settlement (fullest). **Implementation stays in this milestone (Phase 5)** — M9.5 is verification/documentation-only by its own scope boundary, so it may *inform* this decision (Phase 5 may sequence after M9.5 Phase 2.2's shop verification) but never absorbs the implementation. Sub-decision: **gap A** — the Member-visible fiado reference on the close-day form (member-scoped balance read scoped to the close's paired Tab, or drop the §6.5 reference display for Members).
- [x] **1.4** **Gap I — Rejected-close edit window.** §6.13 lets the recording Member edit a `Rejected` close only on the close's own local day, but §7.2 reviews happen next morning — resubmission is impossible for the operator in the normal flow. Preferred: recording operator may edit an explicitly `Rejected` close until the period locks (same-day rule retained for voluntary `Submitted → Draft` recall only); alternative: prior-day corrections become manager-owned and §7.1 is amended.
- [x] **1.5** **Gap J — `DailyClose.Notes`.** Add the write path (recommended — the approval screen needs the variance cause) or drop the column. Today every read DTO returns it and no request DTO can set it.
- [x] **1.6** **Gap O — time-policy dating model.** Choose between **(a)** effective-dated policy rows — as a separate `TimeEntryPolicy` entity, **not** by versioning the branch's unique `Setting` row (it carries `LockDate` and is 1-per-branch by constraint) — or **(b)** a per-entry policy snapshot stamped at entry creation/edit. Either way, changing `DailyTargetHours` / lunch tiers stops rewriting historical balances. Includes the migration/backfill rule for existing branches (the current constants become the initial policy).
- [x] **1.7** **Concurrency + idempotency contract (design 0.4.2 dependency).** The frontend patterns item promises idempotent financial submits and a stale-state convention with no server owner until now. Decide: the idempotency mechanism for financial creates (e.g. a client-supplied `Idempotency-Key` / request id honored by `POST /transaction`, `/transaction/installment`, and the 1.3 payment command if it ships) and the optimistic-concurrency signal for mutations (e.g. an `UpdatedAt`/row-version precondition → 409/412 stale-state response, or an explicit last-write-wins declaration per endpoint).
- [x] **1.8** **Terminal ↔ Tab assignment pairing (gaps L + S).** The workstation-first UX auto-routes fiado to the terminal's paired Tab, which collides with explicit `OperatorAccount` scope. Decide: assigning a Terminal auto-assigns its paired Tab, implies Tab permission without a row, or requires both assignments explicitly (and what happens on pair/unpair after assignment). Also decide the **deactivation rule (gap S)** — verified: `DeactivateAccountUseCase` clears operator links but never the pairing, so an active Terminal can point at an inactive Tab and an inactive Terminal keeps reserving its Tab. Deactivating either side must atomically unpair, or be blocked with a verbatim key while paired. Implementation + tests land in Phase 5.
- [x] **1.9** **Gap G close-chain amendment.** The 1.1 account/day ledger freeze does not protect a later close whose §6.12 opening is derived from an earlier close's counted items. Decide the opening-source eligibility signal; which dependent close loses official status after a real counted-item change; operator correction access; the item-save response; account-wide coordination participants; the Terminal activity/open-close binding and intervening-activity Submit rule; and bounded, cancellable coordination failure semantics. This is an amendment to 1.1, not a replacement: ledger mutations remain frozen from Submit and no official variance is silently recomputed.

**Phase 1 decision outcomes — signed off by the dev team 2026-07-27; amended 2026-07-28 (reviewer follow-ups), 2026-08-11 (Phase 3 integrity-review follow-ups), and 2026-08-19 (Phase 4 floor-integrity audit).** *(Recorded here and in the design gap register; the §6 spec rewrites + shared-spec revision bumps ride with each implementation phase's own spec-sync step, not this sign-off.)*

- **1.1 (Gap G) → (a) freeze at submit.** Submitting seals the `(Branch, Account, Date)` ledger: create / installment create / finalize / cancel are rejected while a non-`Draft` close exists for that key. Graduated, explicit undo — a `Submitted` close → operator self-serve **Recall** (`POST /dailyclose/{id}/recall`, replacing the silent `Submitted → Draft` on item save); an `Approved` close → manager **Reopen** → `Draft` → re-submit → re-approve. Companion rules confirmed: **submit is blocked while outstanding `Draft` rows exist** on the `(account, date)` (finalize/cancel first); recompute-on-read may supplement as a divergence display only, never as the standalone strategy.
- **1.2 (Gap K) → atomic `POST /setting/lock-month {Year, Month}`**, with `LockDate` read-only via `PUT /setting`. Expected `(terminal, date)` pairs are **activity-based** — any terminal with a close (any state) or **direct** cash-ledger activity on the Terminal account that date; **no operating-day calendar**, and weekend / holiday / activation-window dates carry no special rule (they are expected only when they carry direct activity). Per **decision #3 (2026-07-28)**, **fiado on a paired Tab does not by itself make its Terminal expected**. Readiness = all such closes `Approved` + zero `Draft` transactions across the validated interval. **Locking the current, unfinished month is prohibited** — only fully-elapsed months lock, so `LockDate` always lands on a month's last day. Whole-interval validation (every month in `(current LockDate, requested]`; May cannot be skipped by locking June). **Floor amendment (2026-08-19):** branch `CreatedAt` month is the no-data default for the `DateTime.MinValue` seed, not an assertion that earlier history is absent; any earlier active DailyClose or non-soft-deleted Transaction lowers the effective floor to its business month. This preserves legitimate backdating/imports while preventing the command from sealing unvalidated pre-creation data. Clean earlier data requires no manager action; an unresolved intermediate error names its `MM/yyyy`. An empty month with expected closers is **not** lockable. CEF borderô attestation is **out of scope for MVP**. Readiness runs under a serializable / row-lock check-and-set (with the 1.7 `xmin` guard) so a ledger write cannot race it.
- **1.3 (Gap H) → client-level balance + explicit *Receber pagamento* flow.** Per-row `PaidAt` drops out of fiado settlement; balance stays `Out − In` per client; a repayment is an explicit `In` row via the *Receber pagamento* command (honoring the 1.7 Idempotency-Key); the open-receivables / aging query stops surfacing `In` rows and stops keying on per-row `PaidAt`. **Aging (decision #1) → FIFO query-time netting:** a client's `Out` rows ordered oldest-first, cumulative `In` subtracted, only the unpaid remainder shown and aged — partial payments fall out naturally and an overpayment (net-negative) reads as a client credit with empty aging. No per-row settlement state, no allocation table; satisfies Phase 5.4 coherence. **Gap A:** the close-day form's member fiado reference is a member-scoped **paired-Tab** balance read, shown only to an operator who holds the explicit Tab assignment (juniors without Tab access see none). Phase 5 may sequence after M9.5 Phase 2.2.
- **1.3 amended by owner decisions 2026-10-01 → 2026-10-07 (prototype review).** (i) **Linked Terminal movements (M9.5 Phase 2.2 option (a)):** a fiado sale that hands out counted stock also writes a generated Terminal stock saída on the Tab's paired terminal; a cash repayment into that terminal's counted drawer writes a generated Terminal cash entrada; a non-cash repayment writes none. Both rows or neither, finalized/cancelled together, generated rows read-only and identified by structured, unforgeable origin data; the §6.12 formula is unchanged and the Tab balance is never added to it. (ii) **Gap A reversed (2026-10-05):** the close-day fiado reference is removed for every role — no member-scoped paired-Tab read is built. (iii) Repayments only through *Receber pagamento*; its method is required with no default; the generic create cannot bypass it. (iv) `OldestUnpaidDate` is the oldest unpaid **sale** date. (v) The operator summary includes generated rows and reports their share. Spec forward markers: §3.12, §6.4, §6.5, §6.12, §6.14 (v43).
- **1.4 (Gap I) → immutable first-count recorder edits until lock + manager may also correct.** Open stamps its user but does not claim a recorder. The first successful item save, including empty, atomically stamps the immutable recorder user and nullable linked operator; before that claim, any account-scoped Member may first-count an unlocked close and account coordination makes the first claim win. The recording operator may edit an explicitly `Rejected` close until the period locks; a Manager/Admin may also correct it directly. Submit stamps a separate current submitter identity and never reassigns the recorder. The same-day rule survives only for the voluntary `Submitted → Draft` recall.
- **1.5 (Gap J) → add the write path.** `Notes` is **editable on the `Draft` item-save path (`PUT /items`)** so it is never lost while the operator works, **frozen at submit**, cleared with an empty string; concurrency rides the close's `xmin` like any item edit (max 1000, verbatim validation). Surfaced on Daily-close approval. Column kept.
- **1.6 (Gap O) → (a) effective-dated `TimeEntryPolicy` entity.** New entity carrying `DailyTargetHours` + lunch tiers with effective dates; `Setting` keeps `LockDate` and is **not** versioned. `ITimeEntryCalculationService` callers resolve the policy row applicable to each entry's date. EF migration backfills one policy row per existing branch from today's constants; the seed factory creates the initial row for new branches. (Phase 7 branch (a).)
- **1.7 → Idempotency-Key + optimistic concurrency via Postgres `xmin`.** `POST /transaction`, `POST /transaction/installment`, and the *Receber pagamento* command honor a client `Idempotency-Key` (same key → one write + replayed response; concurrent same-key → exactly one write via atomic reservation; same key + different payload → conflict). Mutating endpoints (transaction update/finalize/cancel, close item edits, settings, lock) carry an `xmin` row-version precondition → **409** verbatim on stale writes (no schema migration). This is design 0.4.2's server half — idempotent submits preserved, safe blind-retry on an uncertain save. The Gap G freeze and month-lock guards use an internal DB transaction lock for atomicity, with `xmin` as the client-visible signal.
- **1.8 (Gaps L + S) → require both assignments explicitly + atomic unpair.** A Terminal assignment grants till operation only; Tab (fiado authority) requires a separate explicit `OperatorAccount(Tab)` assignment — Tabs are senior/trusted grants, not defaults. The pairing names the fiado **routing target** (`Terminal → Tab`); the explicit Tab assignment gates **who** may record. **Deactivating either side atomically unpairs**, enforced both directions. **Pairing is terminal-centric (decision #4):** the Tab belongs to the Terminal (existing schema, no migration); "personal to a senior" is expressed purely at the assignment layer — only that senior holds the Tab's assignment. **Access is explicit but multi-terminal:** an operator may hold Tab assignments across several terminals, and a manager grants/revokes the covering terminal's Tab as a first-class action, so a roaming senior is covered while juniors simply get no Tab assignment — **never an implied grant**.
- **1.9 (Gap G close chain) → invalidate the next eligible opening-chain successor explicitly.** A close becomes an opening source on its first successful `PUT /items`, including an explicit empty count; that coordinated save also claims immutable `RecordedByUserId` and nullable `RecordedByOperatorId`, while Open records only `OpenedByUserId`. Submit is rejected until that count exists and stamps separate current-submitter user/operator fields without changing correction ownership. Because this contract first ships against an empty development database, every close follows the new first-save eligibility rule and there is no legacy-close initialization path. When a real counted-item or first-eligibility change moves a later close's opening, the earliest later eligible `Submitted`, `Approved`, or `Rejected` close returns to `Draft` with a dedicated opening-recheck timestamp, source-close FK, and triggering-user audit; a Rejected successor retains `RejectionReason`. The immutable recording operator may correct/resubmit that cascade-demoted past close until lock, even when a different Manager submitted it, while a direct prior-day Manager/Admin Recall or Reopen remains a Manager-owned correction. The cascade is one eligible chain node, recomputed only at re-submit, and `PUT /items` returns the affected successor explicitly as a straight breaking change to the unreleased contract. All close-history and Terminal ledger writes serialize on one `(BranchId, AccountId)` transaction-scoped advisory key: Open, item-save, Submit, Approve, Reject, Recall, Reopen, transaction create/installment/finalize/cancel. Terminal create/installment requires an active close for that account/day; Tab and Bank remain exempt. Submit also blocks an earlier active-transaction day after `max(opening-source date, LockDate)` when that day has no counted close. Coordination uses a stable infrastructure-computed 64-bit key, propagates request cancellation, and bounds acquisition with transaction-local PostgreSQL `lock_timeout = 5s`; SQLSTATE `55P03` maps to a declared retryable `409` resource key. The same-day freeze-at-submit decision 1.1 remains unchanged.

### Phase 2 — Close-day form contract *(gaps D + F + J; needs only 1.5)*

- [x] **2.1** Expand the review projection (`GET /dailyclose/{dailyCloseId}/review` or a sibling read) to enumerate **all active products** ordered by `DisplayOrder` — a fresh, just-opened close included: per item `ProductId`, `ProductName`, `DisplayOrder`, server-derived `OpeningValue?` (§6.5; the variance row stays `null`), **nullable** not-yet-entered `ClosingValue`, `IsCashVarianceProduct`. **This is a breaking DTO change, not additive** — `ClosingValue` goes `decimal` → `decimal?` (and rows now exist for never-entered products): version it deliberately, update the M7.6 OpenAPI snapshot expectations, and note it in the spec revision. Kills the client-side three-call composition and the "Diferença Caixa" name-match.
- [x] **2.2** **Pre-submit variance preview** for `Draft` closes: live §6.12 computation via the **same `ICashVarianceCalculator` submit uses**. The **required** shape is `POST /dailyclose/{dailyCloseId}/variance-preview` accepting **candidate item values** — the operator needs the variance *while typing*, before anything is saved; a computed field on the expanded review (saved values only) is an optional supplement, not a substitute. Never persists: no `IUnitOfWork` dependency; extend the never-commits architecture scan to the new namespace.
- [x] **2.3** `Notes` write path per 1.5 — **editable on the `Draft` `PUT /items` path** (frozen at submit; cleared with an empty string; concurrency via the close's `xmin`; max length 1000, verbatim validation errors); already returned on review/get.
- [x] **2.4** Tests — UseCases.Test: fresh-close enumeration, prior-close opening mapping, preview/submit variance parity. WebApi.Test: a fresh close returns the full product set with null closings; the preview leaves persisted state unchanged (reload assertion); `Notes` round-trip submit → review. Architecture scan green.
- [x] **2.5** Spec sync: §6.5/§6.13/§6.14 review-contract updates; design gaps D/F/J flagged for catalog re-sync.

### Phase 3 — Ledger, close, and opening-chain integrity *(gaps G ⛔ 1.1 + 1.9 · L · I ⛔ 1.4)*

> **Completed 2026-08-12 as the first expanded Phase 3 delivery.** The original uncommitted 3.1–3.7 work was reworked in place with the signed 2026-08-11 close-chain amendment; there is no layered follow-up implementation and no predecessor-wide automatic cascade.

- [x] **3.1 Same-day ledger freeze + authoritative variance.** Preserve decision 1.1 exactly: transaction create, installment create, draft-finalize, and cancel reject while an active non-`Draft` close exists for the same `(BranchId, AccountId, Date)`. Submit/Approve and the four ledger mutations share the persistence-level coordination boundary; Submit enters it before the outstanding-Draft check and §6.12 calculation and holds it through commit. Review, dashboard, cash-variance summary, monthly reconciliation, and ordinary close responses must never substitute live recomputation for the persisted official non-Draft variance; live values remain hypothetical/divergence-only. Align the variance transaction sum with the ledger guards by requiring both `Transaction.Active` and `Status = Active`. WebApi.Test pins every mutation × `Submitted`/`Approved` (and the stricter `Rejected`) matrix and the four official surfaces.
- [x] **3.2 Submit prerequisites + uncounted-activity range.** Submit rejects matching account/day Draft ledger rows (`DAILYCLOSE_OUTSTANDING_DRAFT_TRANSACTIONS`) and rejects the current close when no successful item save has recorded a count (`DAILYCLOSE_ITEMS_NOT_RECORDED`: *“Registre a contagem do fechamento antes de enviá-lo”*); an explicit empty item save remains a valid count of zero. Extract `ILockDateReader` from `LockDateGuard` (missing `Setting` → `DateTime.MinValue`) and add a guard overload that accepts the resolved value, so Submit resolves the eligible opening source and branch `LockDate` exactly once inside coordination and reuses both. Reject with the earliest offending date when any date in `(max(opening-source date or MinValue, LockDate), close date)` has active, non-soft-deleted transactions but no active counted close (`DAILYCLOSE_PRIOR_DAY_NOT_COUNTED`: *“Existe um dia anterior com movimentações e sem contagem registrada ({0}); registre a contagem desse dia antes de enviar este fechamento”*). The interval is exclusive at both ends, ignores Draft/Cancelled transactions, and always has an escape path by counting the blocking day. Drive the query from `ITransactionsRepository.GetEarliestUncountedActivityDateByAccountAsNoTracking`; reuse the resolved opening source in the calculator rather than querying it twice.
- [x] **3.3 Terminal/open-day contract (gap L).** `POST /dailyclose` is Terminal-only; Members open branch-local today, Manager/Admin may backdate only before lock, and every role rejects future dates. Remove the unused `accountId` parameter from `IDailyCloseWorkflowGuard.EnsureCanOpen` and its callers/tests. Complete the drawer binding from 1.9: split the ledger guard so create/installment call `EnsureLedgerAcceptsNewRow` and require an active Terminal close on `(BranchId, AccountId, Date)` (`TRANSACTION_REQUIRES_OPEN_DAILY_CLOSE`: *“É necessário abrir o fechamento diário desta conta e data antes de registrar movimentações”*), while Tab/Bank writes remain exempt and finalize/cancel retain null-tolerant `EnsureLedgerIsMutable` for existing/legacy rows. WebApi.Test covers Terminal/Tab/Bank, every role/date case, missing/Draft/non-Draft close distinctions, persistence rollback on rejection, and the successful retry after Open.
- [x] **3.4 Rejected correction + correction ownership (gap I).** Keep the current contract: `Rejected` remains frozen; direct resubmission is allowed; the first permitted correction save transitions it to `Draft` while retaining `RejectionReason`; no separate “unfreeze Rejected” endpoint is introduced. Ownership is the immutable first-count recorder, not the opener or current submitter: an unclaimed unlocked close may be first-counted by any scoped Member; a later Manager submit cannot steal the correction window. The recording operator can edit and resubmit that correction until lock, and Manager/Admin may also correct it. A close cascade-demoted by a predecessor receives the separate opening-recheck correction window for its recording operator. By contrast, a direct prior-day Manager/Admin Recall or Reopen is a **Manager-owned correction**; it does not synthesize rejection or opening-recheck lineage to grant the Member access. Pin the complete role × state × date matrix.
- [x] **3.5 Explicit Recall + pt-BR contract.** `POST /dailyclose/{id}/recall` is the only ordinary `Submitted → Draft` command; item-save against Submitted returns `DAILYCLOSE_NOT_EDITABLE` and never recalls silently. The recording Member's voluntary Recall remains same-branch-local-day only; Manager/Admin may Recall pre-lock, with prior-day ownership per 3.4. Recall clears `SubmittedAt` and any superseded opening-recheck fields while retaining `ItemsFirstRecordedAt` and the physical CashVariance row. Keep production `Recall` identifiers, route, and resource-key names, but localize the user-facing action as **“Desfazer envio”** and replace unnatural `recolher/recolhido` resource text with `desfazer o envio/envio desfeito`. Document and test the intentional status split: same-day Member permission failure is `403`; invalid state or locked period is `409`.
- [x] **3.6 Manager Reopen.** `POST /dailyclose/{id}/reopen` is Manager/Admin-only and moves `Approved → Draft`, clears `ApprovedAt`, `ApprovedByUser*`, `SubmittedAt`, and any superseded opening-recheck fields, retains the physical CashVariance row and counted-source eligibility, stamps the audit pair, respects `LockDate`, and requires the full submit/approve cycle again. It unfreezes same-day ledger mutations and follows the Manager-owned prior-day correction rule in 3.4. > **Phase 3 completion note (2026-08-12; identity audit amended 2026-08-13 and findings audit amended 2026-08-14 in the same first delivery).** The combined 3.1–3.13 implementation is green with the schema-only `20260812201910_M7_7Phase3DailyCloseIntegrity` migration applied through clean `Database.MigrateAsync()` databases. The identity audit split immutable opener/first-count recorder/current submitter, added the first-save Member claim under the existing account lock, and proved that an elevated opener without an Operator remains reachable while a Manager-on-behalf Submit cannot steal correction ownership. The findings audit made CashVariance suppression ID-only and a required mapping input on every ordinary rich-close response, with an unloaded-`Product` regression; widened SQLSTATE `55P03` normalization through EF exception wrappers; localized the blocking activity date as `dd/MM/yyyy`; removed the test-only `LockDateGuard` constructor and dead item `AddRange`; introduced the successor invalidator interface; and completed cancellation propagation across close-list/dashboard/monthly/variance report reads. The exact-key PostgreSQL timeout test holds the advisory key past the transaction-local five-second boundary; both advisory acquisition and a separately forced row-lock timeout during `SaveChangesAsync` return the retryable conflict, leave no partial state, and succeed after release. Cancellation likewise leaves no partial state and the retry succeeds. Forced grant-order tests use an explicit test-only 30-second timeout and assert the observed first waiter remains inside that budget; production remains at five seconds. Account coordination now emits structured wait/hold timing on every scope. The reproducible real-request timing probe (deliberate 250 ms external hold) measured **258.09 ms wait / 62.33 ms hold / 614.97 ms request**. A like-for-like warm 16-test transaction-create/item-save slice measured **9.79 s at committed v34 → 9.68 s at v35**; no optimization widened the protected boundary. Final gates after the findings audit: build 0 errors; Validator **304/304**; UseCase **1025/1025**; WebApi + architecture **869/869** in **153 s**; EF reports no pending model changes; doc sync green at **v35**. The six surviving `Pending M7.7 change` markers belong only to later phases: pairing, fiado settlement, Gap-A fiado reference, atomic month lock, effective-dated time policy, and project-wide idempotency/concurrency.

### Phase 4 — Atomic month lock *(gap K ⛔ 1.2)*

> **Hard precondition:** the expanded Phase 3 account coordination, opening-chain invalidation, and uncounted-activity protections must be complete. A month cannot be declared ready while an approved close can still become silently stale.

- [x] **4.1** Lock-month command per the full 1.2 contract — expected-pair definition, current-month rule, **whole-interval validation** (every month in `(current LockDate, requested month]` must pass), the data-aware operational floor (`CreatedAt` month unless earlier active operational data exists), and the readiness check running under the 1.2 concurrency guard so a ledger, close-history write, or TimeEntry mutation cannot bypass or race it; one verbatim error key per unmet condition (unapproved close, draft transactions, missing expected closer, future month, unvalidated intermediate month, empty-month rule). A non-MinValue legacy boundary below the operational floor clamps upward instead of scanning empty centuries; an impossible current/future legacy value from the retired PUT path is repaired only through a successful command that validates from the floor. Keep expected pairs defined as an existing close **or direct active Terminal activity**: Phase 3 makes activity imply a close for future API writes, but the direct-activity branch remains a required safety net for historical/imported/persistence-bypassed data.
- [x] **4.2** `PUT /setting` stops accepting `LockDate` (or rejects it with a verbatim key pointing at the command) — the command becomes the only lock path.
- [x] **4.3** Tests — WebApi.Test matrix: each blocker rejects with its key; success advances `LockDate` to the month's last day; locking June with May unresolved is rejected (interval validation); a fresh branch with no earlier data uses its CreatedAt month; earlier close/transaction data lowers the floor, blocks when unresolved with its exact `MM/yyyy`, and passes silently when clean; locking the same month twice is a clean conflict/no-op; a future request month is rejected; ancient partial and future legacy boundaries have bounded, validated recovery. Real PostgreSQL concurrency proves that neither a ledger write, TimeEntry full snapshot, whole-entry deactivation, granular segment correction, prior-day item correction, first count, Recall, nor Reopen can land inside a successfully locked month, that a pre-floor write winning the race is discovered before lock, that two concurrent lock commands serialize to success + already-locked, that a real advisory timeout returns the settings key, and that a Phase 3 cascade-demoted Draft blocks readiness. Direct HTTP tests also pin `TIMEENTRY_DATE_LOCKED` on both entry-level routes, the live monthly-reconciliation projection, and the lock-month OpenAPI path. Define and test canonical acquisition order between the month-level guard and Phase 3 account coordination so no path can deadlock. UseCases.Test: readiness evaluation against the **activity-derived** expected-closer set — a terminal is expected on a date iff it has a close or **direct** terminal cash-ledger activity there; weekend / holiday / activation-window dates are covered purely by activity presence, imported/historical no-close activity is still detected, and **paired-Tab fiado alone does not make its Terminal expected** (decision #3).
- [x] **4.4** Spec sync: §6.6/§6.14 + the monthly-reconciliation contract note; design gap K + the Monthly-reconciliation and Settings entries flagged for catalog re-sync.

> **Phase 4 completion note (2026-08-15; floor-integrity and findings audits amended 2026-08-19).** Items 4.1–4.4 shipped as spec `v36`; the floor audit advanced the contract to `v37`, and the findings audit advances it to `v38`, still without a schema change or migration. The command owns the sole `LockDate` write path and validates the entire unlocked interval under an exclusive branch advisory boundary. `CreatedAt` remains the no-data default and earlier active closes/non-soft-deleted transactions lower the floor; ancient partial legacy boundaries clamp upward to that floor, while an invalid current/future legacy value can be replaced only by a command that validates from the floor. All Phase 3 ledger/close-history participants acquire the same boundary shared before their account-history key; transaction update and every TimeEntry mutation (`PUT /timeentry`, whole-entry DELETE, and granular segment POST/PUT/DELETE) also use the shared boundary and recheck `LockDate` inside coordination. Month and account timeouts are separately configurable, and nested PostgreSQL `55P03` is normalized to the settings busy key at the month boundary. Real PostgreSQL forced-order tests cover ledger-write-first/lock-first (including a pre-floor Draft winner), full time-entry snapshot, whole-entry deactivation, segment correction, prior correction, first count, Recall, Reopen, two simultaneous lock commands, actual timeout, and cascade-demoted Draft outcomes without deadlock or partial persistence. HTTP tests also pin direct-Terminal `MissingExpectedClose` projection and the lock-month OpenAPI contract. Final findings-audit gates: build 0 errors; Validator **310/310**; UseCase **1057/1057**; WebApi + architecture **904/904** in **194 s**; EF reports no pending model changes; doc sync green at `v38`; diff check clean. Five `Pending M7.7 change` markers remain and belong only to later phases: pairing, fiado settlement, Gap-A fiado reference, effective-dated time policy, and project-wide idempotency/concurrency.

### Phase 5 — Fiado settlement coherence + account pairing *(gaps H + A + S ⛔ 1.3 (amended 2026-10) + 1.8, and ⛔ 1.7 for the payment command; M9.5 Phase 2.2's fiado × drawer question is answered by the 1.3 amendment — implementation lives here, M9.5 stays verification/documentation-only)*

> **Phase 3 boundary:** the transaction-requires-open-close rule is Terminal-only. Tab fiado rows and a non-cash *Receber pagamento* never require or create a DailyClose. A fiado operation that writes a linked Terminal row (a counted-stock sale, a cash repayment) needs the paired terminal's open Draft close exactly as any Terminal write does — the Tab row inherits that requirement only through the atomic pair; nothing creates a close implicitly.

- [x] **5.1** Implement the chosen representation: repository/query changes — the open-receivables query stops surfacing repayment `In` rows **and applies FIFO oldest-first netting of cumulative `In` against a client's `Out` rows so aging shows only the unpaid remainder** (decision #1; partial + overpayment covered) — and the *Receber pagamento* command (honoring the 1.7 Idempotency-Key), which carries the receiving Tab `AccountId`: a Tab the caller holds an explicit grant for when a Member, any branch Tab for Manager/Admin. Netting stays branch-wide, so the receiving Tab records only where the money came in. The allocation model is **not** taken. The command's payment method (the repayment transaction type) is **required, with no default**; the generic `POST /transaction` must not be a way around the command — it rejects the fiado repayment types or applies the same rule (contract choice), identified structurally (requires Tab account + client, category direction `In`) or by an explicit marker (contract choice). **Netting partition (mandated by coherence, verified against shipped code):** by `(Branch, Client)` with `Out` rows ordered `DueDate, Date, Id` — matching `ListFiadoBalancesByBranchIdAsNoTracking` (`GroupBy(ClientId)`, branch-wide, no `accountId`) and the existing aging order, so a client's aging stays consistent with `GET /report/fiado/balance` **even when the client owes across multiple Tab accounts**; an `accountId`-scoped view filters **after** netting, never before. WebApi.Test pins a multi-Tab client: a payment nets the branch-wide oldest debt first, and balance = aging remainder.
- [x] **5.2** **Gap A — removed (owner decision 2026-10-05, superseding the signed 1.3 sub-decision).** No close-day fiado reference for any role and **no** member-scoped paired-Tab balance read: a raw Tab balance shows where movements were registered, not what clients owe. Deliverable: §6.5 drops the "form displays it for reference" statement; explicit Tab grants keep gating fiado operations and the client statement (5.3, 5.5). WebApi.Test: none beyond 5.3/5.5's grant boundaries — there is no endpoint to pin.
- [x] **5.3** **Terminal↔Tab pairing semantics per 1.8 (gaps L + S):** the pairing stays **terminal-centric** (Tab owned by the Terminal — no schema change); member-scope resolution grants Tab access **only** through an explicit `OperatorAccount(Tab)` row (multi-terminal assignments allowed for a roaming operator), never implied by the Terminal assignment. `DELETE /account/{id}` **atomically unpairs** both directions. WebApi.Test: deactivating a Terminal with a paired Tab, and deactivating a Tab paired to an active Terminal, both unpair atomically; a junior with only a Terminal assignment cannot record fiado; a senior holding two terminals' Tab assignments can record on both.
- [x] **5.4** Coherence tests (WebApi.Test): a repayment never appears as a receivable; settling per the chosen semantics reduces the aging surface and the client's exposure consistently with `GET /report/fiado/balance`; a linked fiado pair (5.6) moves the Tab balance and the terminal's §6.12 inputs consistently, with zero difference on a correct count.
- [x] **5.5** **Client fiado statement read (design: Client statement + Receber pagamento, gap H).** `GET /client/{id}/fiado-statement?DateFrom&DateTo&Page&PageSize` — the window is optional (omitted: the client's whole history through branch-local today); `PageSize` defaults to 50 (max 100). Returns `ResponseClientFiadoStatementJson { ClientId, ClientName, AsOfDate, OutstandingTotal, OldestUnpaidDate?, DateFrom, DateTo, OpeningBalance, ClosingBalance, Items, Page, PageSize, TotalCount, TotalPages, HasNext, HasPrevious }`; each item `ResponseClientFiadoStatementItemJson { TransactionId, Date, Direction, Value, AccountId, AccountName, TransactionTypeName, Description?, RunningBalance }`, ordered `Date DESC, CreatedAt DESC, Id DESC` (a documented order). Only active fiado rows count (Draft and Cancelled excluded, matching §6.4). `OutstandingTotal` and `OldestUnpaidDate` use the same branch-wide FIFO netting as 5.1; `OldestUnpaidDate` is the earliest sale `Date` (not `DueDate`) among the client's sales still holding a positive unpaid remainder after netting — computed over all of them, not taken from the first row in due-date order — and null when none remains (owner decision 2026-10-05); `RunningBalance` is computed over the client's full chronological history. Invariants: each row's `RunningBalance` equals the chronologically previous row's `RunningBalance` plus its own `Out − In`, so consecutive rows reconcile on any single page; the window's oldest row starts from `OpeningBalance` and its newest ends at `ClosingBalance`, so `OpeningBalance + Σ(Out − In)` over **all** the window's rows — every page — `= ClosingBalance`; and a window ending today has `ClosingBalance = OutstandingTotal`. No page-level boundary balances are returned — a page is checked through its running balances, the window through all its pages. Access: Manager/Admin any branch client; a Member holding at least one explicit `OperatorAccount(Tab)` grant (1.8) sees the same complete branch-wide statement — fiado is the client's debt with the lotérica, and hiding other Tabs' repayments would break the running balances; a Member with no Tab grant is denied. WebApi.Test covers the window invariants, a multi-Tab client whose repayment on one Tab settles debt on another, the grant boundary, paging stability, and agreement with `GET /report/fiado/balance`.
- [x] **5.6** **Linked Terminal movements (1.3 amendment (i)).** A fiado sale that hands out counted stock writes the Tab `Out` plus a generated Terminal `Out` (*Saída de estoque contado — venda fiado*) on the Tab's paired terminal; a cash repayment writes the Tab `In` plus a generated Terminal `In` (*Entrada de dinheiro — recebimento fiado*); a non-cash repayment writes only the Tab `In`. Seed the two system-only types (never offered for manual create). The pair is atomic under the paired terminal's account coordination: it requires that terminal's open Draft close for the date (a non-Draft close freezes the pair), a Member needs the explicit Tab grant **and** that terminal in scope, Draft legs stay Draft together, finalize/cancel of the source carries the counterpart, and an idempotent replay never creates a second pair. Physical counts are never rewritten. Contract design here: how a sale declares its counted-stock effect (the product-team question in loto-specs §9 stays open), error keys, linked-record DTOs, idempotency envelope, and concurrency. Out of scope until separately decided: cash received elsewhere, mixed tender, bank-side PIX reconciliation, reversals/refunds, legacy links. WebApi.Test: the two R$ 5 reconciliations (stock sale → zero difference at count 995; cash repayment → zero at 1,005), a non-cash repayment with no Terminal row, atomic rejection at a submitted close and without the terminal grant, payment replay, coupled Draft/finalize/cancel.
- [x] **5.7** **Generated-row origin and protection.** Structured, system-owned generation origin and source relationship on the transaction (§3.12), exposed on list and detail DTOs; callers can never set them; the initiating user/operator audit identity is preserved and distinct; update/finalize/cancel of a generated row on its own is rejected server-side (verbatim key); source navigation respects branch and account scope. WebApi.Test: forged origin on create is ignored or rejected, independent mutation of a generated row is refused, list/detail expose the origin.
- [x] **5.8** **Operator summary automatic share (owner decision 2026-10-07).** `GET /report/operator-summary` keeps counting generated rows (they carry the initiating operator); `ResponseOperatorTransactionSummaryJson` gains the generated-row count and `In`/`Out` values, overall and per category (field names are contract design). WebApi.Test: an operator with one cash repayment and one counted-stock sale sees each in the totals and in the automatic share.
- [x] **5.9** Spec sync (covers 5.1–5.8): §3.12/§6.4/§6.5/§6.12/§6.14 + the account-pairing rules (§3.7) rewritten to the chosen semantics, discharging the v43 forward markers; design gaps H/A/S + the fiado, Close day, Transactions list, My transaction summary, and Accounts screens flagged for catalog re-sync.
