> **Phase 5 review-fix note (2026-10-17, same delivery) — the independent review returned "needs fixes" with six findings; all six are corrected and pinned; the repeat review accepted them (see the acceptance note above).** (1) Migration `M7_7Phase5FiadoLinkedMovements` now seeds each branch's system types into **new** categories — an active same-name category gets a disambiguated `— sistema {id prefix}` name instead of hitting the active-name unique index (`23505`), existing categories are never adopted, and a branch already holding a role is skipped — and its downgrade deletes only the system types and the categories hosting them alone; `FiadoLinkedMovementsMigrationTest` now seeds an active and an inactive colliding category, upgrades, downgrades, proves both pre-existing categories and the manager's type survive, and re-upgrades. (2) `POST /transaction/preview` with `HandsOutCountedStock` runs `FiadoPhysicalPairService.ValidateCounterpart` (pairing, Member terminal scope → 403, terminal open Draft close → 409) and projects the cash-variance impact on the paired terminal's close. (3) Create-preview aging membership comes from the shared FIFO projection through the new query-only `IFiadoProjectionRepository` (credit can absorb the sale; a sale dated after `asOfDate` is outside aging). (4) The statement validates its resolved window (`DateFrom` after a defaulted today → 400 `REPORT_DATE_RANGE_INVERTED`). (5) The update response keeps `GeneratedTransactionId`. (6) Statement and aging SQL offsets are 64-bit, so `page=2147483647` returns an empty page instead of 500. New regressions: three HTTP cases in `TransactionControllerFiadoLinkedMovementTest`, two in `ClientControllerFiadoStatementTest`, one use-case case each for the credit preview and the resolved window. Gates after the fixes: build 0 errors; Validator **327/327**; UseCase **1129/1129**; WebApi + architecture **1082/1082** in **3m17s** (contract-surface snapshot unchanged — no contract shape moved); EF reports no pending model changes; at that point the doc-sync checker failed only at the then-held `v49` markers, and a disposable copy at `v50` was green with 34/34 regression scenarios; `git diff --check` clean.

> **Phase 5 progress note (2026-10-17) — items 5.1, 5.2 and 5.4–5.8 implemented and tested (accepted after the review fixes; see the acceptance note above).** Baseline `75dc25e`, spec `v50`. **5.1 read side:** one SQL projection (`FiadoNettingSql`: `fiado` → `client_in` → `sales` → `open_sales`) feeds the balance, aging (`ListFiadoOpenSalesByBranchIdAsNoTracking`/`Count…`), `GetFiadoClientPositionAsNoTracking` and the statement reads; FIFO by `DueDate, Date, Id` per client across every Tab; the account filter applies after netting; aging `Value` is the unpaid remainder; aging input is now bounded by `Date ≤ AsOfDate` like the balance; `PaidAt` plays no part in fiado (the open-receivables query, its row type and two dead filter models were removed). Create/edit previews follow: Tab rows ignore `PaidAt` and a Tab `In` never ages. **5.1 command:** `POST /transaction/fiado-payment` (`RequestReceiveFiadoPaymentJson`, persisted `Idempotency-Key` scoped to `POST /transaction/fiado-payment`); the method is a repayment type carrying `TransactionType.FiadoPaymentKind` (`CountedCash`/`NonCash`), configured through transaction-type create/update and only valid on `In` types that require a Tab and a client; there is no default and no kind is seeded or migrated; generic create/installment/previews refuse a Tab `In` (`TRANSACTION_FIADO_PAYMENT_REQUIRES_COMMAND`) and system types (`TRANSACTION_TYPE_SYSTEM_ONLY`). **5.2:** no runtime close-day fiado read existed; §6.5 now states the removal for every role. **5.5:** `GET /client/{clientId}/fiado-statement` per the live contract (`DateFrom` nullable in the response for full history; PageSize 1–100; a Member needs an active grant on an active Tab, checked before validation; deactivated clients stay readable like the balance report). **5.6/5.7:** migration `M7_7Phase5FiadoLinkedMovements` adds `Transactions.GenerationKind` + `GeneratedFromTransactionId` (paired check, filtered unique index, restrict FK) and `TransactionTypes.SystemRole` + `FiadoPaymentKind`, and seeds two system categories/types per existing branch without guessing any origin or method (`CreateBranchSeedFactory` alike); `RequestCreateTransactionJson.HandsOutCountedStock` declares a counted-stock fiado sale; `FiadoPhysicalPairService` builds the generated leg on the paired terminal inside `IDailyCloseAccountCoordination.AcquireMany` (ascending key order), re-reads the pairing, and enforces the terminal's open Draft close and the Member's terminal scope; source finalize/cancel carries the leg; generated rows refuse update/finalize/cancel/edit-preview (`TRANSACTION_GENERATED_ROW_READ_ONLY`); origin is exposed on list/detail/create and `GeneratedTransactionId` on source responses; system types/categories are protected; `ResponseOperatorAccountJson.PairedTabAccountId` gives the workstation routing target in the operator's own context (gap L). Account-coordination participants: thirteen. **5.8:** `Generated*` subset fields overall and per category. **5.4:** `FiadoReconciliationCoherenceTest` drives the shipped commands, variance preview and Submit through the production calculator (stock 995 and cash 1,005 reconcile at 0; the same counts without legs give −5/+5; a non-cash payment moves only the Tab) and keeps statement/balance/aging coherent through partial, full and over-payment across Tabs. Evidence: `ReportControllerFiadoNettingTest` (8), `ClientControllerFiadoStatementTest` (11), `TransactionControllerFiadoLinkedMovementTest` (18, incl. replay after freeze, concurrent same-key, trigger rollback, forged origin, Member grants, self-context), `ReportControllerOperatorSummaryAutomaticShareTest`, `FiadoReconciliationCoherenceTest` (5), `FiadoLinkedMovementsMigrationTest` (upgrade path), plus use-case/validator tests. Gates on the final code: build 0 errors; Validator **327/327**; UseCase **1127/1127**; WebApi + architecture **1077/1077** in **3m15s**; EF reports no pending model changes; contract-surface snapshot diff purely additive (reviewed). **Not covered:** a forced race between a physical pair and a concurrent Submit of the paired terminal's close (it relies on the shared account key like every Terminal write); locked-date pairs rely on the shared preamble lock check. **Open product facts, not decided here:** which fiado sales move counted assets (§9 question 1) and which types each branch configures as repayment methods. **5.9:** spec `v50` rewrites §3.7/§3.10/§3.12/§6.1/§6.4/§6.5/§6.6/§6.11/§6.12/§6.14/§7.3 and discharges every remaining v43 marker; the presentation and ERD are updated semantically; catalog gaps H and L are marked resolved and removed from the gap → screen map, and the affected screen entries are re-tagged. Design sync markers stayed at `v49` until review accepted that reconciliation; they moved to `v50` with the acceptance note above.

> **Phase 5 progress note (2026-10-16) — item 5.3 delivered; 5.1, 5.2 and 5.4–5.9 remain open.** Baseline `ec258dd`, spec `v46`. Member Tab access already resolved only through explicit `OperatorAccount` rows (`MemberAccountScopeResolver`; `AssignAccountUseCase` accepts a Tab like any account), so the 1.8 grant rule needed tests, not code: WebApi.Test now pins that a junior holding only the paired Terminal gets 403 `TRANSACTION_MEMBER_ACCOUNT_OUT_OF_SCOPE` on fiado, an inactive Tab grant is no grant, and a senior with Tab grants on two terminals records on both. Gap S is fixed: `DeactivateAccountUseCase` clears a Terminal's `TabAccountId`, and for a Tab clears every Terminal holding it (`IAccountsRepository.ListTerminalsByTabAccountId`, tracked, any `Active`), in the same commit as the deactivation. To keep that true under concurrency, pair, unpair, create-with-pairing and deactivate take one branch-scoped transaction advisory lock before reading (`IAccountPairingCoordination`, key namespace `account-pairing:`, timeout `AccountPairingCoordination:LockTimeoutSeconds`, default 5 s; timeout → 409 `ACCOUNT_PAIRING_COORDINATION_BUSY`, now declared on `DELETE /account/terminal/{id}/tab` and `DELETE /account/{id}`; the contract-surface snapshot changed by exactly those two 409 lines). No pairing write holds another coordination key, so no lock-order cycle exists. Data-only migration `M7_7Phase5StalePairingCleanup` clears pairings where either side was already inactive (model snapshot unchanged); an upgrade-path test seeds every stale shape beside a valid pair on the pre-cleanup schema and proves only the stale ones clear and the freed Tab pairs again. WebApi race tests hold the pairing key and release queued requests in both grant orders (deactivate-first → the queued pair returns 404; pair-first → the queued deactivation unpairs), plus a 100 ms busy case for pair and deactivate. Gates: build 0 errors; Validator **312/312**; UseCase **1094/1094** (busy paths for all five use cases, both unpair directions, Bank untouched); WebApi + architecture **994/994** in **2m52s**; both derived-HTML scripts pass `node --check`. Spec `v46` rewrites §3.7 and discharges its pending marker; the presentation's `TabAccountId` invariants gain the deactivation clause. Design catalog: gap S resolved and removed from the gap → screen map; the Accounts entry renders the rule. **Found and left open:** gap L's fiado routing needs the workstation pairing in the operator's own context, and `GET /operator/self-context` carries none (`ResponseOperatorAccountJson` has no pairing field) — to be decided with the 5.6 contract rather than invented here. After independent review accepted the catalog reconciliation, both design sync markers moved to `v46`: `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` is green at `v46` and `bash docs/test-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes 34/34.

### Phase 6 — Contract conveniences + frontend-support contracts *(gaps M + B + E + the design-0.4.2 dependencies; 6.5/6.6 ⛔ 1.7)*

- [x] **6.1** `OriginTransactionId` filter on `GET /transaction` (`RequestListTransactionsJson`), preserving §6.10 member account scope unchanged. Each `ResponseListTransactionItemJson` also carries its nullable `OriginTransactionId` and positive `xmin` `Version`, so the list identifies plan membership and supplies the `If-Match` value for direct guarded sibling actions without detail GETs. WebApi.Test: sibling enumeration works for a Member on a linked account, every returned sibling can be cancelled directly with its listed version, and out-of-scope keeps the empty short-circuit.
- [x] **6.2** `DailyCloseId` on `ResponseCashVarianceSummaryItemJson`, joined by `(Date, AccountId)` like the dashboard rule. WebApi.Test pins the join against sibling accounts on the same day.
- [x] **6.3** Stale operator→user link (gap E): enrich `ResponseOperatorJson` with the linked user's display fields resolved regardless of membership state, **or** clear `Operator.UserId` on membership removal — pick at implementation, record why. WebApi.Test: the removed-membership case resolves (or clears) deterministically.
- [x] **6.4** **Server-authoritative branch date/time:** expose branch-local "today"/"now" (`IBranchClock`) on the session/context reads (`GET /branch/current` and/or `GET /operator/self-context`) so no client derives the business date from the device clock. `BranchLocalDate` is a `DateOnly` emitted as `YYYY-MM-DD` / OpenAPI `date`; `BranchLocalDateTime` is an offset-free `DateTimeKind.Unspecified` branch-wall-clock string with no `Z` or numeric offset. Phase 3 item 3.3 is the enforcement baseline for Open-day dates; this item exposes the same authoritative business date to clients. WebApi.Test pins the exact raw strings and `Kind`; OpenAPI regression pins both formats.
- [x] **6.5** **Idempotent financial creates** per 1.7: `POST /transaction` and `POST /transaction/installment` (and the Phase 5 payment command, if shipped) honor the chosen idempotency mechanism — a retried request cannot double-record money. **Transport shape (pin before design 0.4.2 / this phase codes):** a client `Idempotency-Key` **header**, scoped per-endpoint + authenticated branch/user; a canonical body hash backs the different-payload conflict check; the persisted replay stores the resource id + response envelope; retention window recorded in the spec. This client-facing shape is exactly what **design 0.4.2 consumes**. WebApi.Test: sequential same-key replay → one persisted row/plan, deterministic response; **concurrent** same-key requests → exactly one write (key reservation and write persist atomically — a crash between them must not strand the key); same key with a **different payload** → conflict (verbatim key), not a silent replay.
- [x] **6.6** **Project-wide optimistic-concurrency / stale-state wire contract** per 1.7 on mutating endpoints (transaction update/finalize/cancel, close item edits, settings, lock). Phase 2/3 already use the DailyClose `xmin`/Version as an integrity backstop; this item standardizes the exact `If-Match`/ETag-style representation consumed by design 0.4.2 and completes the remaining endpoint families. Stale writes reject **409** with the declared verbatim key. **Aggregate commands guard on the aggregate root's `xmin`:** Transaction for finalize, `DailyClose` for `PUT /items`, Setting for `lock-month`. WebApi.Test for one representative endpoint per family.
- [x] **6.7** **`IsInProgress` filter on `GET /timeentry` (gap T):** the time-entry console's "forgotten punches first" job needs a server-side filter (and/or an `IsInProgress DESC` ordering option) — today the flag exists only on the item DTO, so triage is page-local. WebApi.Test: filter returns only open-segment entries across pages.
- [x] **6.8** Spec sync + design gaps B/E/M/T and the 0.4.2 pattern items flagged for catalog re-sync.

> **Phase 6 completion note (2026-08-20; findings audits amended through 2026-08-23).** Items 6.1–6.8 ship together as spec `v39` with migration `20260820084337_M7_7Phase6Contracts`. Transaction sibling filtering preserves the Member empty/account-scope short-circuits, and every list row exposes its nullable plan-group id plus `xmin` `Version` so listed siblings can be guarded-cancelled without detail GETs; cash-variance rows expose the exact close id without a date-only join; membership removal atomically clears matching Operator login links because the Operator remains the durable employee/history identity; and `GET /branch/current` returns one-capture `BranchLocalDate`/`BranchLocalDateTime` with a `DateOnly` `YYYY-MM-DD` value and an offset-free `DateTimeKind.Unspecified` branch-wall-clock value. Transaction and installment creates require a visible-ASCII `Idempotency-Key` scoped by endpoint + authenticated branch/user, canonical typed-JSON SHA-256, a persisted resource id/response envelope, and a 24-hour replay window; a transaction-scoped advisory reservation and business write share the same database transaction, with sequential/concurrent replay and different-payload conflicts pinned for both create families. The findings audits additionally prove that a frozen-ledger failure after key preparation rolls the reservation back so the same request can succeed after Reopen, that an expired key can be reused for a new payload with one refreshed row and envelope, and that a committed replay resolves under a short idempotency-only transaction before DailyClose account coordination; a forced held-account-key regression returns the durable 201 envelope instead of the ledger-busy 409. Both create families source retention timestamps from `IBranchClock`, use the fast committed-replay path, and retain the original account-coordinated atomic reservation/write path on a miss. A once-daily bounded hosted cleanup now consumes the existing `ExpiresAt` index and physically deletes expired replay rows; it takes each candidate's idempotency advisory key and rechecks expiry transactionally, with PostgreSQL regressions proving batch draining, live-envelope preservation, and a concurrent refresh winning over deletion. The deployment defaults, tuning variables, monitoring query, logs, and no-manual-delete rule are recorded in the database environment runbook. Transaction update/finalize/cancel, DailyClose items, setting update, and lock-month use the exact strong quoted positive-decimal `If-Match: "xmin"` contract, return matching `ETag`/`Version`, and normalize stale roots to their signed 409 keys; aggregate commands guard Transaction/DailyClose/Setting roots. A stale finalize after another manager updates the Draft returns `TRANSACTION_STALE_WRITE` and preserves the newer values. Time-entry `IsInProgress` filtering and `InProgressFirst` ordering execute before count/paging. OpenAPI and real PostgreSQL regressions cover every family, including exact branch-clock raw JSON/OpenAPI formats, migrations, restricted idempotency FKs, `xmin` races, stale finalize, actionable sibling enumeration, concurrent creates, sibling accounts, removed membership, and across-page triage. The closing contract audit also declares the Open-201 and ordinary-setting ETags, maps the idempotency unique backstop and lock timeout to `IDEMPOTENCY_COORDINATION_BUSY`, removes the obsolete DailyClose body-version resource, requires variance deep-link ids at every projection call site, validates each idempotency key once per command path, and exposes a checked plan-level installment `Version` whose rows must share one create transaction. The replay-first idempotency scope now sets its own independently configurable five-second transaction-local `lock_timeout`; a real held-key HTTP regression proves the signed 409, zero writes while blocked, and successful retry after release instead of relying on an artificially configured repository context. `xmin` is explicitly documented as a session-lived concurrency token rather than a durable revision id. Design gaps B/E/M/T are moved to the resolved Phase 6 register, and design 0.4.2 records its delivered server dependencies without being marked complete. Final gates after the findings audits: build 0 errors; Validator **309/309**; UseCase **1065/1065**; WebApi + architecture **942/942** in **2m48s**; EF reports no pending model changes; doc sync and both derived HTML syntax checks are green at `v39`; exactly four `Pending M7.7 change` markers remain for later pairing, fiado-settlement/reference, and time-policy phases.

### Phase 7 — Time-entry policy integrity *(gap O ⛔ 1.6)*

- [x] **7.1** Implement **the model chosen in 1.6** — the two branches differ in shape, so only one of these lands:
  - **(a) Effective-dated:** new `TimeEntryPolicy` entity; EF migration backfills one row per existing branch from its current constants (seed factory creates it for new branches); `ITimeEntryCalculationService` callers resolve the policy row applicable to each entry's date. `Setting` keeps `LockDate` and is not versioned.
  - **(b) Per-entry snapshot:** policy fields added to `TimeEntry`; EF migration backfills existing entries from the branch's current constants; new/edited entries copy the current defaults at write time; reports read each entry's snapshot — no date resolution.
- [x] **7.2** Branch-wide balance roll-up includes **zero-entry active operators** (empty `Days`, zeroed totals) so payroll sees the employee who never clocked in; ordering rule unchanged. Labelling missing days *Sem registro* stays a client-side derivation — no server operating-day matrix in this milestone (the reduced promise is recorded in design gap O).
- [x] **7.3** Tests — WebApi.Test: changing `DailyTargetHours`, `LunchDeductionOver6H`, **and** `LunchDeductionOver4H` today leaves yesterday's reported balance unchanged (all three constants, not just the target); the migration backfill preserves pre-change balances; the roll-up contains a linked operator with no entries **and an active operator with no login link at all** (`UserId = null` — payroll covers employees, not logins). UseCases.Test: policy-resolution logic per entry date.
- [x] **7.4** Spec sync: §3.18/§6.7 updated to the dating model.

> **Phase 7 completion note (2026-08-23; findings audit amended 2026-08-24 in the same first delivery).** Items 7.1–7.4 ship together as spec `v40` with migration `20260823204707_M7_7Phase7TimeEntryPolicy`. The findings audit closed two review defects inside the unreleased delivery: (1) `TimeEntryReadSharedMapper` now recomputes **every** Get/List row under its entry-date policy — closed rows included, matching the report — instead of passing the persisted checkpoint through for all-closed rows, so a closed current-day entry read right after a same-day policy change can no longer disagree with payroll (persisted totals stay a write-time checkpoint reads never rewrite; pinned by stale-checkpoint unit tests on Get/List, a divergent-checkpoint HTTP Get regression, and a Get-vs-report parity assertion inside the historical-stability test); and (2) the mandatory `LunchDeductionOver4H` historical regression is genuinely exercised — the stability test adds a five-gross-hour historical day proving 4.75 h / −2.58 survives the tier change, and the stepwise backfill test adds a second legacy five-hour entry recomputed to its exact checkpoint alongside the nine-hour one, so both lunch tiers participate in upgrade balance preservation. A second amendment pass closed two lower-severity gaps: the migration now runs a fail-loud PL/pgSQL preflight that aborts the whole `Up()` transaction with a named exception when any existing branch lacks a Setting row — the `Branch → Setting` relationship is not database-enforced (`Branch.Setting` is nullable) and the backfill's own `INSERT ... SELECT` would otherwise silently skip such a branch, leaving it with zero policy rows and a first-calculation failure at `TimeEntryPolicyResolver`; a dedicated stepwise test seeds an orphan branch with no Setting alongside a normally paired one and proves `MigrateAsync()` throws with the Phase 7 migration left pending. Post-deployment branch creation's initial policy row now has a real WebApi persistence assertion (`CreateBranchSeedPersistenceTest`) reloading both the Setting and the MinValue `TimeEntryPolicy` row from the database after `POST /branch/create`, closing the gap where only a mocked `Received(Add)` UseCases.Test assertion covered it. Branch (a) landed exactly as signed: the new §3.20 `TimeEntryPolicy` entity carries `EffectiveFrom` (date, inclusive) plus the three constants under a filtered active-unique `(BranchId, EffectiveFrom)` index and restricted Branch FK; `Setting` keeps `LockDate`, is not versioned, and its three constants remain only the current-policy mirror for the settings read surface and the Phase 6 `If-Match`/ETag root — no calculation reads them. All five calculation call paths (upsert persist, segment-mutation recalculation, Get, List, and the balance report) resolve the applicable row per entry date through the single `TimeEntryPolicyResolver` (greatest `EffectiveFrom` on or before the entry's date, deterministic tie-breakers); List and the report resolve per row because one page or window can span a boundary. `PUT /setting` stays the single write path and lands mirror and ledger in one commit: a real change upserts the policy row effective the branch-local day of the change (second same-day change mutates that row in place; value-identical PUTs append nothing), preserving the tier-regression contract that a change affects today's live-running rows immediately. The migration deterministically backfills one `0001-01-01`-dated row per existing branch from that branch's own Settings constants; `CreateBranchSeedFactory.CreateDefaultTimeEntryPolicy` seeds the same row for new branches, so resolution is total even for backdated entries. A dedicated stepwise-migration PostgreSQL test builds the exact pre-Phase-7 schema (target `M7_7Phase6Contracts`), seeds two legacy branches with different constants plus a worked entry, applies the Phase 7 migration, and proves per-branch backfill values and that the persisted balance recomputes unchanged under the resolved policy; a schema test pins the columns, filtered unique index, and restricted FK on the clean `Database.MigrateAsync()` database. HTTP regressions prove that changing all three constants today leaves both a prior Present day and a prior DayOff day byte-for-byte stable while today (the inclusive boundary date) already computes under the new values, and pin the policy ledger rows behind the PUT. The branch roll-up now unions every active operator with the window's entry-holding operators: zero-entry rows return empty `Days` and zeroed totals/counters for both a linked and a `UserId = null` operator, deactivated operators with in-window entries are retained, and the `OperatorName ASC, OperatorId` ordering is unchanged; *Sem registro* remains a client-side window-minus-entries derivation with no server operating-day matrix or fabricated absence rows. UseCases.Test covers resolver boundary dates (day-before/on/after `EffectiveFrom`, between-changes, unsorted input, invariant-breach miss), report-level per-date resolution across a boundary, the settings-update policy upsert/no-op matrix, the seeded initial policy on branch create, and the new roll-up membership. Spec sync `v40` updates §3.18 (mirror note), adds §3.20, rewrites §6.7 constants provenance, updates the §6.14 roll-up contract, syncs the ERD (new entity + relation) and presentation (HR group + calculation card), and discharges the §6.7 `Pending M7.7 change` marker — exactly three markers remain, all owned by Phase 5 (pairing, fiado settlement, gap-A reference). Design gap O moves to the resolved Phase 7 register; the Settings, My time-entry balance, and Time-entry management catalog entries now state the dating model. Final gates after the second amendment pass: build 0 errors; Validator **309/309**; UseCase **1080/1080**; WebApi + architecture **951/951**; EF reports no pending model changes; doc sync and both derived-HTML script syntax checks green at `v40`.

### Phase 7.5 — Work Queue backlog aggregate *(design gap W; resolves the data half of design gap P)*

> **Why a server contract:** the Work Queue must show exact pending-close counts per status on earlier unlocked days, the oldest such day, and the Draft-transaction count across every unlocked day. Composing them from `GET /dailyclose` / `GET /transaction` pages needs several calls, is not a single snapshot, and would rely on list ordering that is not part of the contract. The rule of what counts as pending belongs on the server, shared by web and mobile.

- [x] **7.5.1** Extend `GET /report/dashboard?date=`: `ResponseDashboardJson` gains `Backlog` (`ResponseDashboardBacklogJson { SubmittedCount, RejectedCount, DraftCount, OldestPendingDate? }`) over active closes with `LockDate < Date < date`, statuses Submitted/Rejected/Draft only (Approved excluded), `OldestPendingDate` = the earliest such `Date` or `null`; and `UnlockedDraftTransactionCount` over active-row Draft transactions with `LockDate < Date ≤` branch-local today. Each is one repository-level aggregation (`COUNT … GROUP BY Status`, `MIN(Date)`); Manager/Admin only, as today; an additive, non-breaking change to the M7.6 locked contract.
- [x] **7.5.2** WebApi.Test: per-status counts and `OldestPendingDate`, Approved/cancelled/locked-day/requested-day-and-later exclusions, the `null` oldest date, cross-branch isolation, and OpenAPI required metadata for the new fields (M7.6 fidelity rules; refresh the contract-lock snapshot).
- [x] **7.5.3** Spec sync: §6.14 "Manager dashboard aggregation" documents both fields; revision bump; design gap W flagged for catalog re-sync.

> **Phase 7.5 completion note (2026-10-13).** Items 7.5.1–7.5.3 ship together as spec `v42` with no migration and no schema change. `GET /report/dashboard` additively returns `Backlog` (`ResponseDashboardBacklogJson { SubmittedCount, RejectedCount, DraftCount, OldestPendingDate? }`) and `UnlockedDraftTransactionCount`. The backlog is one grouped aggregate (`IDailyClosesRepository.GetDashboardBacklogByBranchIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking`: `COUNT`/`MIN(Date)` grouped by status over active Submitted/Rejected/Draft closes with `LockDate < Date <` the requested date); the Draft count is one `COUNT` (`ITransactionsRepository.CountDraftByBranchIdAndDateRangeAsNoTracking`: non-soft-deleted Draft rows with `LockDate < Date ≤` branch-local today). The upper bounds differ on purpose — requested date versus today — and both reuse `ILockDateReader` (missing Setting = never locked) and `IBranchClock`, thread the request `CancellationToken`, and stay inside the caller's branch. Manager/Admin-only access, validation, the declared status set, and the selected-day facts are unchanged; no operating-day calendar was introduced. Real PostgreSQL regressions (`ReportControllerDashboardBacklogHappyPathTest`) pin per-status counts and the oldest date, the Approved, soft-deleted, locked-boundary (`Date = LockDate` and earlier), requested-day, and later-day exclusions, the moving upper bound, the `null` oldest date with an explicit JSON `null`, the never-locked branch, the transaction status/date/soft-delete predicates with the today bound holding across past, locked, and future requested dates, and cross-branch isolation of rows and `LockDate`. OpenAPI: both new dashboard members and the three counts are `required`, `OldestPendingDate` is optional and nullable `date-time`, `backlog` is a plain non-null component reference (`DashboardOpenApiContractTest` + `RequiredMetadataOpenApiContractTest`), and the contract-surface snapshot was replaced after reviewing a purely additive diff (one new component, two new members). UseCases.Test pins exact branch/lock/date/token arguments for both bounds and that a Member or invalid date reaches no aggregate. Spec sync `v42` updates §6.14, the presentation's Work Queue card, and the ERD revision metadata; design gap W moves to a resolved Phase 7.5 register, leaves the gap → screen map, and the Manager Work Queue entry now cites the DTOs. Gates: build 0 errors; Validator **312/312**; UseCase **1086/1086**; WebApi + architecture **982/982** in **2m54s**; doc sync and both derived-HTML script syntax checks green at `v42`; three `Pending M7.7 change` markers remain, all owned by Phase 5. **This phase does not close M7.7:** Phases 5 and 8 remain open.

### Phase 7.6 — Close review history, atomic plan finalization, and list type filter *(design gaps X + Y + Z, owner decisions 2026-09-29 → 2026-10-07 from the prototype review)*

> **Why server contracts:** the review screens promise dated, separately named rejection and opening-change events with before/after values and actor names, which latest-only fields cannot reconstruct; a Draft cheque plan must activate all-or-nothing, which per-row finalize calls from a browser cannot guarantee; and finding a transaction by type needs a server filter applied before paging.

- [x] **7.6.1** **Close review events (gap X).** Persist immutable events: a rejection event (time, actor, reason) at Reject, retained through later demotions/correction saves; an opening-change event at the coordinated `PUT /items` that changes a later close's opening — source close/version, affected successor, per-product before/after values (a missing item distinct from zero), time, and actor — including a first count that becomes the new opening source. Repeated corrections are separate events, retained even when the successor is already Draft. Return them through review or a dedicated history read (names undecided) under the close's own read permission, with each actor's **display name** for every authorized reader, Members included, shaped so the name can be withheld by role later. No backfill: legacy closes return no history. The one-successor invalidation rule and the no-silent-recompute rule are unchanged; full audit history is out of scope.
- [x] **7.6.2** **Atomic plan finalization (gap Y).** One command finalizes a wholly Draft plan: resolve the authoritative sibling set by `OriginTransactionId` inside the branch, verify membership, statuses, versions, permissions, dates, and ledger/close guards under the coordination of every account/day involved, and commit all status/audit changes once — any failure leaves every row unchanged. The per-row finalize refuses every Draft installment of a plan (`TRANSACTION_PLAN_FINALIZE_REQUIRED`). **Contract decided in-phase:** no plan entity (the plan id is `OriginTransactionId`); `GET /transaction/installment/{originTransactionId}` with a computed plan status and `POST /transaction/installment/{originTransactionId}/finalize`; the sibling-set token is the exact set of Draft installment ids and versions in the body; retry follows the guarded-write rule. **Owner decisions (signed 2026-10-16):** a Mixed plan (Draft installments alongside Active and/or Cancelled) finalizes its remaining Draft installments only with explicit confirmation — never silently; Draft edits stay per-row saves (no atomic plan save); discarding stays per-row cancel (no plan discard). Existing-plan editing remains the per-row §6.11 correction.
- [x] **7.6.3** **`TransactionTypeId` list filter (gap Z).** Optional filter on `RequestListTransactionsJson`, applied with the other predicates before pagination and counts; branch and Member account scope unchanged; system-generated types filterable.
- [x] **7.6.4** Tests — WebApi.Test: event retention across demotion and correction saves, two successive corrections as two events, a Member reading actor names without `GET /branch/users`, legacy close with no history; a three-row R$ 100 Draft plan contributing R$ 300 at once and a stale row blocking all three (no partial contribution), per-row finalize refused on a Draft plan, a standalone Draft still finalizable; the type filter combined with Draft/date filters and Member scope, with exact `TotalCount`. OpenAPI snapshot refreshed.
- [x] **7.6.5** Spec sync: discharge the §6.3/§6.10/§6.13 v43 forward markers; revision bump; design gaps X/Y/Z flagged for catalog re-sync.

> **Phase 7.6 progress note (2026-10-16) — item 7.6.3 delivered; 7.6.4 and 7.6.5 complete, closing Phase 7.6.** Baseline `af7400f`, spec `v49`. **Contract:** `RequestListTransactionsJson` gains optional `TransactionTypeId` (`GET /transaction?TransactionTypeId=`), carried into `TransactionListFilter` and applied in `TransactionsRepository.ApplyFilter` — the one predicate builder both the page query and `TotalCount` use — after the unchanged branch and Member `AllowedAccountIds` predicates, so paging metadata always describes the type-filtered set. Any type id is accepted without an existence check or new error key: a retired type still filters its historical rows, and an unknown or another branch's id returns an empty page. No use-case, validator, schema or response change; the no-operator, zero-account and explicit out-of-scope short-circuits are untouched. System-generated types do not exist yet (gap H, Phase 5); because the predicate matches any id, they will be filterable as soon as they are written, and the retired-type case pins the same "cannot be created, still filterable" path today. Evidence (real PostgreSQL): type + Draft + date range over five matches with Active, other-type and out-of-range decoys pages 2/2/1/0 with `TotalCount` 5 and `TotalPages` 3 on every page and the exact descending order, while type alone counts 7; an unused type and a random id return empty pages; a retired type returns its one row; each branch's type id returns nothing in the other branch; a Member filtering by type sees only the linked account's two rows across two one-row pages, and an explicit unlinked `AccountId` with the type returns empty. A mutation check removing the repository predicate fails all five tests. UseCase tests pin `TransactionTypeId` reaching both list and count with the other filters, and with the Member's `AllowedAccountIds`. With 7.6.1's event tests and 7.6.2's plan tests already delivered, 7.6.4 is complete. Gates: build 0 errors; Validator **319/319**; UseCase **1112/1112**; WebApi + architecture **1033/1033** in **3m**; contract-surface snapshot diff is the one added line `query TransactionTypeId optional string` on `GET /transaction`. Spec `v49` discharges §6.10's marker (the last Phase 7.6 marker; the remaining markers belong to Phase 5); presentation's list-filter sentence updated; ERD revision only. Design catalog: gap Z resolved and removed from the gap → screen map; the Transactions list filter cites `TransactionTypeId` with `GET /transaction-type` (active types) as the option source; the cross-screen paging rule preserves the type selection. After independent review accepted that reconciliation, both design sync markers moved to `v49`, completing 7.6.5 (the §6.3/§6.10/§6.13 v43 markers are all discharged and gaps X/Y/Z re-synced): `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` is green at `v49` and `bash docs/test-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes 34/34.

> **Phase 7.6 progress note (2026-10-16) — item 7.6.2 delivered; 7.6.3 open, so the shared 7.6.4 tests and 7.6.5 sync stay open.** Baseline `cdfd5e3`, spec `v48`. **Owner decisions (2026-10-16):** a Mixed plan (Draft installments alongside Active and/or Cancelled) finalizes its remaining Draft installments only with explicit confirmation — never silently; Draft edits stay per-row saves (no atomic plan save); discarding stays per-row cancel (no plan discard command). **Contract design (reviewed with this delivery):** no plan entity and no migration — the plan id is `OriginTransactionId`; `GET /transaction/installment/{originTransactionId}` → `ResponseInstallmentPlanJson` with computed `InstallmentPlanStatus` (Draft, Active, Mixed, Cancelled), counts, non-cancelled total and every installment with its version; `POST /transaction/installment/{originTransactionId}/finalize` with `RequestFinalizeInstallmentPlanJson { Installments: [{ TransactionId, Version }], ConfirmMixedPlan }` — the sibling-set token is the exact set of Draft installment ids and versions in the body (one `If-Match` names only one row), retry follows the guarded-write rule with no `Idempotency-Key`. Under the plan account's coordination (all installments share account and business date; a mismatch is an integrity error) the command requires a Draft installment, confirmation when Mixed, an exact set/version match, Member scope, the per-row mutation permission, the lock date and the ledger freeze, then activates every Draft installment in one commit. `FinalizeTransactionUseCase` refuses any plan installment (`TRANSACTION_PLAN_FINALIZE_REQUIRED`); standalone Drafts keep per-row finalize; the architecture coordination-participant set grows to twelve. Evidence (real PostgreSQL): a Draft plan created through `POST /transaction/installment` contributes R$ 0 until one finalize makes R$ 300 Active and appears in open-cheque aging; a Mixed plan refuses without confirmation and activates exactly its two remaining Drafts with it; a Member finalizes their own same-day plan; per-row finalize is refused for Draft and Mixed plan rows; stale version, omitted and extra installments, no-Draft plan, locked date, frozen Terminal ledger, out-of-scope Member and non-recording Member all leave every installment Draft; malformed sets return 400; unknown, standalone and foreign plan ids return 404; two concurrent finalizes activate the plan once; a cancel granted first makes the queued finalize refuse the changed plan, and a finalize granted first makes a queued cancel of the old version stale; a trigger that fails the last installment's update rolls back the whole finalize and the retry succeeds. **Review fixes (same delivery):** a null entry in `Installments` is a 400 `TRANSACTION_PLAN_INSTALLMENT_ID_INVALID` instead of a 500; a per-row edit committed after the checks and before the save now returns `TRANSACTION_PLAN_STALE_WRITE` (new `IUnitOfWork.TryCommitOptimistic`, documented in `server/AGENTS.md`) with nothing persisted, so the error no longer depends on race timing — both pinned by HTTP regressions. Gates: build 0 errors; Validator **319/319**; UseCase **1110/1110**; WebApi + architecture **1028/1028** in **3m01s**; contract-surface snapshot diff purely additive. Spec `v48` discharges §6.3's plan marker and updates §6.6/§6.11/§4; presentation updated; ERD revision only. Design catalog: gap Y resolved and removed from the gap → screen map; Installment plan, Transaction — Edit, Transactions list navigation and the cross-screen pattern use the plan status and the decisions. After independent review accepted that reconciliation, both design sync markers moved to `v48`: `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` is green at `v48` and `bash docs/test-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes 34/34.

> **Phase 7.6 progress note (2026-10-16) — item 7.6.1 delivered; 7.6.2 and 7.6.3 open, so the shared 7.6.4 tests and 7.6.5 sync stay open.** Baseline `6c9be73`, spec `v47`. **Naming decided:** a dedicated read `GET /dailyclose/{dailyCloseId}/history` → `ResponseDailyCloseHistoryJson { DailyCloseId, IsHistoryComplete, CurrentOpeningSource?, Events }`, under the same read permission as `GET /dailyclose/{id}` (Members included), declaring no `ETag` (`FinancialHeadersOpenApiOperationTransformer` now excludes it like variance-preview) and enrolled in the architecture never-commits namespaces. **Persistence:** migration `M7_7Phase76CloseReviewEvents` adds `DailyCloseReviewEvents` + `DailyCloseReviewEventItems` (restricted FKs; actor user/operator names and product names snapshotted at event time; null item values = no saved item) and `DailyCloses.ReviewHistoryComplete` (false for every pre-existing close, true for new ones; no backfilled events). **Writes:** `RejectDailyCloseUseCase` records a `Rejected` event (time, reviewer, linked operator if any, reason); `PutDailyCloseItemsUseCase` records an `OpeningChanged` event on the one next eligible successor whenever counted input changes — real value change or first count — using the opening as it stood inside the transaction (the close's own items before the update, or the previous counted source's items for a first count). `DailyCloseSuccessorInvalidator` now reports an already-Draft successor without demoting it (`OpeningRecheckRequiredAt` null), so its event is still recorded while `AffectedSuccessor` stays demotion-only; the one-successor rule, ownership, locks and coordination are unchanged and event + command share one transaction. Evidence (all real-PostgreSQL WebApi): two corrections 1000→1100→1080 yield two separate events, the second on an already-Draft successor; a rejection keeps its own time/actor/reason after an opening recheck demotes the close; a first count names the replaced source and keeps a recorded zero distinct from a missing item; only the next eligible successor (not an uncounted close between, a later close, or another account) gets an event; an unchanged save records nothing; a scoped Member reads actor names; legacy closes return `IsHistoryComplete = false` with no events; a test-scoped trigger that rejects event inserts makes the correction and the rejection roll back completely, and the retry then records exactly one event; 401/403/404 match `GET /dailyclose/{id}`; an upgrade-path test marks pre-existing closes incomplete without events. Gates: build 0 errors; Validator **312/312**; UseCase **1102/1102** (recorder diff/naming, exact recorder arguments and recorder-before-commit ordering in PutItems and Reject, invalidator Draft reporting); WebApi + architecture **1007/1007** in **3m02s**; contract-surface snapshot diff purely additive (one enum, four schemas, one operation). Spec `v47` discharges §6.13's review-event marker and adds §3.21; ERD and presentation updated; §8's stale table count corrected to 22. Design catalog: gap X resolved and removed from the gap → screen map; Fix & resubmit, Close day and the cockpit now cite the history DTOs with fallbacks keyed to `IsHistoryComplete`. After independent review accepted that reconciliation, both design sync markers moved to `v47`: `bash docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` is green at `v47` and `bash docs/test-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes 34/34.

- [x] **7.8.1** **H1 / SV03 rename.** `GET /report/dashboard?date=` → `GET /report/manager-queue?date=`; `ResponseDashboardJson` / `ResponseDashboardCloseJson` / `ResponseDashboardNotSubmittedJson` / `ResponseDashboardBacklogJson` → `ResponseManagerQueueJson` / `ResponseManagerQueueCloseJson` / `ResponseManagerQueueNotSubmittedJson` / `ResponseManagerQueueBacklogJson` (M11 listed three; the fourth, the Phase 7.5 backlog DTO, follows the same rule so no `Dashboard` schema reaches generated code). Only an export invocation overrides it: `-p:OpenApiGenerateDocuments=true -p:OpenApiDocumentsDirectory=<temp dir>`, with inert, non-secret placeholder configuration in that child process only. Evidence: a no-config, non-Development `dotnet build server/server.sln --no-incremental` succeeds and writes no document; the opt-in export writes one document; no startup or package change beyond this.

- [x] **7.8.5** Validator, UseCase, and WebApi + architecture suites green.

Phase 5 and its 5.9 sync were accepted and closed after this note, so formal close-out is no longer blocked by Phase 5; the 8.4 audits (counted-source symmetry, Draft CashVariance suppression, coordination timeout/cancellation/key parity, grant-order races, Recall documentation) were not re-run beyond the green suites.

### Done criteria

- An operator sees opening values and a live variance **before** submitting — on a fresh close, from candidate values while typing — and can record a note explaining the variance (or the `Notes` column is gone).
- No sequence of permitted ledger **or close-history** calls can leave a surfaced close variance silently wrong on any official or ordinary Draft response: every `Submitted`/`Approved` value was submitted/approved against the counted opening shown, a real predecessor correction explicitly demotes its one dependent eligible successor, and retained Draft snapshots never masquerade as current. The ledger matrix, chain cascade, counted-eligibility predicates, and real concurrent grant orders are WebApi-pinned. A Member can no longer open a close for a day they cannot edit, and Terminal activity cannot bypass an open/countable close chain.
- Deactivating an account can no longer strand a Terminal↔Tab pairing (atomic unpair or block, both directions tested), and forgotten-punch triage has real server-side filtering.
- A month can only be locked through the atomic command — whole-interval validated, race-safe, well-founded from the seeded `MinValue` state — and `PUT /setting` can no longer move `LockDate`.
- Fiado settlement semantics are coherent between balance and aging, implemented **in this milestone**; a client's statement reconciles row by row with its balance across every Tab; fiado operations that move counted cash or stock write their linked, protected Terminal movement so a correct count shows zero difference; and the close-day fiado reference (gap A) is removed for every role.
- Rejection and opening-change events survive later saves with their own times, actors, and before/after values (gap X); a wholly Draft cheque plan activates all-or-nothing (gap Y); the transaction list filters by type (gap Z).
- Manager Work Queue reads exact earlier-day backlog counts, the oldest pending day, and the unlocked Draft-transaction count from one dashboard response (gap W).
- The Manager Work Queue contract carries its final name (`GET /report/manager-queue`, `ResponseManagerQueue*Json`) before the first generated TypeScript client, and `server.API` exports its OpenAPI document only on an explicit opt-in build, never in an ordinary build or test run (Phase 7.8).
- The rejected-close correction journey works in the §7.2 next-morning rhythm.
- Financial creates are idempotent, mutations have a declared concurrency contract, and clients read the business date from the server — the design-0.4.2 patterns have their server half.
- Sibling enumeration, the cash-variance deep-link, operator-link identity, and time-policy dating (with backfill) are contract-complete; spec sync green at the final revision.

**Deferred to [Milestone 11](#milestone-11--post-mvp-reporting-surface) (gap Q):** the upcoming-settlements view and the §7.2 monthly report — named and owned there, not silently dropped.

**Checks actually rerun in this batch:** full .NET solution: **378 Validators, 1,222 UseCases, 1,322 WebApi/architecture**, zero failures/skips. After that baseline sweep, one new PostgreSQL regression, `ReclassifyingTypes_ShouldChangeLockedMonthReports_WithoutChangingFinancialHistory`, compiled and passed separately: clear fee/leftover markers through the real type-update API, read the locked September full report and overview (`NotConfigured` while official variance/hours remain), restore the fee marker, and compare persisted transaction/close/settings versions and values plus close items byte-for-byte with the pre-change projection. This is 1,322 baseline HTTP/architecture tests plus one focused new test, not a claim that a new 1,323-test full sweep ran. Existing EF1002 fixture warnings remain; no new runtime warning or contract change.

The real local runbook was replayed with a unique owned PostgreSQL database, loopback SMTP capture and API process: migrate; CLI preview leaves Users empty; execute delivers setup locally; reset 204/login 200; five failed credentials yield 401, sixth and correct credentials 429 with Retry-After ≤900; restart retains that cooldown; missing account has the same sequence; recovery 202 → captured link → reset 204 removes only that account's persistent rows and login succeeds; a fresh API's 31st request is refused before validation with Retry-After ≤60. **Goal:** Replace the current "add already-registered user" membership flow with an invitation system that allows branch admins/managers to invite users by email, including users who have not yet registered.

**Scope boundary:** Invitation entity, email delivery integration, accept/decline flow, and the registration-via-invitation path — plus the account self-service the 2026-07 design review flagged as launch-blocking (`design/docs/screens.md` gap N): password recovery, change-password, and a minimal profile surface. The 2026-09 decisions add operated onboarding (gap C) with its platform-operator flag, Google sign-in, the caller `UserId` (design gap U), the self-membership guard, and operator-link requests (design gap V). The executable phase map below splits account security/invitations (Phases 1–5) from operated onboarding/platform, verified Google and operational close-out (Phases 6–8).

**Precondition:** Milestone 1 membership management is stable. This milestone was explicitly deferred from Milestone 1.

**Key behaviors:**

- Invitation entity with token, target email, target role, expiration, and status
- Provider-neutral SMTP email delivery with local capture in Phase 3; real sender/domain/provider acceptance stays Phase 8.
- Accept flow: existing user joins branch; new user registers and joins in one step
- Decline/expiration handling
- Permission matrix: `Admin` and `Manager` can invite; invited role follows the existing permission rules from Milestone 1
- **Password recovery (gap N):** forgot-password flow — emailed reset token → set new password; reuses the same email delivery integration. The missing recovery path originally blocked launch; Phase 3 supplies the backend contract, while production sender/UI/operational evidence still gates launch. **Token hardening is part of the contract, not an afterthought:** reset tokens are single-use, expiring, and stored **hashed at rest**; the request endpoint is throttled and **enumeration-safe** (the same response whether or not the e-mail exists); a successful reset revokes **all** active refresh sessions for the user.
- **Authenticated account self-service (gap N):** change-password with current-password confirmation, and a minimal profile read/update (name) — the endpoint behind the frontend's uncataloged Profile/session-menu surface (design M0 item 0.4.6). A successful change-password also revokes **all** active refresh sessions (not just the current one).
- **Logout:** an explicit logout endpoint that revokes the refresh token server-side (identity token expires naturally) — before Phase 2 there was no server-side logout revocation; its UI remains design 0.4.6 work.
- **Operated onboarding (gap C — decided below):** close the self-serve paths that exist today. `POST /user/register` is `[AllowAnonymous]` and `POST /branch/create` accepts any identity token; after this milestone an account is created only by accepting an invitation, and `POST /branch/create` is replaced by the platform-gated onboarding endpoint. Leaving them out of the design catalog hides them; it does not close them.
- **Onboarding use case ("onboard lotérica"):** creates the `Branch` with the default seeds (what `CreateBranchUseCase` does today), optionally the initial structure (terminals with paired Tabs, products, operators), and an `Admin` invitation to the owner's e-mail instead of making a caller the Admin. Two entry points call it: the platform-gated endpoint behind the back-office **"Nova lotérica"** wizard, and a command-line entry point for dev/staging with an optional demo-data option. A branch whose only member is the owner's pending Admin invitation is a valid initial state (the last-active-Admin invariant guards removal, not creation); an expired owner invitation is re-issued from the Plataforma area or the command line.
- **Platform-operator flag:** a flag on `User`, set only from the command line / database and never through the API, so it cannot be self-granted. It gates only the onboarding endpoint and the Plataforma area, and grants no access to any lotérica's data — branch access still comes only from membership.
- **Google sign-in:** signs in existing accounts and creates an account only when accepting an invitation; the Google e-mail must be verified and match the invited e-mail; it links to an existing password account only on a verified e-mail (otherwise the user confirms their password first); removing a membership ends access regardless of the sign-in method.
- **Caller user id (design gap U):** `ResponseUserLoginJson` gains `UserId`, so the client can mark self-recorded closes and the caller's own membership row without decoding the token.
- **Self-membership guard:** `PUT /branch/users/{branchUserId}/role` and `DELETE /branch/users/{branchUserId}` reject a caller acting on their own membership with a new verbatim error key — distinct from the last-active-Admin invariant, which protects the branch, not the caller.
- **Operator link at invitation + link requests (design gap V):** a `Member` invitation may pre-select an existing unlinked operator, and accepting it creates the link. The remaining cases go through a request entity: create (a Member with no operator link, or linked with no usable account assignment) and list/resolve (Manager/Admin).

**Decision outcomes — signed off 2026-09-11.** *(Recorded here and in the design gap register — `design/docs/screens.md` gaps C, N, U, V; the §6 spec rewrites and shared-spec revision bump ride with this milestone's implementation.)*

- **Gap C → operated onboarding.** The Lotero team creates each lotérica through the onboarding flow and invites its owner as `Admin`; every other person joins by invitation from a Manager/Admin under the existing role matrix. No public sign-up, no self-serve branch creation. *Why:* lotéricas are CEF-accredited businesses and early customers are few; setting one up (terminals and paired Tabs, products, operators, assignments, possibly legacy data) needs assistance anyway; it reuses the invitation flow instead of adding a public sign-up page and a create-branch screen; it keeps anonymous accounts and stray tenants out. Self-serve can be added later without changing how employees join. The owner's first screen is Invite accept, then a setup checklist on Manager Work Queue that shows what the team pre-filled as done.
- **Onboarding flow, not a bare command.** One use case, two entry points — the back-office wizard for real customers and the command line for the programming team's test lotéricas (with optional demo data). *Why:* a wizard makes onboarding repeatable for the team; the command line makes realistic test tenants a one-line task.
- **Platform authorization → a platform-operator flag on `User`**, never grantable through the API and carrying no lotérica data access. *Why:* the smallest safe permission above the per-lotérica roles; support access to a customer's data stays explicit through membership.
- **Google sign-in → a second way to sign in, never a second way in.** *Why:* staff commonly use personal Gmail and it cuts forgotten-password support (gap N), without opening a path around invitations; verified-e-mail matching and linking close the account-takeover path.
- **Gap U → `UserId` on the login response.** *Why:* known at the identity tier, one field, no new endpoint, and this milestone already changes that response for Google sign-in.
- **Self-demotion → blocked.** *Why:* self-service demotion has no use case here, and allowing it — even behind a confirmation — lets a Manager lock themselves out of a branch whose Admin is absent.
- **Operator-link requests → invitation pre-link first, requests for the rest.** *Why:* most missing links disappear at the source; the request flow covers the remainder instead of a dead-end "ask your manager".

##### Phase 8 local subset (SV-M8P8, planned 2026-10-21) — login abuse protection, regression and local operations

The composite 8.1/8.2 items above stay open: this subset delivers only what a local checkout can prove. Items tick only after independent review accepts them.

**Policy decision (8.1.1).** Three layers, all configurable under `AccountSecurity:*` with bounded values validated at startup (an invalid value stops the host):

| Layer | Partition | Default budget | Scope | Rejection |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 request limiter (`[EnableRateLimiting("login")]`) | client network | `LoginRequestsPerMinute` 30 (1–1000) per 60 s fixed window opened by the first admitted request, no queue | **process-local**; resets on restart; each API instance has its own budget | 429 before binding, validation, database or BCrypt |
| L2 persistent failure budget | (canonical email, client network) | `LoginClientFailureLimit` 5 (1–100) failures per `LoginFailureWindowMinutes` 15 (1–1440) | PostgreSQL, shared by every instance, survives restart | 429 for `LoginCooldownMinutes` 15 (1–1440) |
| L3 persistent failure budget | canonical email | `LoginAccountFailureLimit` 20 (≥ L2 limit, ≤ 1000; the default is checked against that bound too, so an L2 limit above 20 needs an explicit L3), same window | PostgreSQL | 429 for the same cooldown |

- **Client network** is the connection's remote address after the framework `ForwardedHeaders` middleware, which runs only when `ForwardedHeaders:KnownProxies`/`KnownNetworks` are configured and then unwraps only hops whose peer is trusted. `X-Forwarded-For` from anyone else is ignored. IPv4 (and IPv4-mapped IPv6) partitions by address, IPv6 by its /64; an absent address is one shared `unknown` partition. The existing recovery and capability limiters adopt the same partition and the same limiter implementation.
- **Counting.** Only a completed credential check that fails counts: wrong password for an active account and the dummy-hash check for a missing, inactive or passwordless account alike, and a wrong `CurrentPassword` on the Google first-link proof. Validation 400s, coordination 409s and any 429 neither count nor extend a block. A window that reaches its limit blocks until `now + cooldown`; the next failure after a block or after the window ends opens a new window. The request that reaches the limit still gets the ordinary 401.
- **Admission order.** Validate → canonical email → existing email advisory lock → read both rows `FOR UPDATE` → if either blocks, 429 with `Retry-After` = the longer remaining wait, **without** reading the user, BCrypt, token or refresh row → otherwise the unchanged verification. Failure writes both rows and commits before the unchanged 401; success deletes only the L2 row (a legitimate success never refills a distributed attacker's account budget).
- **Recovery.** Successful password reset and change-password delete every login-throttle row of the account in their existing transaction: a new password supersedes guesses at the old one. L1 is per network and is not reset, so a sign-in right after a reset may still meet the rest of that minute's 429. Renewal, logout, invitation registration and Google sign-in without a password proof never touch the counters.
- **Privacy.** Rows hold only SHA-256 key hashes (`login-account:` / `login-client:` prefixes over the canonical email and network), counts and UTC instants: no email, address, password or user id. All rejections are the existing 429 `SECURITY_RATE_LIMITED` resource with `Retry-After` in whole seconds (≥ 1), identical for every account state; nothing is logged per attempt.
- **Retention.** Each row carries `ExpiresAt` (block end, else window end). A hosted sweep (`SecurityThrottleCleanup:SweepIntervalMinutes` 15, `BatchSize` 500) deletes expired login rows and elapsed `RecoveryThrottle` rows in bounded `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` batches, which also discharges §3.23's retention gap; recovery reads now lock their row. Storage is bounded by keys that failed within window + cooldown + one sweep interval; per account at most L3 client rows exist per cycle, and L1 bounds admissions per client network per instance.
- **Accepted residual risk, disclosed.** A sustained attack from ≥ L3/L2 distinct client networks can keep re-imposing the account cooldown (never permanent; reset clears it, and an already-linked Google sign-in is unaffected). L1 multiplies by instance count and is not distributed; a deployment behind an unconfigured proxy collapses every client into the proxy's partition. Authenticated change-password is not counted (it needs an identity token). No CAPTCHA, lockout notice or policy API is added.

**Local acceptance cases.** LT1 missing/inactive/passwordless/wrong-password give identical 401s and identical 429s after the budget; LT2 the limit-th failure is 401, the next is 429 with no BCrypt, user read or token, a correct password while blocked is 429; LT3 window and block edges with a controllable `TimeProvider` (exact end admits); LT4 case/whitespace variants share one budget; LT5 a blocked client network does not block another until L3; LT6 L3 blocks every network; LT7 success clears only L2; LT8 reset/change-password clear; LT9 a concurrent burst yields exactly the limit of 401s; LT10 L1 budget, window expiry, partition isolation, IPv6 /64, `X-Forwarded-For` ignored by default and honored only from a configured proxy; LT11 Google proof shares the budget and a refused proof consumes no challenge; LT12 sweep deletes only expired/elapsed rows and skips locked ones; LT13 option bounds; LT14 OpenAPI 429 + `Retry-After`, auth-tier and snapshot gates.

- [x] **8.1.1** Policy above recorded before runtime (this record).
- [x] **8.1.2** L1 TimeProvider-based fixed-window limiter, client-network partition, configured forwarded-header trust and `Retry-After` on every 429 except recovery's suppressed 202.
- [x] **8.1.3** L2/L3 persistent budgets: `LoginThrottles` table and migration, guard shared by password login and the Google first-link proof, reset/change-password clearing; real PostgreSQL HTTP tests LT1–LT11.
- [x] **8.1.4** Retention sweep for login and recovery throttles (LT12) and option validation (LT13).
- [x] **8.1.5** Contract: login declares 429; every 429 publishes `Retry-After`; reviewed snapshot, export and Shared regeneration (LT14).
- [x] **8.1.6** Local runbook in `database-environments.md`: owned disposable database, local SMTP capture, explicit environment injection, first-operator preview/execute, option validation and the limiter's deployment assumptions; exercised on a disposable database with the limiter enabled.

> - **Remaining prerequisite before the markers move:** one independent review accepting both the pending M11 v56 catalog reconciliation (its repeat review) and this v57 security reconciliation; then both markers move to v57 and the real gate must pass. This subset does not touch M11 Phase 8, M9.5 3.3/3.7/AA or M10.

> **Independent review round 1 (2026-10-21) and its fixes — final acceptance review pending; no tick and no marker moved.** The reviewer reproduced the suites, Release build, migration/downgrade, Stryker, the runbook replay, OpenAPI/Shared gates and the design checks, and found:
>
> 1. **[P2] Default account budget could undercut a configured network budget (fixed).** `LoginProtectionOptionsFactory` returned an unset value's default without checking its bounds, so `LoginClientFailureLimit=21` with no account limit gave 21/20. The default now obeys the same bounds and startup stops naming `AccountSecurity:LoginAccountFailureLimit`; `ANetworkBudgetAboveTheDefaultAccountBudget_ShouldRequireAnExplicitAccountBudget` pins it (fails against the old code). The runbook, §6.16 and the 8.1.1 table state the rule.
> 2. **[P3] Runbook said every 409 is uncounted (fixed).** It now separates coordination 409s (`SECURITY_COORDINATION_BUSY`, uncounted) from the Google first-link proof's 409 `GOOGLE_LINK_PROOF_REQUIRED`, which counts, as §6.18 says; §6.16's counting sentence names the proof too.
> 3. **[P3] Reset was described as lifting every restriction (fixed).** A reset deletes the account's persistent budgets only; L1 is per network and keeps its current minute. `screens.md`, `product.md`, §6.16, the runbook and the 8.1.1 recovery bullet now say so, and `APasswordReset_ShouldClearThePersistentBudgets_ButNotTheNetworksRequestLimit` pins the reproduced behavior (429 with `Retry-After` ≤ 60 right after the reset, 200 once the window ends).
>
> Re-validation: Validators 378, UseCases 1,222, WebApi + architecture 1,322; Stryker on the factory and guard 51 killed, the same two equivalent survivors; design docs pass; doc-sync 34/34 in a disposable copy with v57 markers, while the real gate still rejects the v55 markers. No contract, OpenAPI or Shared change in this round. The reviewer confirmed the v56 catalog reconciliation matches `18012d5`; it does not close the historical M11 technical review or M11 Phase 8.

**Deferred provider/launch remainder (not part of this subset):** production sender/domain/DNS/TLS and received-link acceptance; a distributed (cross-instance) request limiter if M9 runs more than one instance; hosted `.env` rendering of the new optional keys; Production first-operator rollout; session/key rotation, monitoring and alerting; live Google (7.2.2); design 0.4.6 UI and real-app acceptance.

#### M8 accepted delivery and essential operations

**Accepted backend delivery (2026-10-18):** Phases 1–5 landed in `9ea777c`; operated onboarding, Google sign-in and the repeat-review corrections landed in `b96c5d3`. Independent technical/semantic review accepted both batches. Canonical docs and both design markers aligned at v54; item 5.5 records the final test/export/doc-sync evidence. This is historical acceptance, not a new test run or full-M8 launch approval. Exact API contracts remain in the canonical spec and server OpenAPI, rather than a second delivery document.

**First platform operator, local setup** (the full replayable procedure, with login-protection settings, is in [`database-environments.md`](database-environments.md#m8-local-security-operations)): migrate an owned Development/Staging database and configure `LOTO_CLI_AccountSecurity__ApplicationUrl` plus `LOTO_CLI_SecurityMail__*` for local SMTP capture. Supply the connection through `LOTO_M8_CONNECTION`, not a literal credential in command history. From the repository root, preview eligibility with:

```bash
dotnet run --project server/server.Cli/server.Cli.csproj -- bootstrap-platform \
  --environment Development --connection-env LOTO_M8_CONNECTION \
  --name "Operador de exemplo" --email operador@example.invalid
```

Add `--execute` only to create the first passwordless platform account and attempt delivery. Activate it through the normal `/user/reset-password` flow using the locally captured setup link, then log in and use operated onboarding. The CLI never accepts or prints the password/capability. Delivery retry is restricted to its same, still-unactivated sole account; it is not a general grant command. `FirstPlatformOperatorCliTest`, `M8LocalAcceptanceJourneyTest` and `OperatedOnboardingHttpTest` reproduce the CLI/API/PostgreSQL/local-mail journeys with disposable resources. Never reset a shared database or use the removed public register/create/add-member POST routes.

**Delivery and authority boundaries:** capabilities are hashed at rest; failed commits send nothing. SMTP `Sent` means server acceptance, not inbox arrival. Recovery's bounded volatile queue can lose delivery on crash/overflow; there is no automatic retry or durable plaintext outbox. A later recovery request or invitation reissue replaces the capability. Login failures use the same 401 for missing, inactive, wrong-password and passwordless accounts. Platform authority creates no branch membership; owner access begins only through invitation acceptance. Local mail and injected Google-verifier tests do not prove a real provider integration.

**Migration cautions:** `20261018040013_M8AccountSecurityAndInvitations` aborts on normalized-email collisions rather than choosing an account; email normalization cannot be undone by `Down`, so preserve the owning PostgreSQL locale/collation. `20261018165353_M8OperatedOnboardingAndGoogleIdentity` backfills no platform authority or mailbox proof; downgrading removes newly recorded Google/onboarding/proof state. Treat both downgrades as data-impacting operations, not lossless rollback.

**Remaining acceptance:** 7.2.2 and Phase 8 still require authorized live Google/actual-device sign-in, production sender/domain/DNS/TLS and received-link checks, a cross-instance request limiter if more than one API instance runs (the persistent login budgets of the Phase 8 local subset are already shared), session/key rotation and retention/monitoring review, design 0.4.6 UI handoff and real-app acceptance. Production first-operator setup additionally requires the explicit `--allow-production-bootstrap --execute` gate, `--connection-env`, trusted HTTPS app URL and enabled TLS SMTP under that future runbook. These instructions record the implementation boundary; they do not authorize or certify production setup.

## Milestone 9 — Deployment, CI & Observability

**Goal:** Establish the production deployment pipeline, continuous integration, health checks, structured logging, and monitoring for the hosted `Staging` and `Production` environments.

**Scope boundary:** Infrastructure automation and operational readiness. No new business features.

**Precondition:** Core business milestones are stable enough for a production deploy.

**Key behaviors:**

- CI pipeline: build, test (all three suites), and architecture-test gate on every push
- CD pipeline: deploy to `Staging` on merge to main, promote to `Production` on tag/release
- Health check endpoints for database connectivity and service readiness
- Structured logging and error tracking integration
- Automated EF migration application during deploy
- Environment-specific secret management aligned with the `infra/` contract from Milestone 0
