## Milestone 9.5 — Legacy Reconciliation Semantics Audit

**Goal:** Verify, with the shop's operational knowledge, the real-world meaning of every legacy transaction type and close-product slot the port carried over by name, resolve the representational ambiguities the 2026-07 Access audit surfaced, and pin the chosen representations with tests, seed renames, and spec notes — so the M10 import maps legacy data onto *verified* semantics instead of name lookalikes.

**Re-scoped 2026-10-20 (owner).** The product-team questions sent on 2026-10-20 had no answer in time, so the owner answered them with the best assumptions available from the Access code and Brazilian lotérica/banking practice — see [Working answers](#working-answers-2026-10-20--best-assumptions-before-the-product-team-answers). Phase 2 items are closed on those assumptions, each naming the question whose real answer may still revise it. Only two answers change code before release — **question 3 (Tarifa Bolão)** and the **Operador part of question 9** — because they change the live close-day variance from the first day of use, with or without any import; they ship as Phase 3 items 3.5–3.8. Every other answer is used as a default for M11 reports and M10 import mapping, where it stays configurable. The milestone no longer waits on shop verification, and M10 no longer waits on this milestone.

**Scope boundary:** Verification, characterization tests, seed naming, and documentation, plus the release correction of items 3.5–3.8. The §6.12 variance formula does **not** change in this milestone — items 3.5–3.8 only move two values from counted products to ledger rows; any formula-adjacent evolution (net-zero bucket transfers, per-bucket attribution) exits through the Phase 3 decision gate into its own milestone.

**Precondition:** Backend contracts through **M7.6**; frontend M0 screen catalog at its **reviewed baseline**. **Sequencing note (no cycle):** M7.7 Phase 5 (fiado settlement implementation) waits on *this* milestone's Phase 2.2 — run Phase 2.2 early; nothing here waits on M7.7 completing. Confirmed facts going in (2026-07 audit): the §6.12 **sign convention** is Access-verified and regression-pinned; per the owner, the shop currently creates no lancamento for raspadinha sales. **Historical audit question, now provisionally answered by working Q5:** the canonical representation of ordinary product sales (count-only vs product-transaction-explained). The prospective canonical choice is count-only, subject to Q5; §6.12 retains the exactly-once invariant: each value is a ledger row or a counted asset, never both.

**Key behaviors:**

- **Test-first.** The milestone opens by characterizing **both** consistent representations in the suites — neither is pinned as canonical until Phase 2 verification.
- **The open question that names this milestone:** why do `Raspadinha`/`Telesena` transaction types exist? Hypothesis A: they record **non-sale exits** — bulk returns/encalhe to the distributor (the R$5,686.00 tipo-20 row), prize payouts on winning cards, damaged/void stock — events where value leaves custody with no compensating counted asset. Hypothesis B: they are the legacy way to **explain a product sale as a row**, with the proceeds kept out of the Dinheiro count. Both reconcile when used consistently; Phase 2 decides which the shop practiced and which the new system canonizes.
- **Known mechanical divergence to resolve:** Access included the fiado balance inside the conferência totals (`TxtFiado`/`TxtFiadoIni` in `AtualizarDiferenca`; earliest data even stored it as counted slot `prod_id 1`), so cash tab-paybacks and credit sales of counted stock netted to zero. The port removed fiado from the close entirely (§6.4 query-time) — so a cash payback entering the counted drawer, or counted stock leaving on credit, distorts the terminal's per-day variance by ±V with no terminal-side row. This is the highest-stakes item in the audit.

### Phase 1 — Characterization tests (before any decision; none pins a canonical form)

- [x] **1.1** Count-only representation: stock −V and cash +V with no ledger rows → variance 0.
- [x] **1.2** Product-transaction-explained representation: product-typed `Saídas` row of V, stock −V, proceeds **not** counted → variance 0.
- [x] **1.3** Double-count failure mode: the swap counted **plus** a product-typed `Saídas` row of V → variance +V. The anti-pattern under *either* representation — the same value represented twice.
- [x] **1.4** Fiado divergence characterization: (a) Tab `In` payback of V with drawer counted +V and no terminal row → terminal variance +V; (b) credit sale handing out counted stock −V → terminal variance −V. Pin current behavior explicitly as *characterization pending Phase 2 verification*, with the Access fiado-in-totals contrast in the test comments.

> **Phase 1 progress note (2026-10-17) — items 1.1–1.4 characterized; independently validated and accepted (SV04).** `tests/UseCases.Test/Services/DailyCloses/CashVarianceRepresentationCharacterizationTest.cs` runs the production `CashVarianceCalculator` over one Terminal day (opening cash 1,000 + counted stock 1,000, V = 5): count-only swap → 0 (1.1); product `Out` V with stock −V and proceeds not counted → 0 (1.2); swap counted plus an `Out` V → +V (1.3); fiado cash receipt with no Terminal row → +V and counted-stock fiado sale with no Terminal row → −V (1.4), with the Access `AtualizarDiferenca` fiado-in-totals contrast in the comments. Because M7.7 Phase 5.6 now writes the Terminal legs, 1.4's inputs are built directly (historical, imported or non-counted operations), and a sixth case shows the signed counterparts bring both to 0; the commands were not weakened. No representation is canonized; Phase 2 remains open.

### Working answers (2026-10-20 — best assumptions before the product team answers)

Each answer below is the **best assumption available before the product team answers question N** of the list sent on 2026-10-20 (numbered 1–10 here). It was taken from the Access form code and from common Brazilian lotérica and banking practice, not from the shop. A real answer that contradicts one reopens only the items that consumed it. Canonical `docs/loto-specs.md` §9 keeps its own numbering (§9 Q1–Q6 = the M11 plan's SQ1–SQ5 for §9 Q2–Q6); the cross-reference column links them.

| Q | Topic | Working answer | Consumed by | Cross-ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **3** | **Tarifa Bolão** | **That day's bolão service fee, not a carried balance.** Lotéricas may charge a service fee on top of the bolão quota (up to 35%) and keep it as their own revenue; the CAIXA terminal does not account for it, so it is cash in the drawer that must be declared as an explained inflow. The Access calculation proves the shape: `AtualizarDiferenca` adds the **current day's** `TxtTarBolao` to the initial total only — a carried balance would appear on both sides. The month figure is the sum of the daily values. Source of the value: the day's bolão sales (quota count × fee). | **Release** — items 3.5–3.8; M11 Phase 6 (SQ3) | 2.3 · §9 Q4 · SQ3 |
| **9** | **Old screen fields** | **`Operador` is a typed, expected inflow on the opening side** — most likely the CAIXA terminal's net movement for that operator's day, otherwise a cash top-up (suprimento). Both readings need the same representation (an `In` row on the Terminal), so the code decision does not wait on which one is right; only the type's label does, and admins can rename it. `Fiado` and `Total Caixa` were calculated by the form; `HorasTrabalhadas` and `Ausente` were timesheet data stored in the stock table. Typed by the user: the product counts, `Operador` and `Tarifa Bolão`; calculated: `Fiado`, the totals and the difference. | **Release** (Operador) — items 3.5–3.8; M10 mapping (the rest) | 2.3 · 2.4 |
| 1 | Cards | The card processor owes the shop until payout: debit the next business day, credit in two business days (automatic early payout), matching the seeded `SettlementRule`s. Payouts arrive grouped per day/brand, with the fee deducted at source; the seeded `Tarifa cartão` type records the fee separately. A card row is a receivable, marked paid when the payout lands on the bank account. | M11 Phases 4–5 (instrument seed = `CreditCard`/`DebitCard`) | 2.5 · §9 Q2 · SQ1 |
| 2 | Cheques | Single cheques exist as well as pre-dated plans. "Paid" means **cleared** (compensado), not deposited. A returned cheque (devolução) becomes the client's debt again (fiado). | M11 Phases 4–5 (`Depósito Cheque` = `Cheque`; aging and upcoming settlements also cover single cheque rows) | 2.5 · §9 Q3 · SQ2 |
| 4 | Sobras | `Sobra de Bolão` = bolão quotas unsold before the draw that the shop must keep; `Sobra de Federal` = unsold Federal fractions the shop kept. Both are commercial losses on the Terminal; the month figure is the sum of their active rows. | M11 Phase 6 (SQ4) | 2.5 · §9 Q5 · SQ4 |
| 5 | Products | An ordinary cash sale is **count-only** (stock down, cash up, no row) — consistent with the owner fact that raspadinha sales create no lançamento. `Raspadinha`, `Telesena`, `Troca de Telesena` and `Encalhe Federal` rows record value leaving custody **without** a sale: returns/encalhe to the distributor, exchanges, prizes paid in product, damaged or void stock. Product deliveries and restocks change the count only. | 3.1 (canonical = count-only); 3.2 renames wait for confirmation | 2.1 · 3.1 |
| 6 | Other types | `Depósito Dinheiro` = cash taken from the drawer to the bank (sangria); `PIX` and `MarketPlace` = paid without cash entering the drawer; `Pgto Prêmio` = prize paid in cash; `Desconto` = discount given; `Volante rejeitado` = refund of a rejected slip; `Outras Despesas` = small expenses paid from the drawer. All are Terminal `Out` rows. | Seed names stay (admin-editable); M10 default mapping | 2.5 |
| 7 | Bank | Bank accounts record deposits received, card payouts, bank fees and the CAIXA settlement (`Crédito Banco`/`Débito Banco`). A movement from the drawer to the bank is recorded on **both** sides (Terminal `Out` + bank `In`). Prizes are netted in CAIXA's daily settlement, not reimbursed separately. Bank accounts stay outside the close. | M10 default mapping | 2.6 |
| 8 | Fiado | Anything the shop sells may go on fiado (bolão quotas, games, Telesena, raspadinha, bills paid for the client). When a counted item leaves the drawer, the operator uses the shipped counted-stock flag (M7.7 1.3 / Phase 5.6); otherwise the Tab row alone records the debt. No closing-product selector is added. | Operator guidance; no code | §9 Q1 |
| 10 | Shared terminal | One close per Terminal/day, with recorder and submitter shown (already shipped). Close-derived figures are never split between operators; hours stay per operator/day — the SQ5 recommendation, adopted as the working answer. | M11 Phase 6 (SQ5) | §9 Q6 · SQ5 |

**Release impact of Q3 and Q9, worked out.** Access: `Dif = (Final products + Lançamentos) − (Opening products + Operador(today) + TarBolão(today))`. Before this correction: `Tarifa Bolão` is a counted product, so today's value enters `TotalClosing` and the previous close's value enters `TotalOpening`, while `Operador` is not represented at all. With every row an outflow, port − Access = **2·T(today) − T(previous) + Operador(today)**: R$120 of fees today after R$100 yesterday shows **+R$140** where Access showed 0. Recording both values as `In` rows instead gives `Final − Opening − (In − Out)`, which is algebraically identical **under the stated all-outflow scenario assumptions**, with no formula change; it does not prove global historical Access parity or repair the first transition from an old counted-fee opening.

**Evidence available and its limits.** The checkout holds no Access database or production data; a local export at `../export` (outside the repository) was inspected read-only on 2026-10-17/18. It verifies the exported artifact, not its completeness, production provenance or the shop's practice, and it contains test-like data: `TblLancamentos.csv` row `lco_id 5` has description `teste1`; `TblEstoque.csv` row `est_id 42` holds Tarifa Bolão (`prod_id 7`) at R$2,345,657.00. So the export confirms mechanics, never meanings. Source anchors (line numbers in the inspected export):

- `forms/all_form_code.txt:1036–1062` loads the current day's stock, including `prod 7` into `TxtTarBolao` and `prod 12` into `TxtOperador`; `:1594–1620` adds both to `TxtTotalIni`, adds `TxtLancamentos` to `TxtTotalFinal` and subtracts initial from final.
- `forms/all_form_code.txt:1023–1031`: `strFiltro` filters date and account only, so `DSum("lco_valor", …)` ignores `lco_sinal` and category direction (import caveat — see M10).
- `tables/TblTipoCategoria.csv` confirms Raspadinha 20, Telesena 18, Troca de Telesena 19, Encalhe Federal 22, Sobra de Bolão 6, Sobra de Federal 9 and Depósito Cheque 15; `tables/TblProduto.csv` confirms Tarifa Bolão 7, Operador 12 and the legacy slots of 2.4.

Reproduce the focused checks, including their build step:

```bash
dotnet test server/tests/UseCases.Test --filter 'FullyQualifiedName~CashVarianceRepresentationCharacterizationTest|FullyQualifiedName~CashVarianceCalculatorTest|FullyQualifiedName~CreateBranch' -v minimal
```

The R$5,686.00 row (export `lco_id 172`, 2025-11-22, Terminal account 6, `id_tipo 20`, `Negativo`, empty description) is assumed to be a raspadinha return/encalhe — the size fits a bulk return, not a sale; best assumption before the product team answers question 5.
- [x] **2.2** *(Decided by the owner 2026-10-01 → (a) paired terminal rows: a counted-stock fiado sale writes a linked Terminal stock saída, a cash repayment a linked Terminal cash entrada, a non-cash repayment none; the Tab delta is never added to the variance. Implementation and spec rewrite: M7.7 Phase 5.6/5.9; forward markers in §6.4/§6.12 at v43. Still open for this audit: which other fiado sales move counted assets — loto-specs §9.)* Fiado × drawer interaction: do cash paybacks physically enter the counted drawer? Do credit sales hand out counted stock? If yes to either, choose: (a) paired terminal rows / bucket-transfer representation, (b) include the fiado delta in variance for terminals with paired Tabs (Access parity), or (c) accept and document the per-day distortion. Best assumption before the product team answers question 9.

Best assumption before the product team answers question 7.

- The fiado-in-totals divergence is resolved (M7.7 Phase 5.6) and the opening-side adjustments are resolved by items 3.5–3.8: Tarifa Bolão and Operador are `In` rows and the characterized exact-count scenarios match the explicitly modeled Access opening-side calculation, without a claim of global historical parity.
- M10's default mapping references this milestone's working answers; M10 does not wait for product confirmation.

## Milestone 10 — Branch Go-Live: Opening Count & Access Import

**Goal:** Let a branch start using the system with correct opening values: either by entering an **opening count** per Terminal (*saldo de implantação*) or by **importing its legacy Access data**, which produces that opening as its last imported close. Both are product features — a backend route plus a menu screen that explains what to provide — not a release precondition built around unanswered questions.

**Re-scoped 2026-10-20 (owner).** The import no longer waits on M9.5 shop verification: it maps legacy data with the M9.5 working answers as **defaults the admin can change**, and shows the result before writing. Real data is expected to need mapping adjustments; that is what the dry run is for.

**Scope boundary:** Branch-scoped go-live data only. Not a recurring sync — the import is a one-time cutover per branch. No variance-formula change.

**Precondition:** All entity milestones (2–6) are complete so the target schema exists; **M7.7 is complete** (imported data must be validated against the final integrity contracts — close/ledger rules, month lock, pairing, settlement semantics — not the pre-M7.7 ones); M9.5 items 3.5–3.8 are shipped, so Access `prod 7`/`prod 12` have a target. Detailed phases are written when the milestone starts.

**Release split:** Part A (opening count) ships with the release — without it, each Terminal's first close opens at zero and its official variance is the whole drawer, which then stays in the variance summary and month-close checks. Part B (Access import) ships when ready, before or after release.

**Key behaviors — Part A, opening count:**

- A Manager/Admin records, per Terminal, the counted value of each product as of the go-live date. It becomes the opening of the Terminal's first close and carries **no** variance of its own; it is not a close in the variance summary, Work Queue or month-close readiness.
- **Owner decision (2026-10-21):** transactions are **not** refused on a Terminal without an opening count — a Terminal may legitimately start at zero, and fiado operations already write automatic legs on the paired Terminal. Instead, the Terminal's first close is refused until an opening count (possibly an explicit all-zero one) exists, so "never set" and "real zero" stay distinguishable. A Terminal that already has a prior counted close, or an imported one (Part B), needs no opening count. The phase plan decides the refusal's error key and whether the opening count joins the owner setup checklist (`design/docs/screens.md` currently leaves it to this milestone).
- Today's workaround is a backdated counted close before go-live; it works, but persists the whole drawer as an official variance — the reason Part A exists.
- Opening fiado balances and still-open cheque plans already have entry paths (a Tab `Out` row per client; the cheque-plan create) and need only menu guidance, not new endpoints.

**Key behaviors — Part B, Access import:**

- Upload of the Access export tables, then an **admin mapping step**: each legacy `tipo` → a TransactionType (or skip), each legacy `prod` → a Product, an `In` type (`prod 7` Tarifa Bolão, `prod 12` Operador — M9.5 3.6) or skip (`prod 1/8/9/10` by default — M9.5 2.4), each legacy `conta` → an Account. Defaults come from the M9.5 working answers.
- **Dry run before commit:** per Terminal/day, the recalculated variance beside the difference Access showed, with every mismatch, data-quality flag and skipped row listed. Nothing is written until the admin confirms.
- Map legacy Access IDs to new Guid-based entities; preserve historical transactions, daily closes and time entries; the last imported counted close becomes the opening (Part A is then not needed for that Terminal).
- Handle legacy data quality issues (missing fields, orphaned references); validate against current business rules and flag violations for manual review — never silently correct.
- Import is branch-scoped: each legacy database maps to one `Branch`; a re-run of the same import is idempotent.
- A menu screen explains which Access tables to export and how, what each mapping means, and how to read the dry run.

**Known export facts the import must handle** (read-only inspection of the local `../export`, 2026-10-17/18; line numbers refer to that export):

- **Sign.** The Access conferência summed `lco_valor` without `lco_sinal` (`forms/all_form_code.txt:1023–1031`: `strFiltro` filters date and account only). Joining `tables/TblContas.csv` to `TblLancamentos.csv` on `id_conta = con_id` with `con_tipo = Terminal` gives 118 rows: 116 `Negativo`, 2 `Positivo`. Each positive row counted +V in Access but counts −V through the port's `In − Out`, so Access was higher by 2V on that day: R$10.00 for `lco_id 5` and R$1,032.40 for `lco_id 193`. A per-row comparison, not reconstructed full-day parity; the dry run must surface such days, not assume every historical Terminal row is an outflow.
- **Category mismatches.** `lco_id 5` (R$5.00, `id_tipo 23`, description `teste1`) is filed under category 1 / Receita while type 23 belongs to category 3 / Entradas; `lco_id 193` (R$516.20, `id_tipo 2` / Cliente) is filed under category 3 / Entradas while type 2 belongs to category 9 / Saídas (`TblTipoCategoria.csv`, `TblCategoria.csv`). Flag, never silently correct.
- **Test-like rows.** `lco_id 5` (`teste1`) and `TblEstoque` `est_id 42` (Tarifa Bolão R$2,345,657.00) show the export mixes test data; the import must let the admin exclude rows or date ranges.
- **IDs.** `TblTipoCategoria.csv` and `TblProduto.csv` confirm the §5 `tipo_id`/`prod_id` mapping used by the defaults (M9.5 working answers).

## Milestone 11 — Post-MVP Reporting Surface

**Goal:** Ship the §7.2 manager reports the MVP catalog deferred (design `screens.md` gap Q) and, per the owner decision of 2026-10-16, a separate read-only **Dashboard**: a concise overview of the latest relevant facts from every manager report, each fact linking to its full report. The Manager Work Queue stays the exception-first management home; the Dashboard neither takes its place nor performs its actions. "M11" is this milestone's number, not a count of dashboard areas, and the plan below covers report questions, not a fixed number of cards.

**Scope boundary:** Read-only reporting endpoints, the additive whole-set totals the overview needs, the classification metadata named in Phases 4 and 6 (each an explicit, tested migration), and their spec/catalog sync. No new ledger semantics, no financial writes, and no job queue, push stream, cache, materialized balance or summary table. M8 onboarding, M9 deployment and M10 import are not on this path: no section reads data they create.

**Precondition:** M7.7 (variance integrity and fiado semantics) and M9.5 (verified type/product meanings) stay this milestone's preconditions and gate its close-out (Phase 8). Each runtime phase names the exact earlier items it consumes, and Phase 1.9 records the evidence for any phase that starts before the whole precondition closes. No runtime phase starts before Phase 1 is reviewed.

**Owner schedule (2026-10-16):** this plan → the remaining M7.7 items and the M9.5 evidence this plan names → source-report Phases 2–6, one reviewed phase at a time → overview Phase 7 → close-out and real captures Phase 8 → design D13/D14 → the design M1 comparison resumes. Shared M2 is not made to wait for M11; it regenerates its client when these contracts land.

**Queue rename (history):** this milestone's tripwire tripped on 2026-10-15, and the `GET /report/dashboard` → `GET /report/manager-queue` rename shipped in [M7.7 Phase 7.8](#phase-78--manager-queue-rename--opt-in-openapi-export-server-m11-tripwire-coordination-label-sv03-shared-m2-phase-1-handoffs-h1--h2), all four `ResponseManagerQueue*Json` DTOs included. It is no longer M11 work; the obsolete surface must never return.

### Planning record (2026-10-16)

Audit baseline: `parallel/server` at `26fb2bb` (M7.7 Phase 7.8 integrated), spec `v44`. Read directly: `ReportController` with its eight report use cases, validators, repositories and DTOs; `GET /dailyclose`; `CreateBranchSeedFactory`; the single and installment create rules; canonical §6.4, §6.12, §6.14, §7.2 and §9; M7.7; M9.5; `design/docs/product.md` and `screens.md` (gap Q); Shared M2's D2/D3 and H3/H4 (still undecided).

**What the shipped reports answer, and where they stop** (verified in code):

- **Daily ledger** `GET /report/daily-ledger` — one required `AccountId`, window ≤ 366 days; whole-window `TotalIn`/`TotalOut`/`NetTotal` and a running `OpeningBalance`/`ClosingBalance` over Active rows; items paged (≤ 200). There is no multi-account view, and its balance is a ledger running balance, not cash on hand.
- **Fiado balance** `GET /report/fiado/balance` — one row per client with a non-zero `Out − In` over Active Tab rows dated ≤ `AsOfDate`, branch-wide, unpaged. `TotalOutstanding` is the **signed** sum, so a client credit silently reduces total debt; §6.14 does not document this today.
- **Fiado aging** `GET /report/fiado/aging` — Active Tab rows with `PaidAt IS NULL`, **direction-unfiltered** (repayment `In` rows age as debt) and **not filtered by `AsOfDate`** (it only computes buckets); paged, with no amount totals. M7.7 5.1 replaces it with FIFO netting.
- **Open-cheque aging** `GET /report/cheques/open-aging` — groups rows by `OriginTransactionId`, so only installment plans appear; open means `PaidAt IS NULL`, never filtered by `AsOfDate`; groups are paged with no whole-filter amount or open-row count, and each page reads siblings one group at a time. A single cheque created through `POST /transaction` carries no `OriginTransactionId` and never appears. "Cheque" itself rests on `SettlementRule = OperatorEnteredCheque` (installments are refused for any other rule) — a due-date rule, not an instrument identity.
- **Cash-variance summary** `GET /report/cash-variance` — persisted "Diferença Caixa" values of active non-Draft closes (Submitted, Approved **and Rejected**), window ≤ 366 days; whole-window total, mean, max and min; items paged in memory; no per-status split. The product is resolved by its display name.
- **Monthly reconciliation** `GET /report/monthly-reconciliation/{year}/{month}` — every calendar day with its closes (variance `0`, not null, while a close is Draft), transaction counts by status, structured blockers, and `LockReady` from the lock command's evaluator. It does not return `LockDate`, whether the month is already locked, or whether it has fully elapsed (the command refuses the current month).
- **Operator summary** `GET /report/operator-summary` — one operator: a Member's own, or for Manager/Admin a named operator or `Mine` (otherwise 400); attributed by `RecordedByOperatorId`; no branch-wide mode. M7.7 5.8 adds the generated-row share.
- **Time-entry balance** `GET /report/timeentry-balance` — already has a Manager/Admin branch-wide roll-up (every active operator, zero-entry rows included), per-entry effective policy, one captured branch-local now, and `ContainsInProgress`.
- **Manager queue** `GET /report/manager-queue` and **`GET /dailyclose`** (`Date DESC, CreatedAt DESC, Id DESC`) — together they already hold the queue counts and the facts recent closes need.
- **Seeds** — `Cartão de Crédito`, `Cartão de Débito` and `Depósito Cheque` are `Saídas` (Out) types distinguished only by `SettlementRule` and a mutable name; `Tarifa Bolão` was a counted **product** at this original planning baseline; M9.5 now replaces the fresh seed with an ordinary In transaction type (Phase 6.1 must use that source); `Sobra de Bolão` / `Sobra de Federal` are `Despesas Comerciais` types. None carries a stable instrument or report identity.

In short, the source reports mostly exist, but only cash variance and time entries already give whole-set totals in the preview-plus-total shape the Dashboard needs. Upcoming settlements and the consolidated monthly report do not exist, and three sources — fiado totals, fiado aging and settlement instruments — need signed semantics before they can be summarized honestly.

### Definitions — "latest", windows and freshness

- **One clock read.** The overview reads `IBranchClock.UtcNow()` once. `GeneratedAtUtc` is that instant, `AsOfDate` its branch-local business date, and `BranchLocalNow` its branch-local time (used for in-progress hours). No section reads the clock again.
- **`AsOfDate` is always branch-local today.** It is returned, never accepted. Open cheques, open settlements and fiado repayments are current state (`PaidAt`, FIFO over today's rows), so a past as-of date would mix a historical window with today's settlement state. Historical questions stay in the full reports, which take their own windows.
- **Month selector.** Optional `Year`/`Month`, both or neither, defaulting to the month of `AsOfDate`; a later month is a 400, and the floor is 2000-01 (the reconciliation validator's range). For the current month every month-scoped window ends at `AsOfDate` and is flagged `IsPartialMonth`; a past month uses its full calendar month. The selector never jumps to "the last month with data".
- **Section windows**, each returned inside its section: point-in-time sections are as of `AsOfDate`; day sections cover `AsOfDate` only; month sections cover the selected month window; recent closes cover `[AsOfDate − 6, AsOfDate]`; upcoming settlements cover due dates `[AsOfDate, AsOfDate + 13]` (14 calendar days including today), with overdue items reported separately. Fourteen days covers card settlements one or two business days out even across holidays, plus the next deposit cycle of pre-dated cheques, without turning the overview into the cheque report; the full upcoming-settlements report accepts any due window up to 366 days.
- **No borrowed days.** An empty today is shown as empty for today. Last-activity dates (per account) are data in their own right, returned separately from `GeneratedAtUtc`, and neither implies the other. Refresh is manual; nothing implies live updates.
- **No merged money.** No section adds ledger movements across accounts into revenue, profit or cash on hand; no client-derived variance is persisted; client credit never offsets debt; and a cheque row that appears in both open cheques and upcoming settlements is never added across the two sections.

### Section-to-source matrix

Every section is Manager/Admin of the authenticated branch — branch taken from the session, no Operator link required, in either navigation mode — computed by branch-filtered, read-only, no-tracking server aggregation. Totals cover every matching row whatever the preview size, and previews are ordered deterministically with an `Id` tie-breaker. Money is BRL; variance is signed (`+` surplus, `−` shortage, per §6.12); hours are decimal hours.

### Overview contract — proposal reviewed in Phase 1

- **Route and names.** `GET /report/management-overview?Year&Month`, `[TokenAuthorize(Role.Manager, Role.Admin)]`, request `RequestManagementOverviewJson { int? Year, int? Month }`, response `ResponseManagementOverviewJson`. The screen may be titled Dashboard; the API never uses `dashboard`. `GET /report/manager-queue` is unchanged, and neither name gets an alias.
- **Envelope.** `GeneratedAtUtc`, `AsOfDate`, `BranchLocalNow`, `Year`, `Month`, `MonthDateFrom`, `MonthDateTo`, `IsPartialMonth`, then one member per matrix row: `Queue`, `RecentCloses`, `CashVariance`, `Reconciliation`, `Fiado` (debt/credit and aging), `OpenCheques`, `UpcomingSettlements`, `AccountActivity`, `OperatorActivity`, `Hours`, `MonthlyFinancial`. Section DTOs are named `ResponseManagementOverview{Section}Json`. Each carries its own window fields, a `Status` (`OverviewSectionStatus { Ready, Empty, NotConfigured }`), its whole-set totals, `PreviewLimit`, `TotalCount`, and the ids and filter values its drill-down needs (account, operator, client, origin transaction, month, due window, instrument, status). Account and operator rows also carry `IsActive`, because a deactivated entity with activity in the window stays listed.
- **Failure model: atomic for failures, typed for known states.** Validation (400), authentication (401) and role (403) fail the whole request before any section reads. Any runtime failure or cancellation fails the whole request: there is no partial 200. `NotConfigured` is returned only for deterministic missing configuration (no branch type, active or retired, carries an instrument; report markers unset) and is never rendered as zero; `Empty` means the source ran and found nothing. No "not yet supported" status ships, because the overview is delivered only once every section's source is delivered (Phase 7 gate).
- **Metadata.** Declared statuses `{200, 400, 401, 403}`; no 404, since nothing is looked up by id. Numeric metadata follows Shared D2 (H3 if adopted) and the auth tier follows D3 (H4 if adopted); the overview adds no new Shared prerequisite. Whether a future month gets its own error key or reuses the reconciliation validator's keys is decided in Phase 7.
- **Full reports it needs** (names provisional until their phases):
  - `GET /report/upcoming-settlements?DueDateFrom&DueDateTo&AccountId&Instrument&Page&PageSize` → `ResponseUpcomingSettlementsJson { AsOfDate, IsConfigured, DueDateFrom, DueDateTo, TotalCount, TotalValue, OverdueCount, OverdueValue, ByInstrument, Items, TotalPages, HasNext, HasPrevious }`; items `{ TransactionId, OriginTransactionId?, Date, DueDate, Value, Instrument, TransactionTypeName, AccountId, AccountName, ClientId?, ClientName? }`. Overdue uses the same account/instrument filters and is independent of the due window.
  - `GET /report/monthly-financial/{year}/{month}` → `ResponseMonthlyFinancialReportJson` with month totals, per-`(Date, Account)` close figures (official variance, Tarifa Bolão, recorder and submitter shown), per-`(Date, Account, operator)` bolão leftovers, and per-`(Date, Operator)` hours with in-progress flags. The exact shape waits on Phase 6.1.
  - Additive members on shipped responses (non-breaking): open-cheque aging `OutstandingTotal`, `OpenRowCount`, `ByBucket`; fiado balance `DebtorCount`, `TotalDebt`, `CreditorCount`, `TotalCredit` (`TotalOutstanding` keeps its shipped signed meaning, now documented); fiado aging after 5.1 `OutstandingTotal`, `ByBucket`, `OldestUnpaidDate`; cash variance `SubmittedCount`, `ApprovedCount`, `RejectedCount`; monthly reconciliation `LockDate`, `IsMonthLocked`, `IsMonthElapsed`. Daily ledger, operator summary and time-entry balance also accept a deactivated branch account/operator for Manager/Admin (2.4), with an active flag in the response.

### Read consistency, reuse and limits

- **One snapshot.** The overview runs its sections one after another on one DbContext inside one read-only PostgreSQL transaction at `REPEATABLE READ`, so every section sees the same committed state. It never calls `SaveChanges`, injects no `IUnitOfWork`, and rolls the transaction back on exit; the boundary is a small Domain interface implemented in Infrastructure, named in Phase 7. No parallel EF operations. The full reports keep their current per-query reads: parity is tested on quiescent fixtures, and the overview's snapshot is tested on its own (A17).
- **One rule, two consumers.** Each section calls the same repository query or projection as its full report, extracted where needed, so a predicate change cannot leave the Dashboard behind. Parity tests (A1–A12) compare every section with its full report for the same scope and window, across several pages.
- **Bounded cost.** Every section is one or two grouped queries; previews use `Take(n)` with the full report's ordering; no loop over HTTP endpoints, no page-by-page totals, and the open-cheque per-group sibling reads are not reused. Cancellation flows through every query, and reads never write.
- **Generation guard.** A WebApi OpenAPI test asserts that no path or schema name contains `dashboard` (case-insensitive), backing Shared's generation gate from the server side.

### Shop facts and owner decisions that block financial definitions

Shop facts belong to M9.5; planning and the unblocked phases continue while they are open. The signed 2.2 fiado × drawer decision (linked Terminal movements) is not reopened.

> **Working answers (2026-10-20).** The product team did not answer in time, so the owner adopted the best available assumptions — recorded in M9.5's [Working answers](#working-answers-2026-10-20--best-assumptions-before-the-product-team-answers) table, each pending the product team's answer to its question: **SQ1** → question 1 (cards are receivables until the payout lands; seed `CreditCard`/`DebitCard`), **SQ2** → question 2 (single cheques exist; paid = cleared; seed `Cheque` on `Depósito Cheque`; aging and upcoming settlements cover single cheque rows), **SQ3** → question 3 (that day's fee; the month figure is the sum of daily values; after M9.5 3.6 the source is the `Tarifa Bolão` **transaction type**, not a product), **SQ4** → question 4 (unsold quotas/fractions the shop kept; sum of active rows), **SQ5** → question 10 (the recommendation below, adopted). Phases 4–6 may plan on these answers; each phase records which assumption it consumed so a contradicting answer reopens only that phase.

- **SQ1 — card settlements (M9.5 2.5).** For `Cartão de Crédito` / `Cartão de Débito` rows: is the amount owed to the lotérica by the acquirer until it lands, on which account, does `DueDate` mean the landing date, and does the shop mark `PaidAt` when it lands? If not, cards are not upcoming settlements.
- **SQ2 — cheques (M9.5 2.5).** Does the shop ever record a pre-dated cheque as a single `Depósito Cheque` row rather than a plan, and does `PaidAt` mean deposited or cleared?
- **SQ3 — Tarifa Bolão (M9.5 2.3).** What the counted `Tarifa Bolão` slot holds each day — that day's fee, or a carried bucket — which decides whether the month figure is a sum of daily values or something else.
- **SQ4 — bolão and federal leftovers (M9.5 2.5).** What `Sobra de Bolão` / `Sobra de Federal` rows record, on which accounts, and whether the month figure is the sum of their Active rows.
- **SQ5 — operator attribution (owner decision).** §7.2 asks for figures "by operator by day", but a close belongs to a Terminal/day that several operators may share and that an unlinked manager may record. Recommendation: close-derived figures stay per Terminal/day with recorder and submitter shown, never split between operators, while hours stay per operator/day; the report shows them side by side rather than joining them.

### Phase 4 — Settlement instrument identity

- [x] **4.1** `SettlementInstrument` enum and `TransactionType` column through a migration that leaves every existing row `None`; exposed on the TransactionType create/update/read contracts with an `IsInEnum` validator rule; seed values only as SQ1/SQ2 establish.
- [x] **4.2** Decide in-phase and record: whether installment plans must also carry `Cheque` (today they require `OperatorEnteredCheque`); whether open-cheque aging later widens to single cheques; and whether a type's instrument may change once rows reference it, since a change reclassifies those rows' history (lock it, or allow it as documented metadata). Retiring a type never removes its classification. Nothing changes in this phase without these decisions.
- [x] **4.3** Tests from a clean migrated database, OpenAPI and snapshot, spec §3 and §6.14 sync.

> **Phase 4.2 decision record (2026-10-20, recorded before the dependent runtime; consumes working answers SQ1 = question 1 and SQ2 = question 2).** Settlement timing (`SettlementRule`) and instrument identity (`SettlementInstrument`) stay orthogonal: neither is derived from the other, from the type's name, category or direction.
>
> - **Plans and the cheque instrument.** `POST /transaction/installment` keeps its shipped precondition (`SettlementRule = OperatorEnteredCheque`) and does **not** also require `SettlementInstrument = Cheque`. Requiring it would refuse plan creation on every existing branch, whose migrated types are `None` until an admin classifies them — a silent regression of a shipped command — and the plan's identity is already its `OriginTransactionId`. The fresh seed gives `Depósito Cheque` both values. A plan row reaches upcoming settlements only through its type's instrument (unclassified → absent, and the report says whether anything is classified at all); it is counted once and keeps its `OriginTransactionId`.
> - **Standalone cheques in open-cheque aging (SQ2: single cheques exist; paid = cleared).** `GET /report/cheques/open-aging` keeps every installment plan exactly as before (any type, `OriginTransactionId` set, ordering, paging and plan totals unchanged) and additionally lists each Active, unpaid row with **no** `OriginTransactionId` whose type — active or retired — carries `Cheque`, as its own one-row group. No plan identity is invented: such a group's `OriginTransactionId` is `null` (the contract member becomes nullable, an explicit change), its single row carries the transaction id, and `TotalRowCount = OpenRowCount = 1`. Groups order by oldest open due date, then by the group key (the plan origin id, or the standalone row's own id — both are transaction ids and never collide). `TotalCount` becomes the open-group count (plans + standalone cheques); `OutstandingTotal`, `OpenRowCount` and `ByBucket` include standalone rows. A standalone row of a type that is not classified `Cheque` stays out, whatever its `SettlementRule`. The page's rows come from one batched read instead of one sibling read per group.
> - **Changing a classification.** The instrument is report metadata on the type, never copied onto rows: every read classifies a row by its type's **current** instrument. An admin may set or change it on an active, non-system type at any time, including when rows reference the type — that is how migrated `None` types are configured, and it is reversible. It never changes a row's value, direction, dates, `PaidAt`, status, ledger balance, close variance, lock or plan state; it changes only the current-state receivable reports above, which always answer "as classified now". Rename keeps it; retiring keeps it (retired types cannot be edited, and reports still read them). `PUT /transaction-type/{id}` treats an omitted instrument as "unchanged", so an older client that renames a type cannot silently clear its classification; an explicit `None` clears it. System-owned counterpart types stay `None`.
> - **Known limit, not a business decision.** A type retired before it was classified cannot be classified (there is no reactivation path); its open rows stay out of upcoming settlements. No production data exists before release, so this affects only development/staging data.

> **Phase 6.1 decision record (2026-10-20, recorded before the runtime; consumes working answers SQ3 = question 3, SQ4 = question 4, SQ5 = question 10 and the Operador part of question 9).** Gate note: M9.5 3.3 (the §6.12/§6.13 notes of these answers) is written at canonical v55 and its independent semantic acceptance is still pending; this phase consumes the owner-adopted working answers themselves, so a reviewer's correction of 3.3 reopens only the items below that use the corrected answer.
>
> - **Marker shape.** One non-nullable enum column on the type, `TransactionType.MonthlyFinancialRole { None, BolaoServiceFee, CommercialLeftover }` — explicit configuration by stable type identity, never by name, category or direction. It travels on the TransactionType create/update/read contracts like the instrument (omitted on update = unchanged, explicit `None` clears). A role must match the type's category direction — `BolaoServiceFee` an `In` category, `CommercialLeftover` an `Out` category (409 otherwise) — so a role's rows are always one direction; system-owned types stay `None` (database check). The migration leaves every existing type `None`; the fresh seed marks only its own `Tarifa Bolão` (`BolaoServiceFee`) and `Sobra de Bolão` / `Sobra de Federal` (`CommercialLeftover`) rows. Existing branches configure their types through `PUT /transaction-type/{id}`; until then the figure is reported as not configured (null), never as zero. `Operador` (question 9) is a separate inflow and is never marked or counted as fee.
> - **Tarifa Bolão (SQ3).** Each Terminal/day's figure is the signed sum `Σ In − Σ Out` of Active (non-Draft, non-cancelled) rows of `BolaoServiceFee` types on Terminal accounts; the month figure is the sum of the daily figures. Product counts and product differences are never read; bank-account rows are out of scope (they would be unexplained bank inflows, not the Terminal's fee).
> - **Sobras (SQ4).** Commercial losses: the signed sum `Σ Out − Σ In` of Active rows of `CommercialLeftover` types on Terminal accounts, reported per `(Date, Terminal, recording operator, type)` — so Bolão and Federal leftovers stay distinct — with per-type and month totals. The recording operator is who recorded the row, not who owns the close.
> - **Official variance.** The persisted `Diferença Caixa` of every Active non-Draft close in the window (Submitted, Approved and Rejected, as the cash-variance summary), read by the same repository query, each close once; a Draft (recalled or recheck-demoted) close shows `null` and its retained value is never read. The §6.12 formula is unchanged; a first-transition close whose official value carries an old counted-fee opening is reported as the retained official history it is.
> - **Attribution (SQ5).** Close figures and the day's fee stay per Terminal/day beside that close's recorder and submitter (user and operator), never split between operators and never duplicated per operator; a Terminal/day with fee rows but no close still appears with no close. Hours stay per operator/day from the shared time-entry calculation (each entry's effective policy, one branch-local now; an open segment today is live and provisional, an earlier day's open segment is 0 h and still in progress). The two are shown side by side, never joined, and there is no grand total across variance, fee, leftovers and hours.
> - **Window.** A past month covers its calendar month; the current month ends at branch-local today and is flagged partial; a future month is 400 `REPORT_MONTH_IN_FUTURE`. Distinct from monthly reconciliation (lock readiness) and from the manual CEF borderô comparison, which stays outside the system.
> - **Classification history.** Like the instrument, the role is read at query time: rename and retirement keep it, and a change re-labels every month's figures — locked months included — without changing any row, variance, close or lock. Freezing a locked month's report classification would need effective-dated markers; that is a business decision not taken here (no business answer requires it today).

> **Independent review of Phases 4–7 (2026-10-21) and its fixes — repeat review pending.** The reviewer accepted Phase 4 (enum, `None` migrations, CRUD, seeds, system-type guards, compatibility and standalone-cheque decisions), the Phase 5 predicates/filters/totals/configuration, the Phase 6 Tarifa/Sobras sources, Operador separation, attribution, Draft suppression and hours, and the Phase 7 clock, snapshot, atomicity, access and functional parity, plus the contract and Shared generation; design markers stay v55. Findings and dispositions:
>
> 1. **[P2] Huge valid page → 500 (fixed).** `(page − 1) × pageSize` overflowed `int` in the LINQ `Skip`. `PageOffset.TryGet` answers a page beyond any representable offset with an empty page; upcoming settlements and open-cheque aging (whose group query this delivery rewrote) use it, and the HTTP regression `Page=2147483647&PageSize=200` returns 200 with the whole-filter totals (`ReportControllerManagementOverviewReviewRegressionTest`). Removing the guard makes it fail. Other paged reports predate M11 and still compute the offset in `int`; they are outside this delivery's selected scope and left as a named follow-up.
> 2. **[P2] Cancellation did not reach every query (fixed).** `ITimeEntriesRepository` (both list forms), `IOperatorsRepository.ListActiveByBranchId` and `IOnboardingRepository.PendingRequestCount` now take the token, passed by the projector, the queue reader, the overview and the time-entry balance report (its controller now forwards the request token). The test interceptor gained a recording mode: every command after `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` in an overview must run with a cancellable token, and a real client cancellation stalled at the time-entry, operator and link-request stages must reach that stage's query. Dropping the token from the time-entry read makes both fail.
> 3. **[P2] Overview materialized detail to return totals (fixed).** New repository aggregates share each full report's base predicate: `ListOfficialVarianceStatusTotalsAsNoTracking` + `ListLargestOfficialVariancesAsNoTracking` (one `BuildOfficialVarianceQuery`), `GetFiadoBalanceSummaryByBranchIdAsNoTracking` + `ListTopFiadoDebtorsByBranchIdAsNoTracking` (one `balances` CTE over the netting projection, also used by the balance report), `GetMonthlyFinancialRoleTotalAsNoTracking` (one `BuildMonthlyFinancialRoleQuery`), `MonthlyReconciliationReader.ReadLockContext` (the evaluator's inputs without the per-day variance/detail), and `Get{Account,Operator}ActivityPage…` (listed set counted and capped at 50 in SQL, sums only for the page). `CashVarianceFold.FromStatusTotals` is the one variance formula for rows and aggregates; `MonthlyFinancialReader.ReadTotals` serves the overview and the full report's totals. The hours roll-up stays whole (C# calculation, effective policy, one now) and is computed once for both sections. Recorded SQL proves the overview never runs the per-operator monthly role grouping or the unbounded fiado balance list; a cap test (52 accounts, 52 operators, 7 debtors, 7 closes) pins exact whole-set counts with previews of 50/50/5/5 and the source ordering.
> 4. **[P2] No domain tokens for the new enums (fixed).** `settlement-instrument`, `monthly-financial-role` and `overview-section-status` exist in both themes (`colors.json`), `scripts/check-contrast.mjs` requires them value-for-value against the OpenAPI enums, three negative scenarios cover a missing group, an invented instrument and a missing role value (40 scenarios pass), contrast passes 1,164 pairs, and the badge contract, token README and catalog name them.
> 5. **[P3] Mutation note understated the remaining gaps (corrected).** Stryker 5.0.0 (scratch install, isolated copy) now runs UseCases.Test **and** Validators.Test over `UpcomingSettlementsReader`, `MonthlyFinancialReader`, `CashVarianceFold`, the new report/overview use cases and validators and the role rule. After adding unit tests for the gaps it showed — a coincidental fold fixture, `ContainsInProgress` and row counts in the totals, the unconfigured per-day fee, hours ordering, the pre-cancelled upcoming request and the exact single validation message — it scores **90.48% of 168**. The 16 survivors are: `First`→`FirstOrDefault` on non-empty groups (4, equivalent); tie-breaker `ThenBy` directions in `MonthlyFinancialReader` (10: terminal days, leftovers, leftovers by type, hours — observable only on exact name/date ties, which no unit fixture builds); `UpcomingSettlementsReader`'s `totalCount == 0 ? 0` (equivalent); and `GetManagementOverviewUseCase`'s default year, exercised only by the PostgreSQL A13 test that Stryker does not run. `ManagementOverviewBuilder`, `ManagerQueueReader`, `MonthlyReconciliationReader`, `TimeEntryBalanceProjector` and the repositories were outside the mutate scope; their behavior is pinned by WebApi.Test, not by this score.
>
> Validation on the final tree: Validators 378, UseCases 1,187, WebApi + architecture 1,301 (six new review regressions), build with no new warnings, no pending EF model change; the OpenAPI export is byte-identical (`b2f7dcf0…`, no contract change from these fixes) and `generate:check`, typecheck and the 103 Shared tests pass; design-docs (45/45, 25 enums), contrast (1,164 pairs) and their regression suites (14 and 40) pass. The real doc-sync gate still rejects the v55 design markers, as it should until semantic acceptance. These are validation results, not repeat acceptance.

### Acceptance scenarios *(pinned by the owning phase; Phase 7 re-runs each against the overview)*

- **A1 Queue.** Overview queue counts equal `GET /report/manager-queue?date=AsOfDate`.
- **A2 Recent closes.** Twelve closes in the window → preview 10 in `GET /dailyclose` order with `TotalCount = 12` and per-status counts; a Draft (recalled) close and a recheck-demoted close show null variance despite retained rows; a close dated `AsOfDate − 7` is outside.
- **A3 Cash variance.** Submitted +10, Approved −5, Rejected +3 and a Draft close with a retained 50 → total +8, counts 1/1/1, the Draft value excluded; equal to the full report for the same window.
- **A4 Reconciliation.** A past unlocked month with a Submitted close and a Draft transaction → `LockReady = false` with both blockers; a month at or before `LockDate` → `IsMonthLocked`; the current month → `IsMonthElapsed = false`, never lockable.
- **A5 Fiado debt and credit.** Client A owes 100 across Tab1 and Tab2; B paid in full; C overpaid by 30; D paid 40 of 100 → debt 160 over 2 debtors, credit 30 over 1 creditor, B absent; equal to the balance split.
- **A6 Unpaid aging.** D's 60 remainder ages on its oldest sale; no repayment is aged; bucket totals equal the full aging across all pages.
- **A7 Open cheques.** Three qualifying open plans fill three pages at page size 1: P1 partially paid, P2 with one cancelled row and two open rows, P4 wholly open. Separately, P3 (wholly Draft) and P5 (fully paid) are excluded. Totals exclude every paid, cancelled and Draft row and equal the sum over all three pages.
- **A8 Upcoming settlements.** A card due `+1` (in), a card due `+14` (out), a cheque installment due `+5` that also appears in open cheques (once per section, never summed across sections), a paid card (out), a cheque due `−3` (overdue only); after the last marked card type is retired its open rows still appear and the section stays `Ready`; `NotConfigured` only when no branch type, active or retired, carries an instrument; equal to the full report.
- **A9 Account activity.** Two terminals and a Tab: a Draft row only in `DraftCount`; an idle account shows yesterday's last activity date; T3, deactivated today after recording rows, is listed with `IsActive = false`; the contract has no cross-account total; each row, T3's included, equals the daily ledger for that account and day.
- **A10 Operator activity.** An operator recording on two accounts, generated rows counted in the share; O4, deactivated today after recording rows, is listed with `IsActive = false`; an unlinked Manager reads the overview; each row, O4's included, equals operator-summary for that operator.
- **A11 Hours.** Under one frozen clock instant shared by both requests: an open segment is in progress at `BranchLocalNow` and labelled provisional; O2's forgotten clock-out counts as unresolved-open (0 h, still `IsInProgress`), not live-now; a mid-month policy change; positive and negative balances summed apart; O3, deactivated with entries in the window, flagged inactive; equal to `GET /report/timeentry-balance` for the window.
- **A12 Monthly financial.** Per Phase 6: a shared terminal keeps close figures per terminal/day and hours per operator; a renamed marked type is still counted; markers unset → `NotConfigured`; equal to the full report.
- **A13 Month selector.** Previous month → full calendar window, `IsPartialMonth = false`; future month → 400; omitted → current month, partial.
- **A14 Empty branch.** Every section is `Empty` or `NotConfigured`; none is `Ready` with zeros.
- **A15 Access.** No token → 401; Member → 403; Manager and Admin without an Operator → 200; the second branch's data never appears.
- **A16 Read-never-commits.** No `IUnitOfWork` (architecture rule) and an unchanged database after the call.
- **A17 Snapshot.** A write committed between two section queries (test interceptor) is invisible to the later sections.
- **A18 Cancellation.** A cancelled request ends without a partial response.
- **A19 Contract.** Required/nullable/enum metadata, reviewed snapshot, no path or schema containing `dashboard`, `GET /report/manager-queue` unchanged.
- **A20 Determinism.** With unchanged data and the same frozen clock instant, two calls return identical bodies, `GeneratedAtUtc` and `BranchLocalNow` included; ties resolve by id.
- **A21 Time advance.** With unchanged data, moving the frozen clock forward changes only `GeneratedAtUtc`, `BranchLocalNow` and today's open segment's live hours and balance. Expected values come from the shared calculator with that day's effective policy (lunch deduction tiers at more than 4 h and more than 6 h gross), never a flat "+1 hour". Three fixed cases under the default policy (target 7.33 h, deductions 1.00 h over 6 h and 0.25 h over 4 h):
  - **Within one tier.** Clock-in 08:00, clock 09:00 → 10:00: payable hours 1.00 → 2.00, balance −6.33 → −5.33.
  - **Across a tier.** Clock-in 09:00, clock 15:00 → 16:00: gross 6 → 7 h moves the deduction from 0.25 to 1.00, so payable hours go 5.75 → 6.00 and balance −1.58 → −1.33.
  - **Across branch-local midnight, within the month.** `AsOfDate` and every day-based window move to the new day. The previous day's still-open segment stops contributing live hours (0 h — the forgotten clock-out rule, §6.7), but it keeps `IsInProgress = true` and its operator's `ContainsInProgress = true`, as the shipped report does, so it stays visible for manual correction. In the overview it moves from the live-now count to the unresolved-open count.

### Fixture recipe

Built with the shared WebApi.Test seed helpers (test fixtures, not public endpoints); Phase 8's demo reproduces it through supported APIs.

- **Branches.** B1 (main) and B2 (isolation, with its own close, fiado client, cheque and `LockDate`).
- **People.** In B1: an unlinked Manager, an Admin linked to operator O2, a Member linked to O1, O3 deactivated with time entries in the selected month, and O4 deactivated today after recording transactions.
- **Accounts.** Terminals T1 (paired with Tab1) and T2, Tab1, Tab2, Bank1, and T3 deactivated today after recording transactions.
- **Calendar.** `LockDate` at the end of month M−2; month M−1 elapsed and unlocked with one blocker of each type; the current month M.
- **Fiado.** Clients A–D as in A5/A6 across Tab1/Tab2, including one cash repayment and one counted-stock sale after Phase 5 (linked Terminal rows).
- **Cheques.** Qualifying open plans P1 (three rows, one paid), P2 (three rows, one cancelled) and P4 (two rows, none paid); excluded plans P3 (wholly Draft) and P5 (fully paid); and a single `Depósito Cheque` row if SQ2 says the shop records them.
- **Cards.** Credit and debit rows due `+1`, `+14` and `−3`, one marked paid — classified only after SQ1.
- **Closes.** The last eight days on T1 and T2 covering Submitted, Approved, Rejected, a recalled Draft with a retained variance row, and a recheck-demoted Draft.
- **Time.** O1 with an open segment today, O2 with closed days and a forgotten clock-out on an earlier day of M, a policy change on day 10 of M.
- **Bolão.** Rows and counts per Phase 6.1.

### Done criteria

- Every matrix row ships with its full report and overview section, and each section is parity-tested against that report for the same scope and window.
- Upcoming settlements and the consolidated monthly report implement their signed meanings; no shop fact is assumed and no classification relies on a display name, category, direction or `SettlementRule`.
- The overview reads one snapshot, fails atomically, distinguishes `Empty` from `NotConfigured`, returns exact whole-set totals with labelled windows, and leaves the Work Queue unchanged; no `dashboard` name appears in the contract.
- §6.14 and §7.2 point at the shipped surfaces; D13's catalog entries exist and gap Q is closed.
- M7.7 and M9.5 are complete.
